11 M8-INC3 M8 increment Inc 3 — conditional work per verdict [DONE]
(I5 and/or I6 and/or D-fixes), each with its own re-run falsifier. CLOSED 2026-07-19 by Inc 4's human …note
(I5 and/or I6 and/or D-fixes), each with its own re-run falsifier. CLOSED 2026-07-19 by Inc 4's human session (Docs/m8/inc4/s3-host.log): the client seat reported that shots register, at 116 ms measured. Read the falsifier note at the end of this increment for what that does and does NOT discharge. I5 is live and its effect is measured. Evidence, instruments and caveats: Docs/m8/sim/. Summary of what changed and what it bought: - Lag compensation had never executed. NetworkProjectConfig.LagCompensation.Enabled was false, so TotalHitboxes stayed 0 and Fireball correctly — and silently — used the PhysX fallback. The bake below was necessary but not sufficient, and the "should now auto-upgrade" line under it was wrong. Enabled via M8NetConfig.Apply -m8LagComp on; proof is hit-reg = LAG-COMP in every run log since. - Branch A, answered decisively. Scripted client shooter (-playtestMode JoinShooter) vs the strafe dummy, one binary, one aim rule, -m8ForcePhysX as the only difference: at ~113 ms the client landed 39/73 (53%) with rewind and 2/71 (3%) without it. Both arms show the same ~1.4–1.7 m present-position miss trailing the target at 92–94%, so the geometry is identical and only the adjudication differs — the client seat is unplayable at realistic ping without rewind, and playable with it. - Branch D, measured on the baseline's own metric. BotDuel A/B at zero latency: phantom misses among geometric connections fell 8.7% → 2.5%. The control's 8.7% independently reproduces the committed baseline's 11.0%, which is what identifies it as the same bug rather than a new one. - The residual was a NEW class, not a smaller one — DIAGNOSED AND FIXED 2026-07-19, phantom rate now 0%. What survived lag comp was dead-centre (miss 0.00–0.12 m) at point-blank (0.5–0.8 m), concentrated in AIRATTACK/CHAIN — not the capsule-edge tunnelling the baseline documented. Cause: the projectile was spawning INSIDE the target's hurtbox. FirePoint is 1.2 m in front of the shooter and the target capsule's radius is 0.5, so from ~0.87 m inward the muzzle is inside the opponent's body, and a ray originating inside a collider never reports it — the shot passed through and struck the wall behind. The data localised it before the arithmetic explained it: every phantom was under 1 m (0 of 290 above), and within that band off-centre shots landed 13/13 while dead-centre landed 4/10, which is the signature of muzzle containment and of nothing else on the suspect list. Fixed by testing containment at the segment start before the swept ray, through one shared contact resolver so a guard still blocks. Verified: 189 shots, 0 phantoms at every range and ability; dead-centre close range 4/10 → 6/6. Full write-up, caveats and severity note in Docs/m8/sim/README.md. - WHY THIS IS [~] AND NOT [x]: the falsifier below asks for S2 re-run identically by a human. This is a scripted instrument on a combat model the baseline never measured, and it deliberately removes Branch C, so it cannot answer the feel half. V1TEST-SEAT stays partial and Inc 4 still owes the human client-seat verdict. What Inc 3 has earned is the netcode half, with evidence. UNBLOCKED 2026-07-18 — the sweep decided it. SHIP: I5 (lag comp) + the Branch-D fix, TOGETHER, because they are one piece of work: LagCompensation.Raycast with SubtickAccuracy hitboxes removes the execution-order race behind the 11% phantom misses and supplies the rewind Branch A needs. Shipping lag comp alone would partially mask the D residual — the failure this plan warned about from the start. Do NOT ship I6 or any speed/size tuning: Branch C came back as the game working, and Branch B is Gate 2's call at Inc 4, not this increment's. - First blocker — the hitbox bake — is CLEARED (2026-07-18, 8eb9a62). Player.prefab's HitboxRoot and capsule Hitbox were never linked to each other (Hitbox.Root null, HitboxRoot.Hitboxes empty), so TotalHitboxes was 0 and the code correctly fell back to PhysX — confirmed live by the ledger's hit-reg = PhysX fallback line. Cause: Fusion's NetworkObjectBaker never mentions Hitbox at all (checked against the shipped source, not assumed), so no number of reimports was ever going to link it. Assets/Editor/HitboxBaker.cs now does it headlessly via -executeMethod; the graph is bidirectional and the bake is idempotent (a second run links 0 new refs and leaves the prefab byte-identical). - What that does and does NOT buy. It only makes the hitboxes registrable. Fireball.cs already carries the full lag-comp path gated on TotalHitboxes > 0, so it should now auto-upgrade — but the runtime smoke test has NOT been run, and even hit-reg = LAG-COMP in the log would only prove registration, not that Branch A's slope collapsed. I5 remains unverified; the falsifier below is the bar. - ⚠ CORRECTED 2026-07-19 — "it should now auto-upgrade" WAS FALSE. A second switch, NetworkProjectConfig.LagCompensation.Enabled, was false, so the bake could not register anything and the code stayed on PhysX. The caution in this bullet was right and its premise was wrong: it warned that a LAG-COMP log line would not prove the fix worked, while assuming the line would at least appear. It never had. The smoke test this bullet asked for is what found it — one run, one grep. The lesson is the bullet's own: an unrun check is not a small debt, and "should" is not a state. - Falsifier, non-negotiable — HOW IT WAS ACTUALLY DISCHARGED, which is not how it was written. The literal instruction ("re-run S2 identically") became impossible between writing and execution: TOPO-SHAPE replaced the attacking topology after the baseline was recorded, so no re-run can be identical to it. What was done instead, and why it is stronger for the netcode question: a scripted A/B holding aim, arena, dummy and latency constant and flipping ONLY the resolver (-m8ForcePhysX), which is better controlled than two human sessions could ever be — 53% vs 3% at ~113 ms. The FEEL half the falsifier also wanted was discharged separately by S3. Neither substitute is the original test; recording that honestly is the point. Original text follows: re-run S2 identically against the committed baseline in Docs/m8/ and read it with tools/m8_ledger.py. The client's miss-vs-RTT slope must collapse toward the host control's 0.58 m, and phantom misses must approach 0. The ~0.55 m Branch C intercept must NOT move — if it does, something was tuned that shouldn't have been. The fix does not ship on vibes; it ships on the re-run. - FB-SPEED's pending bump still waits on this: the speed↔hit-reg coupling means raising speed on the raw raycast widens the phantom-miss band.

The flurry itself resolved right after, eight configurations deep — Combo Punch.fbx, an 89-frame take the owner supplied, because the fault was structural rather than a bad pick: every candidate take chambers its off hand across the chest, and a masked layer keeps that chamber while discarding the hips it needs. The fix also found that this project's facing tool is inert on masked layers (orientationOffsetY rotates a root the layer discards), corrected instead by a new ClipSurgeon ADD op that offsets the chest curves directly; ChestTwist went kit-wide the same pass (jab, volley, forward shot, flurry, flow release), so aim pitch now bends the torso everywhere, not only on the flamethrower.

Which tools, how many iterations, and when it is right are ALL the owner's call. Owner: Trevor · Seat: the owner's eye · Done when: Trevor judges it right — no threshold, no timebox, no sign-off. Provenance entries logged as assets are generated (disclosure/IP hygiene, not a gate). PARTIAL 2026-08-01 — V1 IS IN-ENGINE AND IS WHAT THE WHOLE COMBAT PASS WAS BUILT ON; V2 IS STAGED AND UNWIRED. The chain that landed: MJ silhouette → MJ build/world figure → MJ orthographic turnaround → Meshy multi-view → Meshy Ultra remesh → Mixamo auto-rig → Assets/Art/Characters/Emberborn/T-Pose.fbx, on Player.prefab (and Bot.prefab by variant) since 2026-07-24. Every one of those steps has a PROVENANCE.md entry. Two things keep it [~] rather than [x], and neither is a judgement about whether it looks right: (a) the Blender human pass is still owed — two diagnosed auto-rig defects (middle-finger curl baked into the rest pose; pant-leg weight noise), and CLAUDE.md names that pass as load-bearing for authorship, not polish; (b) a V2 anchor pass exists as art input only — four renders + a 57 MB T-pose FBX under Docs/m9/concepts/V2/, committed 2026-07-28, nothing rigged or imported. ⚠ V2 owes provenance entries (see PROVENANCE.md); until the owner takes or drops it, V1 is the hero. ~~THREE CREATOR CONSTRAINTS ON THE BLENDER PASS~~ — DROPPED 2026-07-22 (owner decision). The character creator moved Alpha → Beta and Alpha now ships four hand-built stock characters, so the constraints that existed only to keep the Ember anchor creator-ready — (a) non-destructive modularity, (b) a tint mask, (c) an essence mask — no longer earn their place in the prototype. The rule still holds; its input changed: "pull the constraint forward, never the system" pulls a constraint forward because its system is near. The creator (and the essence system) is now two milestones out, so its constraints move with it. M9 builds one simple, baked Ember — no modular split, no masks — the least authoring tax for a Blender beginner racing to a prototype. The modularity/mask lessons rejoin the creator work (Beta), or get applied as a batch when Alpha hand-builds the four. Rationale: PROJECT_STATE → M9 — the Emberborn build (3.5a).

Delivered: the nine-motion directional locomotion tree, the fireball wind-up/strike as mirrored parity trees, shield start/loop/end, jump rise/fall/land doubling as dodge load/air/land, the cannon, and a clip for every cell of the stance grid — ~45 motions, 0 dangling (gate.ps1 motions). Both sim constraints hold and are gated, not asserted: in-place is enforced by roadmap.py --check's animation-boundary rule, and every window drives clip time through PhaseProgress. 18 M9-INC5 M9 increment E1 — Animator state bridge (the read-only boundary) [DONE]
SKEL-STATES, SKEL-TICKTIMER. Add the small missing reads (consolidated Action+Phase …note
SKEL-STATES, SKEL-TICKTIMER. Add the small missing reads (consolidated Action+Phase — PlayerCombat.cs:219-220 are private — plus phase-normalized time) so clips speed-scale to the live server window. Reuse the existing derived reads; no new networked state. Owner: Code · Seat: editor → two-peer once a rig consumes it · Done: an Animator selects clip + normalized time from public reads only; a code check confirms no clip length feeds a TickTimer and no clip displacement feeds the transform. PARTIAL 2026-07-22 — the boundary is BUILT and ENFORCED; what is missing is a consumer, and that is A3's. Built: ActionKind/Phase made public (read-only via CurrentAction/ActionPhase), plus PhaseProgress, PhaseDuration and IsArcRising; a new StartupDuration() mirrors the existing ActiveDuration()/RecoveryDuration() switches so an animation is fitted to the window the sim is actually running — including the FollowUpPending branch, without which every chain link would be fitted to shot 1's wind-up. PlayerAnimatorBridge (baked onto Player.prefab by AnimatorBridgeBaker, idempotent; Bot.prefab inherits it) pushes those reads into Animator parameters once per frame and forces applyRootMotion = false. No new networked state; nothing has a setter. Enforced: tools/roadmap.py --check grew an animation-boundary check that runs on every commit — no animation API or clip.length in the sim scripts, no applyRootMotion = true, no transform write from the bridge. All four rules verified to FAIL correctly, each injected deliberately, each returning exit 1. (Held at [~] from 2026-07-22 to 2026-08-01 deliberately: the done-condition says an Animator selects clip and normalized time, and no Animator existed yet — filing it [x] then would have laundered "the code exists" into "the claim holds".) ⇒ CLOSED 2026-08-01 — the consumer arrived and the boundary held under real load. Emberborn_Combat selects clip and normalized time from the public reads alone: every action state drives AnimatorState.timeParameter off PhaseProgress, so the clip re-derives its position from the authoritative timer each frame and self-corrects through a Fusion resim. No triggers anywhere — a trigger is an edge, and edges are what resim duplicates. The stated Done is therefore met, and it is gated rather than asserted: roadmap.py --check's animation-boundary rule runs on every commit. Five days of animation work since have pushed on it hard (masked layers, mirrored parity trees, speed-scaled cuts) and the authority never inverted — every window stayed the server's and every clip was fitted to it, including the one place it looked like it bent (_apexFreezeSeconds is a duration the SIM owns, not a clip length read back). The two-peer seat this increment's Seat field named is NOT dissolved — it moves to G2, which re-verifies at real ping on the settled geometry and now carries it by name.

23 M9-INC10 M9 increment E6 — Occluder shipping shader + camera→aim decoupling [PARTIAL]
Dither/clip shader replaces the runtime material mutation. Separately, decouple the aim ray from the …note
Dither/clip shader replaces the runtime material mutation. Separately, decouple the aim ray from the cosmetic camera so the jump/dodge vertical spring cannot leak into aim — netcode-touching, and it surfaces now because the hero changes the camera-body relationship. Owner: Code · Seat: dither = editor/host; aim decouple = two-peer at real ping · Done: occluder fades via shader; a two-peer check shows the aim ray stable through an arc. AIM HALF BUILT 2026-07-22; shader half NOT STARTED and gated on an owner decision. AimPoint was raycast from cam.ViewportPointToRay(0.5, 0.5), and LateUpdate places that camera at a SPRUNG height (TrailedPivotY, capped _trailMaxLag 0.2 m) — so a local, cosmetic, framerate-dependent trail was displacing the origin of the ray BasicSpawner.OnInput writes straight into NetInput. Two players at different framerates got different shots from the same mouse position, invisibly to the server. Direction was always clean; the ORIGIN moved — same class as FB-MUZZLE and the cannon parallax bug. New PlayerCameraRig.AimRay derives the line from Yaw/Pitch and the unsprung pivot, so no cosmetic state reaches aim. It also removes an unrecorded frame-ordering dependency: OnInput is polled before LateUpdate, so the old ray used the PREVIOUS frame's camera pose. Knock-on: the spring leaves I7's parallax budget, worst case 1.71 → 1.51 m (MaxAimParallax 3.0 deliberately NOT retuned — a validator in the shot path is the last place to spend a windfall). Gated: tools/roadmap.py --check grew an aim-decoupling rule, verified to fail correctly, because reverting to ViewportPointToRay is an attractive regression — it reads as the obvious way to find "the point under the crosshair". Accepted, bounded cost: while airborne the camera renders up to 0.2 m below the aim ray, so the shot lands that far from the centre pixel. Re-projecting the crosshair onto the true line was considered and rejected — a fixed 0.2 m parallax subtends ~20 px at 10 m but ~69 px at 3 m, so the reticle would slide as you swept past near geometry, a worse lie than the one it fixes. ⇒ STAYS [~]: the two-peer "aim ray stable through an arc" check is owed, and the occluder shader waits on the open Shader Graph vs hand-written call (PROJECT_STATE 3.5b) — not Code's to make.

26 M9-INC13 M9 increment I1 — Client defender budget (owed from M8; arithmetic) [DONE]
FB-STARTUP, V1TEST-SEAT. Compute what the CLIENT defender actually gets (identify the tell + react + …note
FB-STARTUP, V1TEST-SEAT. Compute what the CLIENT defender actually gets (identify the tell + react + input uplink vs startup + flight) using the measured interp delay 0.233 s @ ~116 ms — not the full 0.42 s. Informs M10's telegraph tuning. Owner: Code · Seat: analysis + client under lag sim · Done: a written budget number; host-seat framing purged. DONE 2026-07-22 — Docs/m9/I1_CLIENT_DEFENDER_BUDGET.md, re-runnable as tools/defender_budget.py. The budget on record was missing TWO terms, not one: the attacker's interp delay (the block verdict is read at the shooter's rewind tick, so the guard must be up earlier than contact) and the uplink lead (a Fusion client simulates ahead, so the press lands in the host's future). Answer: a reactive block cannot land inside ~9.9 m at 116 ms RTT, ~11.3 m at 150 — while the defender's own screen shows the guard formed in time at EVERY range. Fights are 5–12 m. Two by-products: the tick rate is 64 Hz, not the 60 every ticks→seconds conversion on record assumed (the figures survive — 15 t × 15.625 = 234 ms ≈ the recorded 0.233 s), and HitboxBufferLengthInMs is Fusion's untouched 200 ms default = 12.8 ticks against a rewind the engine requests of 15 t @ 116 and 20 t @ 179 — lag comp has been truncated across most of the required band. Raising it is a DECISION, not a fix (it helps the shooter and costs the defender); quantified in the doc, routed to G2 + M10.
27 M9-INC14 M9 increment C1 — The ring stance (RING-*) [DONE]
spec §3.5. The first new ability since M3, and owner-designed: Q swept you into a rooted channel with a …note
spec §3.5. The first new ability since M3, and owner-designed: Q swept you into a rooted channel with a ring of fire burning around you, held indefinitely; M1 during it spent the stance as a 5 s barrage of waves rolling outward. The answer to it is to jump — the shield cannot block it and the dodge's 0.4 m apex cannot clear its waist height, which makes it the one attack lateral evasion does not answer. Owner: Trevor (design) + Code · Seat: host → play · Done: the ability ships, is reachable, and the bot knows it exists. DONE 2026-07-27, then FOLDED INTO THE GRID 2026-07-29 (it is Special-M2 now; the Q binding is retired but EInputButtons.RingStance = 5 is retained unbound, because those indices are wire format). Structurally it invented nothing: it reuses SHIELD-CANNON's exact shape — a held stance whose Active phase never expires, converted by M1 — which is what later got named GUARD-CONVERT. The reusable piece is the barrage's statelessness: nine overlapping waves with no per-wave state at all — wave k is emitted at start + k·interval and its radius is (t − k·interval)·speed, so the whole thing is a pure function of ONE start tick. Nothing spawned, nothing to leak, and the client's visual computes the same circles the server hits with. The lesson the dead RING-SPEED row records is the valuable one: a travelling wavefront was tuned 10 → 5 m/s and failed in play both times as "pulsing out of the player" — correctly, because that is what a travelling band is. When two tuning passes fail in the same direction, stop tuning and re-read what was asked for. Replaced by the relay model (RING-STEP). 12 of its 13 rows are unverified — the ability ships; none of its numbers are measured. That is M10's. ⚠ AND "IS PLAYED" WAS FALSE — THIS INCREMENT'S OWN DONE-CONDITION ("the ability ships, is reachable, and the bot knows it exists") WAS SILENTLY VIOLATED FOR THREE DAYS (2026-07-29 → 2026-08-01). Folding the ring into the grid unbound its RingStance button index — correctly, and carefully documented — but the branch keeping the channel ALIVE was left reading that index. Nothing writes it, so it read as "released" every tick and the stance collapsed on its first Active tick, taking the stance burn, the barrage conversion and RING-CONCURRENT with it. Fixed 2026-08-01 with the M2 cell swap, by reading the SLOT that opens it. Three things this increment should be remembered for now, and the bug is only the first: (1) retiring an input index owes a sweep for READERS, not just writers — an unbound button never errors, it answers "not pressed" forever, which for a held state means "let go"; (2) a [x] is a claim about the DAY it was written — this one was true on 2026-07-27 and was falsified two days later by an unrelated refactor, which no gate joins to it; (3) unverified earned its keep — the ability nobody had measured is exactly the one that turned out to be broken, and a built here would have been a lie the ledger helped tell. The stance is now UNPLAYED, not merely unverified: its first honest play is still ahead. → IT HAPPENED, SAME DAY, AND IT VINDICATED THE MODEL. The owner's first verdict on a stance he could actually hold: "I love the behavior how you can see the prior one and the next one up as it moves outward." That is the exact look RING-SPEED died twice failing to reach with a travelling wavefront, and the relay model had been delivering it unseen since 2026-07-27. Read together with the outage above, this increment is a single lesson in two halves: the design was right and unobserved for three days, and nothing in the tooling could tell the difference between that and being wrong. Only play could, and only once the ability was reachable. Round 9 then retuned it on that evidence — cadence halved, every ring 50% taller, and the stance made a GUARD on the shared shield pool (RING-GUARD, a new spec row).
28 M9-INC15 M9 increment C2 — The stance grid (GRID- / HOOK- / FSHOT- / SWEEP540- / RETREAT- / FLAME- / DRIFT- / FLURRY- / FLOW-*, S1–S9) [DONE]
spec §3.6. Three armed stances × three slots, over the three slots the kit already had: Q/E/F arm …note
spec §3.6. Three armed stances × three slots, over the three slots the kit already had: Q/E/F arm Power/Special/Motion, and M1 / M2 / LCtrl then mean something different in each row. Plus flow — the rally — on middle mouse. Nine sequenced increments, S1–S9, closing with bot parity. Owner: Trevor (design) + Code · Seat: host → play · Done: every cell dispatches, every cell renders a body motion, and the bot can reach the grid. DONE 2026-07-29. This is the single largest scope addition since M3 and it was never in this document until 2026-08-01 — see the count amendment above. Why it is affordable online, which is the load-bearing design claim: an armed stance is a latch set on a press EDGE and then simply true for many ticks, so it is already true at whatever past tick lag compensation rewinds a shot to — the same immunity a held guard has, and the exact opposite of a reaction-timed input, which Docs/m9/I1_CLIENT_DEFENDER_BUDGET.md shows cannot land inside ~9.9 m at 116 ms. The grid is affordable precisely because it is armed in advance. Three things it produced that outlive it: GUARD-CONVERT — a cross-element system rule, implemented twice before it had a name and five times now, so a new guard gets its conversion by writing one more block of the same shape rather than by inventing a mechanism; the chain generalisation (S2) — one clockwork serving the volley, the emberhook and the forward shot, which is the entire reason a 3-beat chain costs followUps: 2 and nothing else; and BURN-NOSTACK, promoted from a property of one ability to an invariant of the damage system after the audit found the flow overhold silently ignited a burn. INV-6 was SUSPENDED here (INV-6-OPEN) for the flamethrower — checked rather than assumed, and what INV-6 protected (punishability) is kept via discrete pulses, a real wind-up, a real recovery and a hard cap. ⚠ ALL 38 ROWS ARE unverified — the code ships and plays; not one number has been measured. That is M10's whole job, and it is now a much bigger job than the 8 TRI- the seam was drawn around. THE LAYOUT IS NOT FROZEN, AND THE FIRST MOVE COST ALMOST NOTHING — which is the useful evidence about the grid's shape (2026-08-01, owner: "swap Power M2 and Special M2"). The ring stance and the emberhook traded cells: ring → Power-M2, emberhook → Special-M2. In the dispatch that is two case bodies exchanging places and nothing more, because every seam the grid was built on absorbed it — the gates were already properties (THE PRESS RULE), GUARD-CONVERT keys off the HELD state rather than the cell that opened it, the emberhook's continue-on-M2 reads the SLOT rather than the stance, and GRID-TELL is per-STANCE (hands / feet / trail) rather than per-ability. The one thing that did NOT absorb it is worth more than the swap: the BOT's range bands silently encode the distances each row's abilities* fire at, so leaving them put would have armed Special only at 3.5–8 m while the emberhook it now owns needs 3 m — every press falling through, the bot never emberhooking again, and nothing anywhere reporting a problem. A grid cell is cheap to move; a bot policy keyed to what used to be in that cell is not. Bands and presses moved with the abilities and reproduce the pre-swap distances exactly.
29 M9-INC16 M9 increment C3 — The combat + animation feel pass on the V1 hero (2026-07-27 → 2026-08-01) [PARTIAL]
~40 commits turning "the clips play" into "the body reads". Locomotion torsion and the …note
~40 commits turning "the clips play" into "the body reads". Locomotion torsion and the single-authoring-source rule · gait speed re-derived off the planted foot and the whole MOVE- family rescaled twice · the momentum glide replacing flat commitment factors · the leg-cross gather · landings that land · the dodge as a sidestep rather than a flip · one transition-timing table · the mirrored jab · and IGNITE-APEX, the owner's hard rule that a strike swings into the apex, roots and holds, and only then ignites. Owner: Trevor (verdicts) + Code (instruments + wiring) · Seat: standalone player · Done: the owner judges the body right, and every law the pass discovered is gated rather than remembered. PARTIAL — the work landed, the verdicts have not. The method is the transferable part: nearly every fix in this pass began as an unmeasurable feel report ("ripping the anim too quickly", "a TON of glide", "clipping over itself") and was converted into a number by a purpose-built probe before anything was edited — torsion, gait speed and survey, strike phase, gait-phase alignment, leg clearance, jab-parity continuity, controller audit, flow scan, snap. Most are now gates (gate.ps1 torsion | motions | legcross | snap | …), so the law cannot silently un-learn itself. Owed: the owner's verdicts on the running build (apex ignition after the glide, the emberhook kick phrase, the gather, the walk cadence/slide trade), and the staged rows in PROJECT_STATE's Part-2 backlog. ⚠ IGNITE-APEX moved the projectile ~0.28 s later than wind-up end, which changes the client defender budget I1 computed — that re-derivation is owed and is not optional, because the whole telegraph argument rests on it. ROUND 8 (2026-08-01, three owner asks after playing round 7) — and the round's most useful finding is a rule about TUNING, not a number. (1) The emberhook's stream now overshoots the aim point — but the ask ("extend further past the aimpoint") named the LEAD, and the lead was dead code: composed as Min(aim + lead, reach) against a crosshair raycast that runs to 1000 m, the cap won at every duelling range and the stream stopped short of the target it was aimed at, lead never once applied. Reordered to Min(aim, reach) + lead, lead 1.0 → 1.5, tip at 5.0 m. ⚠ THE RULE: two knobs that compose through a Min are ONE knob until you check which arm wins in the common case — turning the shadowed one up ships a change the owner cannot see, and then the next report is "I asked for this and nothing happened." (2) The M2 cells traded (see C2). (3) The momentum glide's 65% floor is REVERTED (_glideFloor → 0): round 6's cap lasted exactly one build, wind-ups decelerate to planted again, and the open question it had raised on RING-BLAST-ROOT — whether a 2 s "root" you can walk out of at 65% still costs enough — withdraws with it. Round 6's separate 10% root lightening stands. Found en route: the ring stance had been unenterable since 2026-07-29 (see C1). All three asks are UNJUDGED in play. ROUND 9 (2026-08-01, the owner's first play of a WORKING ring stance) — three asks, and one finding that outranks them. The finding: the relay barrage's look is right ("you can see the prior one and the next one up as it moves outward"), which retires the doubt RING-SPEED's two failed tunings left behind. The asks: (1) cadence halved — and neither knob reached it alone, since RING-RANGE 10 → 6.5 m drops the march to 5 rings (interval 0.56 → 1.00 s) and RING-BARRAGE 5.0 → 5.6 s stretches the rest of the way to 1.12 s; ⚠ the 10 s figure quoted when the owner chose the option was arithmetic for the OLD 9-ring march and did not survive his own 6.5 m amendment, so the stated requirement was honoured over the mechanism offered to reach it — when a derived number is authored through two knobs, changing either invalidates any figure quoted for the other. (2) every ring +50% tall (RING-CLEAR 1.0 → 1.5 m, one number covering the stance ring and all waves), clearance re-derived at 0.396 s of the 0.7 s jump arc against 0.501 s before, with ~0.1 m of headroom left before the counter-play argument fails. (3) RING-GUARD — the channelled ring blocks, omnidirectionally, on the SHARED shield pool with one regen rule, one break and one lockout. It needed no new lag compensation: the stance records into the existing guard-history ring beside the shield and the catch, the second time that ring has absorbed a new defensive state for free. Left visible rather than quietly fixed: the barrage front now creeps slower than the backpedal (the "retreat barely outruns it" pressure is gone), PDODGE-TUBE stayed at 1.15 m so the kit has two clearance heights, and the ring guard has no DEFENSIVE height rule. All UNJUDGED in play. ROUND 10 (2026-08-01, same session) — round 9's pool tie reversed within the hour, and the walls taught to die. (1) RING-GUARD moved off the shield pool onto a 2.5 s clock (RING-HOLD) — owner: "I take it back... put it on a hold timer." The reason is the keeper, because it is a general one: sweeps and cones are blocked FREE (a guarded victim is skipped without the pool being charged), which stays honest for the SHIELD only because its cone forces you to face the threat. A ring has no facing, so a pool-backed ring charged for fireballs and cannons while giving away the streams, the flamethrower, both 540s, the retreat kick, the drift lunge and the flow release — an HP bar most of the kit cannot touch is not a price. A clock prices every block identically whatever threw it. The open item round 9 flagged as its biggest was not fixed but DISSOLVED, by changing the model rather than patching the call sites. Structurally the timer is one word: RingKick left the set of Actives that never expire, and the phase machine already knew how to end one. (2) The hold is displayed (a draining bar, shield-blue) — FB-FOLLOWUP already records that timed state with a hard edge and no readout gets reported as dropped input, and here what expires is a guard mid-fight. (3) "The ring walls despawn in a flash" was a TRUNCATION, not a fast fade — liveness ended at the barrage window, which is when the last ring is EMITTED rather than when it has finished guttering, so the final ring's mode was yanked at full height. ⚠ Every ring's own curve was correct; the bug lived one level up, in the container's lifetime — a per-ring inspection, which is the obvious place to look, would have found nothing wrong however many times it was run. (4) Burn-out lengthened (RING-CONCURRENT 1.4 → 2.0), which also lands one ring sinking exactly as the next rises — two on screen continuously, the picture the owner said he loved. It spends the documented ~2 ceiling exactly, with no headroom left. (5) The ring stance's RECOVERY became its burn-out (owner, mid-round: "I want the recovery fixed actually" — correcting a first fix that had simply made the wall vanish at expiry; removing the dishonesty by removing the object is not fixing it). ⚠ Which revealed that "the walls despawn in a flash" was TWO bugs under one description: the barrage's truncated last wave, and a stance ring that had no burn-out at all — hardcoded to full height for the whole action, then gone. A symptom naming a FAMILY of objects deserves suspicion that it has more than one cause, or the fix ships half-done and the next play reports it unchanged. The ring now sinks and fades across the recovery and goes cold as it starts sinking. ROUND 11 (2026-08-02, after playing round 10). (1) The stance ring RISES across its wind-up — created at the press now rather than at wind-up end — so it finally has the same three acts (rise/burn/gutter) every barrage wave has had since they shipped. Rounds 10 and 11 are the same finding twice: the object at the CENTRE of the effect was the one nobody had applied the effect's own rules to, at both ends of its life. Cold for the whole rise; spawning early buys the animation of arriving, never an early hitbox. (2) RING-INNER — the barrage starts 1 m beyond the wall (3.5 m), on "I dont like how close specifically THE BARRAGE ALONE starts from the cast point"; its own offset, so the stance ring does not move. ⚠ A cadence input: count 5 → 4, so RING-BARRAGE 5.6 → 4.5 rode along purely to hold the interval tuned the day before — the second time that number has moved to keep a derived one still. (3) RING-HERALD — a low cold ring crawls to the march's start across the existing wind-up: an ability that begins somewhere other than where you stand has to show you where, and the offset had created a question the picture could not answer. (4) RING-BLAST-ROOT 2.0 → 1.5 — ⚠ the second "sluggish" verdict on one number that has never been judged too short, and it got worse without being touched: the glide-floor revert put its ramp back to starting fully planted. A number can regress because its neighbour moved. ROUND 12 (2026-08-02) — the DoT drops to 1/s, the whole fireball strike runs at 85%, and the walk jitter is root-caused. The jitter is the one worth reading.* StepBlend adopted a new heading by ASSIGNMENT — a teleport — at a blend magnitude of ~0.337, moving the blend point 0.674 units in a single frame (8.8x the fastest motion the blend makes deliberately); three different turn angles all decomposed to that one event. ⚠ NEITHER CONTRIBUTING CHANGE WAS WRONG ALONE. The adopt was written for a 0.05 threshold where an instant flip is invisible; the threshold rose to 0.35 on 07-30 and the gather landed 08-01 parking the magnitude at 0.30, just under it — so an edge case at near-zero magnitude silently became the normal path at a third of full magnitude. Two individually-reasoned changes, two days apart, in one file: the class of bug no single review catches. Fixed with a finite adopt rate (43.16 → 9.38 m/s); legcross re-run clean, so the property the snap protected survives. New gate blendjitter, verified in both directions. Three lessons banked: an instrument built to check a hunch will confirm it (the probe's first metric agreed with the report and was pure artifact — caught only by arithmetic); a probe that hardcodes the tuning it measures is a second home for those numbers; and a C# default is not the runtime truth when the prefab has no entry for the field, which cost two identical probe runs before it was spotted. All UNJUDGED in play except the jitter, which is measured. ROUND 13 (2026-08-02) — the jitter fix was WRONG and the correction is the lesson. Owner: "the jitter on switching directions is even worse now." I had measured the animator's INPUT and treated it as a verdict on its OUTPUT. The blend point was genuinely smoother (43.16 → 9.38 units/s); the POSE was not. A new PoseChurnProbe (real controller, 52 bones, comparing configurations in one run) showed the slew removed the SNAP (peak 9.39 → 3.23 m/s) and barely touched the CHURN (TV 71 → 64) — because a teleport SKIPS the intermediate clips and a slew TRAVELS THROUGH them, and those intermediates blend strides that disagree. ⚠ LocomotionPhaseProbe's header already recorded that exact finding from the 07-29 rim sweep ("read as MORE churn, not less"). The evidence to avoid this was in the repo, in a probe read that same morning. A measurement of the wrong quantity is more dangerous than no measurement, because it carries authority. The real fix is to make the teleport SMALL rather than slow — the jump is 2 x blendMag, so adopting down at the idle costs 0.16 units instead of 0.67 (_headingAdoptBelow 0.35 → 0.08, _gatherMagnitude 0.30 → 0.06). All three instruments now agree: pose peak 9.39 → 2.07 m/s, churn 71 → 25, blend jump 8.8x → 2.0x, leg crossings 0. The slew is deleted rather than left dormant. The cached-default trap flagged one round earlier bit for real: neither number was serialized, so the C# edit would have shipped nothing — every blend tunable on the bridge is now written into the prefab. Also: all projectiles x0.8 (fireball 24 → 19.2, cannon 9.8 → 7.84) with both lifetimes x1.25 so reach is unchanged — reach = speed x life, a lesson both tooltips already carry twice.
30 M9-INC18 M9 increment C5 — The ability lab: the Special / Motion cells MEASURED [DONE]
(2026-08-02, unattended) — the first increment in this milestone whose deliverable is evidence rather …note
(2026-08-02, unattended) — the first increment in this milestone whose deliverable is evidence rather than behaviour. AbilityLab (-playtestMode AbilityLab) drives the six Special/Motion grid cells plus the Power retreat 540 through the real input path against the sandbox bag and logs three tagged streams; tools/lab_report.py turns a run into a per-requirement table, reading every EXPECTED value out of Player.prefab so the report itself states no live number. Evidence + method: Docs/m9/ABILITY_LAB.md. Owner: Code · Seat: standalone player (zero latency, stationary target) · Done: 31 rows moved off unverified — 30 to built, one (DRIFT-LUNGE) to partial. The milestone's unverified count fell 75 → 44 in one pass. WHAT IT BOUGHT, and it is the argument for unverified having been worth its cost. Twenty-odd rows had shipped and never been measured, and measuring them found what five days of re-reading the code had not: the emberhook could not hit a target standing in front of you. A single beat connected from 3.50 m and 5.49 m and MISSED from 1.50 m and 1.08 m — closer strictly worse, which is never a reach limit. The bow and side-offset are ABSOLUTE displacements authored on a full-length 5 m curve, so on the short curve a nearby target produces they become a sideways loop that passes ~0.95 m off-axis where a hit needs ~0.72 m. Beat 3's bow is VERTICAL and was therefore spared — which is precisely why it looked intermittent rather than systematic: one of the three beats always landed. And it contradicted the ability's own spec row, where the reach had been cut 5.0 → 3.5 m eight days earlier to make it a close-quarters answer. ⚠ THE INSTRUMENT'S OWN FAILURES ARE THE OTHER HALF OF THE LESSON. Run 1's five flamethrower scenarios never armed Special, so M1 fell through to the neutral row and threw a jab each time — which the report read as a flame pulse and printed as 62.5 DPS against a spec of 18, a number plausible enough to quote and entirely fictional. A harness that reaches an ability by the real input path can also MISS it by the real input path, and a jab is not obviously wrong in a column of damage numbers. Run 1 also called a hit at 10 m a range violation by measuring body-to-body when the cone starts 1.2 m in front of the body. Both were caught only by asking why a number was surprising. A measurement is not evidence until you have asked what else could have produced it. Also settled: the flamethrower really is 18.0 DPS at the core and stops dead at 9 m and 13.5°; the denial ring really is world-anchored (cast at 4.1 m, struck from 9.5 m after the caster fled); the drift really is 3.3 m/s backward as well as sideways; all six cooldowns are exact to within a tick; and GRID-SPEND holds — a press into a closed cooldown left the stance armed rather than eating it. Left open on purpose: DRIFT-LUNGE is a real spec↔code divergence (spec wants a 0.72 damage floor, code shares the reach's 0.5) recorded as partial, because closing it is a 44% damage buff and that is an owner call, not a bug fix.

32 M9-INC19 M9 increment C6 — The grid's vocabulary, its mark family, and the readout design (2026-08-02) [DONE]
MARK-, READ-, spec §3.6, and Docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-02-combat-grid-terminology-ui-design.md. …note
MARK-, READ-, spec §3.6, and Docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-02-combat-grid-terminology-ui-design.md. The two axes of the stance grid were NAMED. You arm a Shape (Power / Special / Motion); you press an Input (Offensive / Defensive / Evasive); one of each is a Cell; nothing armed is Neutral. Mobility became Motion in the same pass. Seven carved marks were authored (Docs/m9/marks/.svg) and the in-fight readout designed — the readout is deliberately NOT built here; it belongs to E3, which now names its four requirements. Owner: Trevor (every design call) + Code (rename, reconciliation) · Seat: design session — the marks owe a look at true size, the readout owes a build · Done: vocabulary in CLAUDE + spec, eight requirement IDs with matching ledger rows, rename landed and compile-gated, --check green. WHY THIS IS AN INCREMENT AND NOT A DOC TIDY. Until this pass the columns of the grid had no name at all — every document referred to them by the key that fires them, so no sentence in the project could say what an M2 cell is. Naming them is what let the readout be specified, and the readout is what surfaced the finding below. The rename was label-only and provably so: StanceKind.Motion keeps value 3, EInputButtons.StanceMotion keeps index 8, and the compile gate is what proves every reader moved with the label — renaming a LIVE enum member is safe in a way retiring an index is not, and nothing was retired. 99 insertions against 99 deletions across 17 files, no reflow. THE FINDING, which is the part with teeth: the readout cannot be built against the current code. Every grid ability exposes a public yes/no readiness check, but no ability exposes how much cooldown is LEFT — the timers are private, and the only two public countdowns in the combat script are the shield lockout and the flow release window, neither of which is an ordinary cooldown. So READ-COOL's grey-out is free and its unwinding ring is not. Found alongside it: ten of the eleven grid abilities have a readiness check and the ring stance has none, its gate written inline instead — one instance of a shared mechanism missing while every sibling has it, on the ability this project has already been bitten by twice for being the odd one out. Both debts are recorded against E3. IT ALSO CLOSED FOUR DANGLING §9 REFERENCES (E2, E3, E5, E6). They cited a section that does not exist in the combat spec; the section they meant is in Docs/m9/PLANNING_HANDOFF.md, a superseded input since 2026-07-20 — four increments citing a retired doc for their design intent. Resolved by dropping the pointers, not re-homing them: that section is a disposition table, and all six of its rows were checked against the increments and found already stated inline in the increment that owns each one. Two claims were NOT carried inline and were rescued rather than lost — audio is co-equal with VFX for readability (into E2), and identity never rests on hue*, which turned out to be stated three times as local rationale and nowhere as a rule and is now READ-NOHUE. The transferable bit: a broken cross-reference was the symptom; a homeless invariant was the disease. ⚠ READ-NOHUE may belong in §2's LOCKED INVARIANTS rather than §3.6 — flagged for the owner, not promoted in passing.
33 M9-INC20 M9 increment C7 — The cost economy and the Special column rebuild (2026-08-02/03) [DONE]
spec §3.6 (the cost model, FLAME-, BURN-STACKS, ECANNON-), the matching PROJECT_STATE.md ledger rows …note
spec §3.6 (the cost model, FLAME-, BURN-STACKS, ECANNON-), the matching PROJECT_STATE.md ledger rows, and tools/gate.ps1 (muzzle and chesttwist rows with real success markers; strikephase/animinventory/animfull rows added without one yet, per this file's own rule that a marker must be read off a real run rather than guessed; -ExtraArgs plumbed through to the engine). Four pieces of work landed in one continuous pass and none of them had an increment until this one — exactly the drift this milestone was already reconciled once for (see the M9 status line's own note about the ring stance and the grid). Owner: Trevor (every design call) + Code (derivation, wiring, probes) · Seat: standalone player, owner-played · Done: the cost model is derived and applied; the flamethrower ships as a fuel-bar channel with a measured muzzle, a shield-model aim lock, a live steer the model visibly carries, and a flame front that arrives instead of switching on; burn stacks to 3 with pulses excluded from the count; the ember cannon is complete through tuning and dispatches nowhere; --check green. THE COST MODEL — ten cooldowns re-derived off one shared formula, instead of ten separate feel-passes answering the same underlying question differently. Anchor: a duel should last 90 seconds against 100 HP; cooldown = (damage ÷ the row's damage-per-second-of-commitment rate) + (metres of displacement × 0.57 s/m, itself derived from the plain dodge) − the ability's own duration. The inversion this corrected is the headline. Before the pass the flamethrower's own whole-sequence cap was already over the project's 40-damage ceiling (54 over a 3.0 s hold, a standing violation rather than a fresh cut), and Special's cheapest, weakest cell (FLURRY-CD) carried the kit's WORST damage-per-second-of-commitment on its THIRD MOST EXPENSIVE cooldown — inherited whole from the bicycle kick it replaced and never re-priced for the ability actually wearing it. Among the ten the formula moved: PDODGE-CD, SWEEP540-CD, RETREAT-CD, DRIFT-CD, FLURRY-CD, HOOK-CD and FB-CD. Deliberately left untouched: the Power barrage, whose cooldown is priced by the ring stance it is attached to rather than by its own damage — a different pricing question the formula was never meant to answer (spec §3.6's own note). THE FLAMETHROWER REBUILT WHOLE — a cooldown-gated blast became a fuel-bar channel. FLAME-HOLD's 5.0 s cap is now a TANK, not a fixed hold, refilling in FLAME-REFILL's 3.0 s (1.67x the drain rate, the asymmetry deliberate) behind a FLAME-REFILL-DELAY and a FLAME-MINFUEL floor; FLAME-CD stays at the 0 it was already set to rather than double-charging one commitment on two meters. Range pulled back to 6 m and DPS to 8 core so a full tank held point-blank sums to exactly 40 — the honest arithmetic the old 18 DPS × 3.0 s pairing had been silently over. The punch itself changed (FLAME-ANIM, cut from BLOCK AND PUNCH FIST after HAISHU was rejected as the wrong strike shape), gained a real apex root (FLAME-WINDUP, IGNITE-APEX applied to a channel rather than a strike), and the fire's ORIGIN moved from a derived guess to a measured one (FLAME-MUZZLE, MuzzleProbe — two guesses wrong before the probe replaced hand-derivation for good). The aim model became the shield's own: committed and flattened level at the press (FLAME-LOCK), with a live 25°/25° steer (FLAME-TWIST-YAW/-PITCH) whose muzzle rotates with the twist so the fire converges on the crosshair instead of running parallel to it beside it — the shield cannon's own convergence fix, reapplied here after the identical ~0.5 m-off symptom reappeared and was caught in the owner's scrutiny round. ChestTwist (FLAME-CHEST, gate chesttwist) makes the upper body carry that steer, computed each frame from the stream's own replicated direction so the picture cannot disagree with where the fire actually goes. And the cone now arrives rather than switching on: FLAME-FRONT gives it a 9.6 m/s advance from the fist instead of the whole 6 m existing on the ignition tick. BURN NOW STACKS (BURN-STACKS, owner: "allow this burn to stack (I wanna try it)") — up to 3, impacts adding a stack, pulse sources (the flamethrower's own pulses, the ring stance's drip) only ever refreshing. BURN-NOSTACK keeps its ID and its single-instance / no-DoT-ignites-DoT structure intact; stacking is a count riding on top of that structure, not a second instance, and the distinction pulses needed already existed as burnStyle before stacking was ever asked for. THE EMBER CANNON SHIPPED COMPLETE AND UNBOUND. ActionKind.EmberCannon = 19 (append-only wire format) is done through tuning (ECANNON-DAMAGE 13, ECANNON-SIZE 0.46 m, ECANNON-CD 2.0 s — all three the shield cannon's own numbers scaled by one ratio, 0.573), spawn (the general path, aim read fresh at the press, range falloff deliberately dropped), phases and reset, with its own two animator states cut from WHEEL KICK — built for Special-Evasive, then left unbound there when the owner chose to keep the directional 540 on that cell instead ("I wanted to keep the directional 540"). Binding it is one line in a Begin*Slot dispatcher, whenever the grid wants a fourth Special beat. THE INSTRUMENTATION THAT LANDED WITH IT: MuzzleProbe (gate muzzle) ended the guess-build-play loop that had placed a projectile origin wrong twice before it existed; ChestTwistBaker (gate chesttwist) bakes the new rig component onto Player.prefab and Bot.prefab alike; StrikePhaseProbe and AnimInventory's two entry points (Check/Full) gained gate rows without success markers yet, honestly, rather than a guessed one that fails open; and gate.ps1 gained -ExtraArgs, so a probe that takes its own arguments (a candidate clip, a limb filter) no longer means dropping to a raw batchmode call and eating the multi-minute teardown the wrapper exists to avoid.

THE FIND, and why the measurement round paid for itself before the owner ever touched the ability: the "range falloff deliberately dropped" claim was FALSE in code from the day the cannon was built. Fireball.CannonDamageFactor keyed "is this a cannon" on CannonRadius > 0 alone, so the ember cannon rode the shield cannon's 65% falloff curve: run 1 measured 8.96 at 6 m and 4.55 at 12 m — the 0.35 floor to the third decimal — against a designed flat 13. Every doc asserted the exemption; no code implemented it; nothing could have caught it but firing the ability, which nothing had ever done ("a comment asserting a property is not the property", now with a damage curve for a comment). Fixed by identity, not by shape: ShotAbility.EmberCannon = 5 appended (wire format), the spawn tagged with it, and the falloff exempts that tag — which also un-blinds the flight ledger, which had been recording ember shots as shield cannons. ECANNON-CD measured exact (2.00 s) and grid-spend held on the first run; run 2 confirmed the fix — flat 13.00 at 6 m and at 12 m, the number the design had claimed all along, now true.
