The morning's standing throw had read the written design as the whole intent; the intent was always the LEAP with the cannon as payload. So the cell settled as the 540 CANNON: the directional 540 leap (revived — SWEEP540-DIST back from a few hours of dead, BeginSweep540 live again) throwing the ember cannon from its crest at the point committed at the press — the retreat kick's own apex-payload pattern on the same shared latch, and the same one LaunchEmberCannon the dormant standing throw calls, so the two can never fork on what the projectile is. The trailing flame and Sweep540Cooldown stay retired: one press, one price, and the price is ECANNON-CD's, repriced 2.0 → 3.6 s because the pairing adds the 5 m displacement term the standing throw's own note said it didn't owe. Run 3 measured the settled cell whole: leap 5.40 m, damage flat 13.00 from 6 m AND from a 12 m cast (~14.5 m of true flight after the backward neutral leap), cooldown 3.60 s exact, grid-spend holding with Sweep540 as the second cast. With it: FLAME-WINDUP 0.75 → 0.975 s (+30%, owner) — under the natural-rate-plus-hold model the whole extension lands in the held apex root (0.45 → 0.675 s), no playback rate moved, states rebaked for the fade pair. The morning's "this cell does not evade" caveat died with the standing throw. JUDGED IN PLAY 2026-08-07, POSITIVE — owner: "I like the 540 cannon now a lot." The flamethrower's longer telegraph rode the same session without complaint; no separate verdict recorded on it.
35 M9-INC22 M9 increment C9 — The Motion-row ability pass (opened 2026-08-07, owner: "Lets move on to mobility moves pass") [PARTIAL]
the Special row's per-cell treatment applied to Motion's three cells (flurry, ember drift, retreat …note
the Special row's per-cell treatment applied to Motion's three cells (flurry, ember drift, retreat kick). First landed item, same day: THE FLURRY'S ARMS HAD BEEN FROZEN FOR FIVE DAYS AND NOBODY KNEW. Owner's opening report: "I don't see the punching animation at all anymore." A runtime probe on the bridge showed the layer machinery perfect — weight 1.0, states entered, parity alternating — but Flurry_A's state reporting an empty state's default length: its motion reference DANGLED. Cause: the 2026-08-02 flamethrower rebuild re-split BLOCK AND PUNCH FIST.fbx (the same FBX holding the flurry's right-hand punch), the re-import renumbered the AIR clip's internalID, and the controller's by-ID reference died — the fist was the ONE clip in the project still loaded by GUID+fileID while its sibling flick was loaded by name. Two gate lessons, both now recorded at the scene: (1) states had been printing WARN flurry clips missing... layer skipped inside a GREEN gate since 2026-08-02 — a warning inside a pass is a finding nobody is forced to read; (2) motions (the dangling-motion gate, built for exactly this) catches it in 17 s and was simply never run after the split — verified both directions today: it failed with Flurry_A -> missing motion before the fix and passes 61/0 after. Fix: the fist loads by name (FindClipByName, the mechanism built for this hazard), the rebake reassigned the clip, and the runtime probe re-read len=0.200 — the real 8-frame punch fitted to the shot gap. Confirmed by the owner watching the lab run: "It looks better!" A split run now owes a motions run, per ClipSplitter's header. Second landed item, the same day: THE RESTORED ARMS WERE SKEWED — "the right arm is punching up toward the sky." The raw clip measured LEVEL (StrikePhaseProbe, which gained an elevation column for the question — it had measured forward reach and azimuth but never pitch, the exact blind spot an upward punch hides in). The skew lives in the COMPOSITION: the masked FlurryUpper layer replaces spine+arms from the clip while hips stay the base walk's, and the AIR cut was authored mid-LUNGE (front foot +0.31 m, rear −0.61) with a spine that compensates for hips the mask throws away — re-based onto upright walking hips, the right fist rendered at +29..+35° elevation against an authored +9..+12. New instrument at the scene: FlurryPoseProbe (gate flurrypose) poses the real prefab with the layer at weight 1 and measures the composite — its first threshold was a guess (+35) that passed the known-bad at +34.9 while the owner pointed at the sky; recalibrated against that known-bad to +20-at-extension and verified to fail-then-pass across the fix. The fix ran FOUR answers across 2026-08-07→13, each retired on a number its predecessor couldn't measure: the jab's upright DOUBLE PUNCH FIST STRIKE composed +15.7° ("a bit higher than natural"); a chest pitch trim died on paper (the layer trims both beats or neither); the mirrored FLICK PUNCH JAB leveled at last (+1.7/+1.9, azimuths symmetric to half a degree) — and was a corkscrew: 96° of WRIST-LOCAL spin per beat on both hands ("hands are doing some kind of odd fast rotating motion"), uniform through the cut, so no re-cut could calm it. The probe grew with each round: a wrist-local metric (world rotation cannot tell a spin from an honest arm arc — the flick's 148° world was the LOWEST of the punch pool while its wrist was the highest), and an audition mode that measures candidates through the composite before any is wired — which caught its own hand-assumption bug when a right-handed candidate's "broken" numbers turned out to be the chambered off-hand, and whose self-override sanity check reproduced the wired baseline exactly. The measured-lineup pick, DOUBLE PUNCH PALM STRIKE (wrist 45°/beat, +11.6° at extension, ±12° azimuth), shipped clean on every number — and the owner's verdict was the week's hardest fail: "Its VERY wrong now... Elbows are fixed at a 90 and the hands are 'wiping' the fireballs out from each hand." A 3-frame impact pose stretched to 0.50x holds perfect poses at every sampled instant and does not MOVE like a punch; no metric in the kit measures whether a beat travels. That fourth failure is the finding: the instrument class is the gap, not the clip pool — pose sampling cannot certify motion. The flurry is PARKED on the mirrored flick (level and forward; the wrist-corkscrew complaint stands as its open item), and the pass pauses for the owner-directed pipeline rebuild: an isolated animation review environment and a beat-anatomy standard — Docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-13-animation-review-environment-and-pipeline.md, opened with the week's full gap inventory, research in flight. Third landed item, its own design record 2026-08-14 and five commits the same day: THE DRIFT CELL BECOMES A REAL HOVER, WITH A NEW HELD POSE, THREE FIRE JETS, AND A REPLACED LUNGE STRIKE. Owner's brief, quoted because each clause carried a separate decision: "I want this to be a true fire glide... hands raised up palms down and flame propelling down from the palms and out of the feet. Player lifts 6-12inch up from the floor during the glide," then on the lunge, "should use one of our jabs that fit in smoothly instead of the kick we have now, doesn't look natural," then, deciding the whole mechanism, "I'd like the real player to hover realistically." The hover is real — PlayerMovement pre-cancels gravity on the one line before its _kcc.Move call, so the capsule and its hit area rise together at 0.30 m (raised from the design record's 0.23 m the same day, to leave room for the centre jet), captured off a flat floor at the press and breathing on under-critical damping rather than snapping level (DRIFT-HEIGHT). ⚠ THE FRAME STRIP CHANGED THE DESIGN BEFORE A SINGLE CURVE WAS WRITTEN — the design record asked for arms raised and palms down, cut from the jump loop; a strip of the raw clip showed a flying TUCK instead, arms reaching forward and soles facing backward-up, which would have fired the foot jets into the character's own back had the brief been built as written. Shown the picture rather than told the problem, the owner kept the tuck on sight ("a flying glide rather than a standing hover"), which deleted the surgery pass the plan had budgeted for entirely — the clip is wired unmodified (DRIFT-POSE). Three procedural fire jets follow, two palms and one centred under the feet rather than one per foot (owner, mid-build: the tuck draws the feet together), each a nested core-and-flare cone clamped by construction so no jet can ever reach the floor, ordered after ChestTwist so the palm jets carry the arm's own twist instead of lagging it (DRIFT-JETS) — the project's first authored fire component, against a project with zero fire assets to import. The lunge trades BACK KICK CUT, a spinning kick that turned the character's back to the target and ran at the controller's second-fastest rate, for a jab cut sized by arithmetic rather than by eye: DOUBLE PUNCH FIST LUNGE, its apex landing 0.027 s before the lunge's own cone spawns, under two ticks at 64 Hz (DRIFT-STRIKE). ⚠ AND THE SAME GUARD-LIST GAP THAT HAS BITTEN THIS PROJECT BEFORE FOUND A SECOND VICTIM: AnyState -> Locomotion had carried every action from 1-12 and 14-18, and action 13 — the drift — was simply never named, because nothing had ever held a pose against it before. Adding ActDrift closed the one condition missing; without it, the drift's own recovery tick would have yanked the held pose back to Locomotion mid-hold, presenting as a fade bug rather than the guard-list gap it actually was. All four pieces landed as five commits with every relevant gate green — compile, states, motions, driftjet, split, audit, flow — and NONE of it has been played. One automated zero-ping lab run from 2026-08-02 remains the entire evidence base for the drift's underlying mechanics; the hover, the pose, the jets and the jab have never been judged from the owner's chair. THE FIRST PLAYTEST LANDED THE SAME DAY, AND IT FOUND WHAT THE LAB RUN COULD NOT. A temporary [Hover] trace, 2470 lines from the owner's session, showed every glide ending with the body 13-20 cm above its own captured floor, falling at ~0.8 m/s, with grounded FALSE — so for a fraction of a second after EVERY glide, all thirteen ground-gated abilities (dodge, jump, flow, ring, flurry, another drift) silently refused, with nothing on screen to say why. Root cause: a spring of 60 against a damping of 8 settles over about a second, against a 0.18 s rise and a 0.20 s fall — the hover was never going to arrive in time. Fixed two ways: spring 60 → 300 and damping 8 → 22 (the ratio held at 0.63, so the hover got faster without reading stiffer), plus a new land-bias term that sinks the target BELOW the floor as the profile falls, pulling the body down and planting it rather than easing toward a height it cannot reach in time — the capsule cannot pass the ground, so the overshoot is free. VERIFIED FIXED FROM A SECOND SESSION, 1124 trace lines: at profile zero the body reads its own standing height with grounded TRUE, and the hold now slightly overshoots its target (1.338 against 1.329) — the under-damped settle working as designed. Also answered: the design record's open question of whether the CharacterController would fight a small sustained lift near the floor. It does not. Four more owner calls landed on that same build. "Invert the cones so that the cones base comes from the hands and the feet and the cones tip sits at the bottom" — a jet leaves a nozzle at its WIDEST and tapers; the blast cone the jets were modelled on starts at an apex and spreads, so copying the blast's geometry had copied the wrong physics along with it. Heights cut by two thirds (hands 0.95 → 0.32 m, foot 1.25 → 0.42 m) and the half-angle parameters replaced by an explicit mouth radius, because with the cone this way round the mouth width is the thing being judged. "I also need the player to lean in the direction they are moving" — a new DriftLean, tilting the RIG rather than the networked root (the authoritative position stays server-simulated and upright, and HEAD-HITBOX rides the head bone so it leans with the body it protects — the honest choice, not the convenient one), pivoting at the FEET rather than the hips, because a hip pivot reads as sliding rather than tipping. ⚠ AND THE JETS WENT DARK THE SAME BUILD, FOR A REASON THE OWNER NAMED EXACTLY: "cones aren't following the hands and feet, they are fixed in the air and the body rotates around them." DriftJetVisual ran at execution order 101 and the new DriftLean at 102 — so every frame the jets sampled the hand and foot bones and THEN the lean rotated the whole rig out from under them, drawing fire from a pose that no longer existed by the time the frame reached the screen. The chain is now ChestTwist (100) → DriftLean (101) → DriftJetVisual (102), and the rule it earns, written at every scene in the chain: anything that reads a bone must run after everything that writes one. Two more screenshots, two more landmark corrections, next build. The hand jets left the WRISTS, because a Mixamo "hand" bone IS the wrist joint — fixed by walking 60% of the way toward the middle finger's first bone, a second landmark rather than a fixed offset in metres, since an offset right for one rig is silently wrong for the next. The foot jet rode the midpoint of both feet, which floats up beside whichever foot the held tuck pose happens to raise — it now picks whichever foot is LOWER every frame, which is also the one that reads as carrying the weight. Neither fault was visible to any instrument in the project — no probe here measures where a cosmetic mesh sits relative to a hand — which is why the animation rules already put the owner's eye last rather than treating a green gate as a verdict. ⚠ THE METER WAS NEVER BROKEN. THE ABILITY MOVED OUT FROM UNDER IT. Owner, after playing the hover: "I forgot we'd done that for the bar. I'm not sure I want to go in that direction anymore. Lets make it a true resource." DRIFT-CHARGE filled by DISTANCE and drew only above zero, which read as a live meter for as long as the drift was a fast walk — always moving, so always climbing. The fire glide is a hover you can SIT IN: hold still and the charge stays at zero, and the bar does not draw at all. Nothing in the HUD had changed; the ability had moved out from under it. DRIFT-FUEL replaces it whole, in the flamethrower's own tank shape rather than a new mechanism — a 3.0 s tank, shorter than the flamethrower's 5.0 because the glide is the fastest movement in the game in every direction, refilling in 2.5 s after a 0.4 s delay armed on every exit including running dry, with a 0.5 s minimum to open. The lunge's damage came off the old distance scale and onto the Motion band's flat middle, 7.75 — the owner's pick over the old ceiling of 9, because fixing at the ceiling would have quietly bought every lunge a free upgrade. ⚠ THIS CLOSES DRIFT-LUNGE's LONG-STANDING partial: the spec's damage floor and reach floor used to disagree by one shared scale, and there is no unearned lunge left for that disagreement to be about. Then the conversion itself changed shape. Owner: "Lets change the lunge to ignite a fireball instead of a close range fireblast." The cone is retired, not deleted — BlastKind.DriftLunge and its four fields survive unreferenced, the same treatment the bicycle kick's cone got. The damage stays flat for free: range falloff only exists on a nonzero cannon radius, so an ordinary fireball has no curve to apply in the first place. Three consequences flagged and left unpriced: lag compensation inverts (the cone resolved with no rewind by design — "the counter-play to a cone is to not be standing in it" — the fireball rewinds per-seat, handing the attacker rewind he did not have); the opponent gains counterplay of his own, since a fireball can be shielded and caught by flow where a cone could be neither; and DRIFT-CD's cost derivation was built from damage and displacement alone, with range appearing nowhere in it, so the conversion becoming a projectile leaves that cell owing a fresh derivation. Fourth landed item, 2026-08-14, commit eca97e7: THE RETREAT KICK TRADES ITS CONE FOR A THREE-BEAT FIREBALL CHAIN, MIRRORING THE VOLLEY ON DIRECT INSTRUCTION. Owner: "For the F+dodge... I want it to be a fireball instead of a close range blast and add 3 charges to it same way our chain volley works... just with a backward dodge," then, pinning the numbers down: "Just match the exact format on the numbers the chain volley uses." RETREAT-CHAIN borrows the Power volley's own window (6.0 s, decaying to 3.0 s then 1.5 s), floor (0.133 s) and per-beat cooldown push (1.35 s) VERBATIM — not re-derived, borrowed on direct instruction. ⚠ The floor's 0.133 s happens to equal the beat's own startup, also 0.133 s — two different fields from two different sources landing on the same number by coincidence, flagged so nobody reads a shared constant into the match, the same caution RETREAT-CD/PDODGE-CD already carry for their own coincidental tie. The beat's own startup and its 0.28 s recovery stay the KICK'S fields throughout, never the volley's, which is also why matching the kick's own numbers made three phase-table edits unnecessary — its floor already sat under its recovery. RETREAT-DAMAGE is retuned rather than killed: beat 1 keeps the old cone's measured 6.48 core, beats 2 and 3 take the volley's own 70% follow-up ratio (4.54 each), 15.6 total, comfortably inside the 40-damage sequence cap. PlayerFireBlast.BlastKind.RetreatPulse and its four cone fields retire unreferenced, the same treatment the bicycle kick's and the drift lunge's cones already got. ChainKind.Retreat = 4 and ShotAbility.Retreat = 7 are both appended (wire format, append-only). RETREAT-GROUNDED HAS NO PRECEDENT IN THE KIT. Every other chain — the volley, the emberhook, the drift lunge's conversion — is a grounded ability; the retreat kick is a committed airborne ARC that owns the capsule for its flight, and the chain machinery re-opens a follow-up in Startup, a phase outside the airborne set. Opening beat two mid-flight would have dropped the airborne flag and handed the body back to ordinary movement halfway through a jump, so each beat must land before the next can open. Owner's call, and it costs nothing to require — the window is 6.0 s, a landing is roughly 0.8 s. Rhythm: kick, land, kick, land, kick. THREE SILENT HALF-ABILITIES CAUGHT BEFORE PLAY, worth recording because each would have LOOKED correct. (1) The apex latch that fires each beat's shot cleared where the ABILITY begins rather than where each ARC does — beats two and three would have dodged backward and thrown nothing. (2) The arc was built once at the first press and no follow-up ever re-entered that method, so beats two and three would have flown beat one's STORED direction — the way the body faced at the first press, even across a turn — which only ever matched RETREAT-DIST's "from FACING" rule by coincidence, since facing used to lock for the whole (then single-beat) action; it now rebuilds fresh per beat, off the body's LIVE facing. (3) The kick never captured an aim POINT, because a cone never needed one, so the fireball would have flown at whatever the last ability had aimed at — a stale aim point that does not error, it just fires somewhere the player never looked. All three were caught and fixed before any build shipped with them live, the same discipline DRIFT-LUNGE's own aim-point catch banked eight days ago in this same milestone. ⚠ A PRE-EXISTING BUG WAS FOUND AND FIXED ON THE WAY, AND IT IS NOT PART OF THIS ABILITY — recorded here because this is the code path that surfaced it. DropAction cleared the action, the shield, the pending ignition and the heat, but left the chain kind, its window and its pending flag alive. A player killed or gated mid-chain kept a live window that went on claiming its continue key — the same ~1.9 s swallowed-press failure class of 2026-08-02, reached down a path nobody had checked, and it survives a respawn in any session with no GameManager. Same file, same shape, as the IgnitePending line directly above it, added for the frozen drift and never swept down to its neighbour. Owner: Trevor (reports + verdicts + design calls) + Code (probes, diagnosis, fixes, gates) · Seat: runtime + edit-mode probes, lab runs, two playtest sessions with a [Hover] trace read afterward; owner's eye on the result · Done so far: flurry parked on the mirrored flick pending the pipeline; the drift cell built end to end and played twice — a real hover with its landing bug found and fixed, three re-shaped fire jets, a body lean, a true fuel tank replacing the old distance charge, and the lunge re-cut as a flat-damage fireball; the retreat-kick cell converted from a single cone into a three-beat fireball chain mirroring the volley, with its own must-land-between-beats rule and three pre-play bug catches, plus a pre-existing chain-leak bug (DropAction) fixed alongside it. All three Motion cells have now been built by this pass — the per-cell conversion work is complete. What remains is play, not construction: the fuel tank, the jets, the lean and the drift fireball are judged in a sandbox only; the retreat chain is judged nowhere at all; the bot chains at the emberhook but not at the retreat kick; and RETREAT-CD (like DRIFT-CD beside it) has no cooldown formula yet for a multi-beat, positional ability, only for the single hit each was priced against.
36 M9-INC23 M9 increment C10 — The animation pipeline: AnimReview lands (2026-08-13, owner: "Go ahead and build the AnimReview scene") [PARTIAL]
increment (ii) of the design record's five (Docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-13-animation-review-environme …note
increment (ii) of the design record's five (Docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-13-animation-review-environment-and-pipeline.md). The isolated environment exists and both seats work. -playtestMode AnimReview loads a one-object scene (everything else — grid, light, cameras, trails — is built by AnimReviewController at runtime, so the scene cannot drift from its baker): the hero rig on a gray unit grid, NO Fusion session, NO combat code. RAW mode drives any of 89 baked clips (controller clips + both packs + the Emberborn folder, labels <fbx>::<clip> because cut names collide by design) through a manual PlayableGraph — frame-exact scrub, 1/0.25/0.1x, frame-step, pre-sampled whole-arc hand trails. COMPOSITE mode drives the real controller (the flurry preset: mask, mirror seam and all), labeled on screen, because the week proved the two truths diverge. Cameras: over-shoulder (acceptance), ortho profile (arcs/elbows/elevation), ortho top (azimuth/wipes), orbit. The agent seat is the load-bearing half: -animReviewClip <label> -animReviewOut <dir> emits a 60-sample hand-velocity profile to [AnimReview] log lines and exports a 24-frame PNG strip (3 cameras × 8 moments), then quits — and the agent READS the images. Proven the day it landed: the first strip ever exported surfaced two findings no bone probe had ever caught (the flick strikes with OPEN, SPREAD FINGERS — finger bones are in no probe — and its ~77° stage-facing offset is visible on sight) plus one bug in the strip itself (the 100% frame wrapped to the start pose; fixed to clamp, with the lesson in the code: a sample asked for the end must show the END). AnimReviewBaker (gate animreviewbake) builds/reconciles the scene and registers it in the build list. Owner: Trevor (directive + review) + Code (research, design, build) · Seat: the environment itself, both modes · Done so far: D2 MVP live and CONFIRMED WORKING BY THE OWNER 2026-08-13 (after the inc23b input fix — the first owner-mode launch shipped dead controls and an unpositioned camera; one legacy-input fault, three symptoms); owed next: D1 threshold calibration, D3 BeatProbe (gate beat), D4 workflow rollout, then the flurry re-solved through it as the proving case (D5-iv) and the roster audit (D5-v). THE RECONCILIATION LANDED THE SAME DAY (owner: "resolve all existing raw animations back to their original state and fix all of the animations that are rotated inappropriately... reconcile any existing USED animation"). Four moves, each gated: (1) all 41 split takes' pristine originals RESTORED to the pool as <take> ORIGINAL (restoreoriginals) — additive, because the FBX bytes were never edited (all splicing lives in import settings, which also answered the owner's "reimport from the bundles?" with NO NEED) and deleting cuts would dangle ~45 wired motions; (2) the FACING AUDIT (facingaudit) measured every original and found TWO PACK BASELINES, not forty errors — V1 ~7° (owner: fine), V2 ~37° (owner: fix), plus V1 jump/block outliers at 54–72° past baseline; (3) the fixes applied as measured rigid yaws (fixfacings) — with both predicted traps fired and beaten the same hour: the importer field RENDERS NEGATED (the first, negative-signed pass doubled every error to 74°/151°, caught because the audit was rebuilt onto the Animator path after raw-curve sampling proved structurally blind to its own fixes), and the ID renumbering dangled EIGHT states (jump, dodge, both cannons, ring windup) — all eight now wired BY NAME, the flurry's five-day lesson industrialized into both state tables; (4) end state measured: 14 centered, 0 off-center, 26 turning (technique rotation on centered baselines), motions 61/0, flow probe clean, 129 clips rebaked into AnimReview. The owner's flurry candidate — the double-palm take "played twice" — stands recorded for after his library review. THE ROSTER PASS'S THREE QUEUED SURGERIES LANDED, AND THE FLURRY WENT THREE MORE ROUNDS — one thread closed clean, the other hit a wall curve surgery cannot cross. A new ResolveClip helper in CombatStates.cs reads a clip value containing / as a surgery asset and anything else as the raw FBX sub-clip name, and a MISSING surgery asset returns NULL rather than falling back to the raw cut — deliberate, because the raw cut is the exact pose the surgery exists to replace, so a silent fallback would restore a known-bad pose behind a green gate. Five clips are wired through it: PUNCH SWING/HOLD/RELEASE FIRM now drives the three Flamethrower_Swing/Hold/Release states, fixing a held pose that had extended into a drooped wrist and half-open clawed fingers on the kit's most-watched frame; DOUBLE PUNCH FIST STRIKE FIRM now drives FB_Strike's parity slots A, C and D (D mirrored, slot B the palm strike and deliberately untouched), fixing a held final frame that sprang the fist open with splayed fingers on every neutral jab; BLOCK RAISE GROUNDED was built for Shield_Raise and is deliberately NOT wired — see the review findings at the end of this entry. One bug caught in passing: the Shield_Raise sync line printed a hardcoded "<- BLOCK RAISE" no matter what it actually wired, so a correct swap would have read as a failure. Gates green across compile, states, motions (61/0), audit and flow — ⚠ and the flow probe ran against a STASHED pre-change baseline as a controlled comparison: 29 findings before, 29 after, the same list, ~1% numeric drift on three pre-existing findings — the one check no frame strip can give, because a strip samples poses and cannot see the path between them. Owner's verdict on playing it: "small improvements on a few other animations." THE FLURRY'S LEFT HAND TOOK THREE MORE ROUNDS AND SPLIT IN TWO (owner: "Flurry left hand still twisting into the chest. Doesn't look like anything we just did made any difference"). FlurryPoseProbe gained a CHAMBER TRACK — the non-striking hand's lateral offset in the chest's own frame, sampled across the whole loop — because every existing metric followed the EXTENDED hand, so a hand folding across the body while its partner punched was invisible to the probe even as it kept returning "VERDICT: FORWARD." Measured: the left hand sits at or across the midline for 16 of 21 samples, about three-quarters of the loop, roughly 0.20 m off the chest — that is how the take is authored, not a curve fault. Round 1's pin had WORKED and its REFERENCE was wrong: refTime 0.13 s is normalized 0.28, where the hand measures -0.009 lateral, dead on the midline — it froze the hand in the complained-of pose, and a held wrong pose reads as no change, which is why the round looked like a no-op instead of a miss. Round 2 moved the reference to 0.33 s and measured WORSE (+0.027 against -0.009) and was reverted. Two re-posing attempts cost more than they bought: damping UpperChest Twist Left-Right 55% pulled the chamber under 0.005 m — the shoulder hangs off the upper chest and the twist carries the arm with it, 1.287 of swing in the supposedly-held window — but pushed the right palm's own strike +0.107 to +0.145 lateral, pure cost for no gain; an explicit re-pose of the off arm threw it behind the body at shoulder height at -0.9, parked it by the ear at -0.7, and did not visibly shut the hand at a -0.5 finger close, every setting worse than the authored chamber. Both are reverted and recorded in the tool. What stuck: Left Forearm Twist In-Out moved from a 0.3 damp to a full pin — it measured the largest movement of any left channel inside the supposedly-held window, a 1.823 spread, and is the channel the job's own note names as the main offender — plus Left Forearm Stretch, the elbow, pinned for the first time by any op. Net: the twisting is fixed and measured; the position is authored into the take, and needs a re-posed chain or a different take — that is authoring, not curve surgery. ⚠ THE INSTRUMENT LESSON, worth keeping past this one clip: the chamber track APPROVED the -0.9 re-pose (clearance 0.20 → 0.28 m off the chest) while the frame strip showed a diving pose nobody would ship. Clearance is a necessary number and nowhere near a sufficient one — nothing in the probe kit yet separates "out at the side" from "flung backward," the same instrument-class gap the week already paid for once, when pose sampling alone could not certify motion. THEN THE STRIP REVIEW OF THE FIVE WIRED CLIPS OVERTURNED HALF OF THE ABOVE, WHICH IS THE POINT OF RUNNING IT. Two findings, both verified on the named frames before anything was changed. (1) THE HAND FIXES WERE RUNNING AT HALF STRENGTH, BY ARITHMETIC NOBODY HAD DONE. Weight ramps an op in over feather from its start AND out over feather to its end, so when 2 x feather >= the range the two ramps cross before either reaches 1: the op peaks at 0.5 in the middle and falls to nothing at both edges. Four ops were specified exactly that way — the flamethrower's swing wrist and finger close, its release, and the jab's tail — so each ran at half the value its Job asked for, weakest at the extension end, which is the only part anyone watches. The reviewer's words for it were "the fist is firm while chambered and opens as the arm extends", which is that curve described from the outside. ClipSurgeon now FAILS the gate on such an op rather than warning (a warning inside a passing run is a finding nobody is forced to read), verified in both directions — an injected 0.09 feather over a 0.14 range exits 1 naming the op and its 0.39 peak. Separately, the value itself was wrong: one RightHand. selector had to serve both curl and spread, and -0.6 rendered a half-open claw. Selectors now join terms with & (RightHand.&Stretched), so curl goes to -1.0 and spread to -0.3 independently. ⚠ The fingers themselves remain UNCONFIRMED VISUALLY: at strip resolution a hand is ~30 px, so finger shape is below what that instrument resolves. The numbers are right; the owner's orbit-and-zoom pass in AnimReview is what can settle the look. (2) BLOCK RAISE GROUNDED LIFTS THE CHARACTER OFF THE FLOOR AND IS THEREFORE UNWIRED. Pinning both legs did remove the knee-lift, and across the 0.2 s raise the lowest silhouette pixel climbs ~26 px — about a foot's height, monotonically — while the head sinks the same amount; the figure compresses from both ends, and every other clip in the set holds flat ground contact, so it is the edit and not the render. Pinning leg MUSCLES cannot hold a foot on the ground, because foot height is the root curve plus the leg chain and the op owns only the second half — the same class of mistake as pinning an arm while the torso carries it, met twice in one day from opposite ends of the body. A guard that hovers is worse than a hand clipping a thigh, so the raw cut stays wired and the hand-through-thigh finding returns to the owed list. THE FLURRY IS RESOLVED, AND IT TOOK EIGHT CONFIGURATIONS BECAUSE THE FAULT WAS NEVER IN THE CLIP POOL. Six more takes were tried in one evening — the palm take's whole 1-2, its 3-frame second beat, the neutral-jab take's two authored strikes alternating, the palm's right-hand drive alone, two mask configurations — and every one failed on the OFF HAND or on aim, because every take in these packs chambers its off hand across the chest: the chamber IS the technique, and a masked upper-body layer inherits that chamber while discarding the hips it was authored against. What landed is Combo Punch.fbx, an 89-frame Mixamo take the owner uploaded himself: StrikePhaseProbe measured all six of its punches, and the owner's instinct to use "the last 4" is right ON THE NUMBERS — f52 L, f56 R, f67 L, f72 R alternate hands and all land LEVEL (elevation -9 to -1°), against +21° on the take's first two, the skyward read that dogged every earlier candidate. Cut COMBO FLURRY = f48-80, bounds chosen for the BLEND rather than the punches, both hands retracted at f48 and settled by f80. ⚠ THE CLIP IS NOT LOOPING, AND THAT FIXED TWO PHANTOM PUNCHES. The owner reported "you added 2 punches and 2 shots" though the shot count had been 4 all day; the ability runs LONGER than the clip (startup 0.18 s + shots + recovery 0.25 s) and the masked layer holds full weight for the WHOLE action, so a looping clip wrapped and replayed its first 0.43 s — exactly the punches at f52 and f56. The clip is now fitted to the whole ACTION and does not loop; Unity holds the final retracted frame as a settle. ⚠ AND THE PROJECT'S FACING TOOL IS INERT ON THIS LAYER, the most reusable finding of the night. The punches rendered 40° to the character's left; orientationOffsetY was written into the importer at -41 (confirmed in the .meta) and the composite azimuth did not move a tenth of a degree, because that field rotates the ROOT and this layer masks the root off — every facing pass this project has ever run works through the root, so none could ever have straightened the flurry regardless of which clip was in it. The residual 40° is the take's own hip yaw (25-45°) being discarded by the mask. The correction went into the CHEST CURVES instead, via a new ClipSurgeon op, ADD, which OFFSETS a channel while keeping its shape (Pin/Damp/Set all destroy the motion they touch); the amount was SOLVED, not guessed — composite mean azimuth read -41.4° at offset 0.00 and -23.5° at +0.30, 59.7° per unit, so 41.4/59.7 = +0.69. ⚠ AND +0.69 OVERSHOT BY ~14°, which is a lesson about the MEASUREMENT rather than the arithmetic: both calibration points were sampled at the probe's old HARDCODED sample times, which belonged to a clip this one replaced — on this take they fall between punches and after the last one, so the fit was exact about instants that are not strikes. Once the probe was taught to find the real peaks (0.08 L, 0.28 R, 0.53 L, 0.73 R — the four punches, alternating) the residual became measurable and the value settled at +0.46: azimuth -4.4/-2.2/+2.2/+4.4° (mean zero, symmetric either side), elevation 2.2-6.6°, reach 0.47-0.49 m, travel intact. Square, level, and the punch keeps its motion. Timing: _flurryShotGap is 0.2125, the owner's 0.85 s over four shots; the whole action is 1.28 s and the clip is fitted to all of it at 0.83x, inside the beat standard's band. A separate, kit-wide fix rode the same pass (owner: "looking downward at a target from high ground still has the chest and arms firing perfectly flat") — ChestTwist already rotated spine/chest/upper-chest by an aim's yaw AND pitch but was gated to the flamethrower alone; it now also runs for the jab, volley, forward shot, flurry and flow release, driven by the [Networked] PlayerCombat.AimPitch, torso taking 75% of the angle and the arms the rest. ⚠ The clamps are ASYMMETRIC ON PURPOSE, 45° down against 20° up, because a spine flexes forward 60-80° and extends backward only 25-35° — a symmetric clamp would snap the model into a backbend on an uphill shot; sign verified rather than assumed (HeadLook builds Euler(AimPitch, yaw, 0), positive Euler X pitches down). Tooling built or repaired, all verified in both directions: AnimImport.Retarget (new) prepares a downloaded FBX — Humanoid, avatar copied from the pack mannequin, materials off — and ⚠ FALLS BACK to building an avatar from the model itself, because an outside file is usually not on the pack skeleton (the mannequin avatar wants a hip bone named pelvis, and a failed rig exposes NO CLIPS AT ALL, a zero that reads exactly like a corrupt download and cost one needless re-export before the import log was read); it also re-imports before counting clips, since reading sub-assets straight after SaveAndReimport returns the OLD set. ClipSurgeon gained the ADD op above and & selectors (RightHand.&Stretched) so finger CURL and SPREAD take different values, after one shared selector at -0.6 rendered a half-open claw, plus a feather guard that now FAILS the gate instead of warning — the finding that caught four hand ops running at half power in the first place. FlurryPoseProbe gained a CHAMBER TRACK (the non-striking hand, which no metric had followed), a REACH column and floor, and a TRAVEL test. ⚠ Its elevation test had been ONE-SIDED for its whole life — el > 20, born from a "punching at the sky" complaint — so a composite at -60° with the fists BEHIND the body printed "VERDICT: FORWARD" while the owner reported chaos on screen: a threshold shaped by one direction of one bug is blind to its mirror image.
37 M9-INC24 M9 increment C11 — The two owed cooldown derivations, and one dead term found on the way (2026-08-14) [PARTIAL]
RETREAT-CD, DRIFT-CD, FB-CD/FB-FOLLOWUP, MOVE-BACK, DRIFT-SPEED, and one new term in spec §3.6's cost …note
RETREAT-CD, DRIFT-CD, FB-CD/FB-FOLLOWUP, MOVE-BACK, DRIFT-SPEED, and one new term in spec §3.6's cost model. C9 closed by naming its own debt — "RETREAT-CD (like DRIFT-CD beside it) has no cooldown formula yet for a multi-beat, positional ability" — and this increment pays it. THE HEADLINE IS THAT THE MISSING TERM WAS NEVER MISSING; IT WAS A DISCOUNT NOBODY HAD WRITTEN DOWN. The retreat chain ships at 5.6 s, recorded at the charge site as "the owner's instruction… a deliberate departure rather than smuggled in as a derivation" against a model that asked 7.7. Price a repeat beat with its displacement counted at half and the model returns 5.69 — beat one at 2.93, beats two and three at 1.38 each — against the shipped 5.60, agreement inside a tenth of a second. So §3.6 gains exactly one clause, a repeated metre of displacement counts as half a fresh one, RETREAT-CD closes as derived rather than as a departure, and no shipped number moves. The justification is the one the code already gave for distrusting the 7.7: that arithmetic treats 10.5 m as three separate dodges, and the third retreat is not a fresh dodge, because the opponent has already read the first two. ⚠ Recorded against itself: the term is fitted to ONE ability's owner-set number, so it is not independent evidence the model is right — the same circularity §3.6 already admits about its dodge-derived 0.57 constant, and it needs a second ability before it can be trusted as a rule. ⚠ THE OTHER HALF OF THAT DEBT WAS ALREADY SETTLED IN A COMMENT NOBODY HAD READ. Three documents said nobody had decided whether a mixed evade/damage chain should price per link or whole-at-the-opener. The dispatch site had decided it: the emberhook arms its whole price at the opener because its length is FIXED; the volley and the retreat chain push per link because their length is the PLAYER'S choice. The principle was sound and simply never reached the spec or the ledger. The lesson is the inverse of this project's usual one — the docs are normally ahead of the code and stale; here the code was ahead of the docs and right. DRIFT-CD — 3.7 → 3.31, AND THE ERROR WAS A STALE INPUT NO GATE COULD SEE. The derivation priced 9 damage. The owner retired the 6.5-to-9 charge ramp earlier the same day and fixed the lunge at a flat 7.75, and the cooldown was never re-run: 7.75 ÷ 3.5 = 2.21, plus 3.2 m at 0.57 = 1.83, less the ~0.73 s the lunge takes — 3.31. ⚠ The class matters more than the 0.39 s: a cooldown is DERIVED from other fields, and nothing joins a derived number to its inputs. The prefab check proved 3.7 was faithfully stored; it proved 7.75 was faithfully stored; both were green while the arithmetic between them was wrong. The pair rule the master rule already states, applied to a pair living in two different fields. AND THE SAME CELL WAS CHARGING TWICE. The timer was armed at the glide's PRESS, which did two wrong things at once — it billed the lunge's price (the number is derived from the lunge's damage and the lunge's 3.2 m) whether or not you ever lunged, and it made this the one cell in the kit paying a cooldown and a fuel bar for a single commitment. _flamethrowerCooldown's own note calls that "the exact double-charge the project refuses elsewhere", and its cooldown is zero for precisely this reason; the drift was doing the thing the note forbids, one field away from the note. The arm moves to the conversion. ⚠ What that deliberately buys the player, flagged so nobody later reads it as a slip: dropping the glide unspent is now cheap, short taps are cheap in a way a full lockout never allowed, and the refill delay is the only thing standing against feathering. At the shipped tank, delay and refill this settles near a 50% duty cycle — roughly 3 m/s sustained and omnidirectional, above the forward sprint. UNJUDGED; watch it first. ⚠ The RANGE debt this row carried is NOT paid by any of this and survives intact: the conversion is a projectile now, the attacker gained lag-compensation rewind a cone never had, and the opponent gained counterplay a cone never allowed (a fireball can be shielded and caught by flow). Judged to roughly offset, and that judgement is unmeasured. ⚠ FOUND ON THE WAY, AND THE BIGGEST FINDING OF THE SESSION: THE VOLLEY'S CHAIN COOLDOWN HAD A DEAD TERM AND HAD NEVER ESCALATED. Each beat rebuilt its cooldown from PhaseTimer.RemainingTime — but that write runs inside OnActivate, which the phase loop calls on the tick the phase timer expires and before it re-arms, so the term was always ~0. Every beat wrote the same ~1.82 s, and beat three cost exactly what beat two did. The chain's price did not grow with its length at all — against the line's own stated intent, against the field note claiming base + 2 × push = 4.3, and against TOPO-SHAPE's roster-wide rule that a shape's cost escalates per release. Now each beat extends the running cooldown by one push, reaching 1.6 + 1.35 + 1.35 = 4.3 s of cycle from the opener press — the number the field note has claimed all along — against a model wanting 4.62, so 7% under. A dead term reads exactly like a live one; it is only ever zero. ⚠ Two corrections rode with it. The old derivation cited 37.5 damage for a three-beat volley; the true figure is 30, because follow-ups take the 0.7 ratio — a wrong input that lands near the right answer leaves nothing to trip over. And a recovery term was dropped from the per-beat sum as double-counting, since the model already subtracts the ability's own duration and recovery is part of it. The class sweep the master rule demands came back CLEAN: every other read of that term samples a fraction during a live phase, which is correct; the cooldown write was the only one reading it at the instant of expiry. ⚠ New and unmeasured: a slow chain is now cheaper than a fast one, because an expired cooldown contributes nothing to the next beat's sum. The emberhook shares that property. The retreat chain does not — its push is an absolute base + push × (beat−1). Two chains, two shapes, both landing on their target; unifying them would move a shipped number. TWO OWNER CALLS ON MOVEMENT LANDED IN THE SAME PASS. The fire glide goes 3.3 → 4.0 m/s and is now the fastest movement in the game by design — 1.6× the sprint, 2.0× the walk, omnidirectional where the sprint is forward-only — affordable only because it is a fuelled state you cannot attack out of. And walking becomes ONE SPEED IN EVERY DIRECTION (owner: "I want the walk forward speed to match all the other walk direction speed"), backpedal 1.4 → 2.0 to meet forward and strafe. ⚠ That RETIRES MOVE-BACK's design reason, and it is recorded as a retirement rather than as a requirement met — the differential existed so that advancing was itself a small commitment, and closing distance is free in both directions now. Editing a requirement down to match the code is the one move this project's authority rules forbid outright, so the row says what was lost. ⚠ MOVE-SPRINT's forward-only rule loses half its stated justification with it ("would undo MOVE-BACK" — there is no longer a MOVE-BACK to undo) and stays, on its other reason alone, which was always the load-bearing one: an omnidirectional sprint is a dodge with no cooldown. Flagged rather than quietly re-justified. ⚠ A NEW FAILURE MODE, AND NO GATE IN THIS PROJECT CAN CATCH IT: A RELATIONSHIP ROTTED WHILE BOTH ITS NUMBERS STAYED GREEN. DRIFT-SPEED's row described the glide as "above a walk and near the sprint." That was true when it shipped. The field then never moved while the walk family was rescaled twice underneath it (sprint 3.46 → 3.01 → 2.05 → 2.5), so long before today a glide described as "near the sprint" was already 32% faster than one. The prefab check binds the number, and the number was correct on every run. A sentence that describes one value in terms of another is a claim with no owner — re-read it whenever either side moves. Same session, same shape, three more found: the per-link push reads +0.7 s in spec §1.5 and the ledger against a live 1.35; FB-CD's derivation cites "the Power row's 5.0" when Power is 6.5 and 5.0 is Special's (the number is right and the label is wrong — a neutral press is not a Shape, has no row rate, and 5.0 is the kit-wide target rate the model was built from); and the movement prose across four files still quotes the 2.41/3.01 generation. Owner: Trevor (three calls: the glide's charge, the two movement numbers, the volley fix) + Code (derivations, the dead-term diagnosis, the sweep) · Seat: compile gate + --check; the feel verdict is owed entirely to the owner's chair · Done so far: both owed derivations closed — one as a discount the model was missing, one as a stale input re-run; the drift's double-charge ended and its price moved onto the conversion it prices; the volley's chain cooldown made to escalate for the first time since it shipped; two movement numbers retuned on owner call with the requirement they retire recorded openly. Owed next: ALL OF IT PLAYED. Nothing in this increment has been judged in a fight — the glide's new duty cycle is the first thing to watch, the volley's escalation is a real nerf to the kit's strongest sequence, and one walk speed in every direction changes every spacing claim in the interaction triangle.
38 M9-INC25 M9 increment C12 — The movement pass: the sprint retires, the walk is re-solved, and the jump gets a speed of its own (2026-08-14/15) [PARTIAL]
MOVE-FWD, MOVE-BACK, MOVE-SPRINT, plus the locomotion blend and two new components. FIVE CONFIGURATIONS …note
MOVE-FWD, MOVE-BACK, MOVE-SPRINT, plus the locomotion blend and two new components. FIVE CONFIGURATIONS OF THE WALK WERE BUILT AND FOUR WERE WRONG, and the shape of the mistake is worth more than the fix. The owner reported the walk as "very robotic". Three fixes aimed at the hips — a Damp on the hip curve, Unity's foot-IK checkbox, and a full curve reshape through a new Wave op — were each built, measured and reverted; every one made the planted foot WORSE and none touched the complaint. The hips were never the fault. The owner located it himself: "it goes away when two directions are used at once, only forward and backward." The controller said why — forward and back carried timeScale 1.0 while every strafe carried 0.508 (= 1.00/1.97), so the two 1.97 s walk takes were played at TWICE their authored rate while the 1.00 s strafes ran at their own. A slow, deliberate performance run at double speed loses every settle in it, on exactly the two directions named. ⚠ THE FIX THAT FOLLOWED WAS WRONG TOO, AND IT WAS MINE. Dropping the forward/back children so the diagonals covered them fixed the tempo — and introduced a left leg tracing a circle (sideways travel 38.6 cm against 49.3 fore-aft), a stride cut from 89 cm to 49, and 63% ground slide. The owner said he did not believe the raw backward take had the orbit. He was right: traced individually the two dropped takes measure 0.06 / 0.22 sideways-over-fore-aft — the cleanest in the set — while every diagonal orbits on its own (0.42 to 1.88). The orbit existed in no input; the mixing made it. Restored at the new 1.4 m/s walk they need only 1.71x playback, so the tempo fault does not return: slide 63% → 6%, orbit gone, stride 49 → 89 cm. The lesson, recorded at the scene: a fault measured downstream of a mix is not evidence about any one input, and three rounds were spent asserting otherwise from a real, reproducible measurement of the wrong quantity. ⚠ A REGRESSION FOUND ON THE WAY, invisible and ours: with no child at pure forward the input landed BETWEEN neighbours and the blend's centre — the IDLE — took 11.8% weight while walking flat out, shortening every stride by ~9%. Fixed by pulling the walking children to 0.8 of the unit circle so full stick clears the ring; a single take now plays at weight 1.000. Nothing failed, the walk simply got quietly shorter-strided. THE SPRINT IS RETIRED (owner: "Remove the sprint completely, our fire glide replaces it effectively") — EInputButtons.Sprint = 4 retained and unbound, both writers and the single reader removed after a reader sweep run before the unbind, which is the discipline the ring stance's three-day silent death earned. MOVE-SPRINT is dead, its prefab binding removed rather than repointed (a binding on a dead requirement spends the gate's credibility on a fiction). ⚠ Two consequences named rather than discovered: the bot loses its only closing gear and now tops out at the walk against a player who leaves at 4.0 on the glide; and _maxGaitScale did NOT die with the sprint — the glide saturates it, and its "sprint's gear ratio" label was false the moment the ability went. THE WALK WENT 2.0 → 1.85 → 1.4, one walk speed in every direction throughout, each step measured against foot slide (18% → 11% → the number the takes could actually carry). ⚠ _authoredGaitSpeed moved 0.82 → 0.26 → 0.82 with the tree it describes: a stale divisor does not error, it quietly changes which term binds, and at one point it stretched the gait cycle 0.98 s → 1.15 s and silently undid a tempo the owner had just approved. THE JUMP GETS A SPEED OF ITS OWN (owner: "decouple from the walking speed and double it") — _jumpHorizontalSpeed 2.8, direction still taken from movement so a standing jump still goes straight up. This also repairs a silent 26% loss: the jump had no speed of its own and stashed whatever ground velocity was available, so retiring the sprint had shortened every jump-in with no number edited and no gate able to see it. ChestBobDamp — NEW COMPONENT. The hips must move because the legs are drawn against them, so the upper body absorbs part of their travel instead, the way a spine does. ⚠ Shipped with a bug and fixed the same hour: it averaged hip height while AIRBORNE, so landing from a jump yanked the chest against a stale average and stretched the spine for about a second. Now the average freezes off the ground, grounding is read from the controller rather than inferred from which ability is running, and the offset is clamped. GaitBobProbe (gate gaitbob) — NEW INSTRUMENT, and it made most of the real findings. It traces hip, foot, hand and chest paths through the live Animator, plus blend weights, foot carry, arm swing, cycle counts and pair-cancellation correlations. It exists because every other height probe here samples a RAW CLIP and therefore cannot see blending, cadence or IK. It settled the foot-IK question in twenty minutes, caught the idle bleed, and finally measured the raw takes that reversed the whole diagnosis. Owner: Trevor (every verdict, and both of the two observations that actually located the faults) + Code (probes, fixes, reverts) · Seat: standalone player, owner-played · Done so far: sprint retired kit-wide with its sweep and its consequences recorded; walk re-solved at 1.4 with the original takes, 6% slide and no orbit; idle bleed closed; jump decoupled; chest damp shipped and its landing bug fixed. Owed: the pack's leading-stance asymmetry — the right leg strides about a third of the left in every take — which no wiring change reaches and which is an import, not a tuning. ⚠ THE PROCESS FAILURE, recorded because it cost more than any of the bugs. Twice I ran an analysis the owner asked for and then shipped its conclusion instead of reporting it — once undoing a change he had explicitly approved and reinstating the complaint that started the thread. His words: "STOP DOING THINGS I DONT ASK… your persistence to drift into your own decisions is infuriating." Every round that went well began with a question; every round that went in circles began with me deciding. Baked to memory as deliver-analysis-not-decisions.

42 M9-INC29 M9 increment C16 — The dodge's rendering debt, paid in one long day (2026-08-16/17) [DONE]
the owner reported one word, "twitch", and it took FIVE distinct diseases to make the dodge read clean …note
the owner reported one word, "twitch", and it took FIVE distinct diseases to make the dodge read clean, every one found by his eye against an instrument and closed the same day. The record matters more than usual here because four of the five were invisible to every gate. In order of discovery: (1) THE IDLE ARMS WERE PLAYING, NOT POSING — the DodgeArms layer's whole job is a held guard, and its motion was the LOOPING idle at weight 1.000 (~200 deg/s of arm motion mid-flight); frozen at speed 0. Real, but not his twitch. (2) THE AUTHORED TURN — all four takes carry a ~180° body turn in their POSE channels, filmed headless with every basing flag cleared: no import flag can remove it, bakeRot renders it, keepOrig renders the stage facing instead (measured: the child 180° off the root while a world-held hand sat pinned), and the C13 cut era had merely HIDDEN it by re-basing thrice per hop — which is also exactly what whipped the seams. Four failed fixes corrected the ARMS against it before a measurement named the carrier (spine twist → wrenched; shoulder positions → warped skin; live-root anchor → slow twist; world snapshot → arms behind a turning body). The fix rotates the one thing that cannot fight itself: the whole visual child, counter-yawed to the root per frame by StanceYaw's own shoulder-line expression. Owner: "THAT DID IT, the twisting is gone." (3) THE REPLAY — his words found what three smoothing rounds could not: "it looks like the dodge animation freezes and then plays again mid flight." The three dodge states scrub ONE take via per-state cycleOffset (0/0.15/0.55), and Unity honors cycleOffset only on LOOPING clips — non-looping, every state restarted the take at frame ZERO: the crouch replayed mid-air and again at landing, every dodge, every direction, since the C14 rewire. The two-day-old state trace had recorded both from-zero entries (nt=0.18, nt=0.22) and they were dismissed as "normal per-state clocks" — a dismissed anomaly is a finding somebody explained away. Fixed with loop: true whose only job is arming the offsets; nothing ever wraps. Owner: "THAT FIXED IT, the studdering is gone." (4) THE GUARD'S CARRIAGE — hands riding the take's chest sat ~11° above the idle's and VARIED (−6..−23 vs −17.1), so a fixed bend could not level them: the waist now closes the measured gap to the idle's pitch EVERY frame, reference cached live from real neutral frames, and the whole hold FADES across the landing crossfade instead of releasing in one frame (his catch: "settles in and then it snaps"). (5) THE CROSSING LEGS — his review-seat comparison: raw F/B legs never cross, the build's did. Two causes, both with prior art: the blend DIRECTION snapped to (0,0) — the four-way tree's dead centre — while the landing state still faded (latched through the tail now), and the retired STEADY trims' thigh residue was back because its cause was back (the discarded turn strands its compensation in the thigh muscles; trims resurrected from their own tombstone, lateral swing halved, hop shape untouched). The de-stutter smoothing survives as a FINISH, not a fix — measured eating 24-30% of the knees' peak bend, removed at his request, and chosen BACK by his eye over raw ("looks worse" without it): taste, with its cost known. Tools this day left behind: armcarrier (the carrier-vs-carried separator), clipjitter (source-clip smoothness vs the locomotion controls — the question no delivery-side probe asks), clipdiff (raw vs surgery output, per channel), and the standalone reviewer confirmed as THE review seat (Shapers.exe -playtestMode AnimReview — an Editor path was added and deleted the same hour after costing two corrections). The pattern under all five, worth the increment's last line: the owner's plain-language reports outperformed every probe in the room, five for five — "freezes and plays again" was a literal spec of the bug — and each fix that FAILED had corrected a part against a whole. Owner: Trevor (every report, every verdict, and the two sentences that named diseases 3 and 5) + Code (probes, films, traces, fixes, reverts, the record) · Seat: standalone player owner-played throughout; the standalone AnimReview for the raw-vs-live comparison; headless film + curve probes for every measurement · Done: owner's verdicts in sequence — "the twisting is gone", "the studdering is gone", "Looks good" (hands), "The snap is gone", "Its fixed, we can move on. 43 M9-INC30 M9 increment C17 — Flow becomes a catch and redirect (2026-08-17) [PARTIAL]

FLOW-TOGGLE, FLOW-WALK, FLOW-CATCH-ONE, FLOW-REDIRECT, FLOW-FIZZLE land; FLOW-HEAT, FLOW-HARVEST, FLOW-RELEASE and FLOW-ROOTED retire; FLOW-WINDOW becomes the hold clock. Flow now catches the opponent's fireball, holds it at the fist, and throws the same fireball back. First of three planned increments — the embers and the siphon follow, C18 and C19 below. MOVE-FWD Forward speed ~1.4 m/s [BUILT]
NCC maxSpeed 1.4 on Player.prefab. ⚠ 1.85 → 1.4 ON 2026-08-14, AND IT IS NOT THE SAME REASON AS 2.0 → …note
NCC maxSpeed 1.4 on Player.prefab. ⚠ 1.85 → 1.4 ON 2026-08-14, AND IT IS NOT THE SAME REASON AS 2.0 → 1.85. _backpedalSpeed moved with it, so one walk speed in every direction still holds. WHAT CHANGED UNDER THE WALK: the forward and back CHILDREN left the locomotion blend tree the same evening, so pure forward and pure back are now interpolated from the DIAGONAL takes. Those takes stride about three times shorter than the forward take — 0.51 m per cycle against 1.61 — and they step at 45 degrees, so only the forward component of each step carries you. Measured through the live blend by gaitbob, the planted foot carries 0.52 m/s. Against a 1.85 m/s body that was 72% ground slide; at 1.4 it is 63%. The walk came down to close some of that gap. ⚠ The slide is REDUCED, not fixed, and 63% is high — record it plainly. The owner chose the diagonal blend because it reads far better in motion: it plays at its authored tempo, where the forward take was being played at DOUBLE its own rate, which is what he reported as "very robotic". He has now twice preferred the performance over the foot lock. That is a deliberate art call, not an oversight. ⚠ The slide arithmetic below is SUPERSEDED — it was computed off the forward take, and that clip no longer plays. Read it as the record of that decision, never as the current geometry. ⚠ THE REAL FIX IS AN IMPORT, NOT A TUNING, and the spec already carries that note on this row: a forward take with a LONG stride authored near walking pace. Nothing on hand is longer, so the owed task is unchanged and its numbers are not restated here. ⚠ ONE MORE FIELD MOVED IN THE SAME EDIT, and it is the interesting one. PlayerAnimatorBridge._authoredGaitSpeed is the divisor in the cadence law, and its old 0.82 was measured on the forward take — a clip no longer in the tree. At that stale divisor the NEED term started winning as soon as the walk reached 1.4, which stretched the gait cycle from 0.98 s to 1.15 s and made the legs visibly slower. That would have undone the tempo just approved, silently. The new divisor is the measured forward carry per unit of cadence for the tree that actually plays. It lives on the prefab, it is bound to nothing here, and this row refers to it rather than re-homing it. ⚠ A stale divisor does not error. It quietly changes which term binds. ⚠ 2.0 → 1.85 ON 2026-08-14 (owner call: "Lets slow down the walking all together… Lets go with 1.85"). _backpedalSpeed moved with it, so one walk speed in every direction still holds. WHY 1.85, AND WHY IT IS NOT ARBITRARY: the feet carry 0.82 × cadence = 1.64 m/s at the live cadence cap, so ground slide is 1 - 1.64/speed. That gives 18% slide at 2.0, ~11% at 1.85, and exactly ZERO at 1.64. The owner was shown those three points and picked the middle one — he bought back speed and kept some skate, knowingly. Record the slide as REDUCED, never as removed. ⚠ 1.64 is also within a whisker of the 1.65 this row narrates below as tried and REVERTED for playing "ROUGH" on 2026-07-19 — and it is NOT the same experiment. That one cut a 4–5 m/s walk by about 60%; this one cuts 2.0 by 18%. The reversion is written out below; connect to it there rather than reading it as a verdict on this step. WHAT PROMPTED IT: the owner reported the walk animation as "less smooth" and asked whether slowing the walk would help. This is the answer to that question, and the answer is partial. ⚠ Slowing the body reduces SLIDE and nothing else. The hip bob, the planted-foot drift and the left/right leg asymmetry are baked into the clip. All three are indifferent to how fast the body travels. Nobody may later read this change as the fix for the bob. 1.64 → 2.0 on 2026-08-02, the SECOND step of the retest: the owner played 1.64 and judged the slow walk GOOD but 'a bit slow', and asked to speed movement and animation up together now that the stop is softened. _gaitBaseCadence moved 2.0 → 2.44 with it, which is what keeps foot slide at ZERO — the identity is bodySpeed = 0.82 × cadence, so the two numbers must move together or the skate comes straight back. Gait cycle 0.99 s → 0.81 s, i.e. a brisk walk rather than a stroll, still short of the 0.67 s jog tempo that read as a frantic scramble. ⚠⚠ 2026-08-02 — THE SLOW-WALK RETEST (owner-approved experiment, NOT a settled tuning): 2.41 -> 1.64, backpedal 1.69 -> 1.15, sprint 3.01 -> 2.05, the 0.7x / 1.25x ratios preserved exactly as every previous rescale preserved them. WHY 1.64 SPECIFICALLY: it is the speed at which foot slide is ZERO. The wired forward walk is authored at 0.82 m/s (GaitProbe.Check) and the cadence cap is 2.0, so the feet carry 0.82 x 2.0 = 1.64 m/s; at the old 2.41 the feet accounted for 68% of travel and the remaining 32% was skate. THE CASE FOR RE-OPENING A TWICE-BURNED NUMBER: 1.65 was rejected on 2026-07-19 for playing 'ROUGH', and the recorded diagnosis was NOT the speed — it was that 'with rooted wind-ups on top, movement went binary between slow and frozen', i.e. the RATIO of moving to stuck. The roots were then rebuilt on 2026-08-01 (the momentum glide replaced the flat 0.3 factors and the whole-phase hard roots), so the condition that sank the experiment no longer exists and the experiment has never been re-run against it. If it plays well, the walk is slide-free at natural cadence and the whole glide problem closes without touching the art; if it plays slow, revert these three numbers together and the finding is that the speed itself was the objection after all. Previously, 2.41 since 2026-08-01 (the glide reconciliation returned it to the judged midpoint — see the M9 note) (2.77 from 2026-07-28 to then, +15% on an owner feel call) (2.41 from 2026-07-27) (4 from 2026-07-19) — lowered from 5 to give MOVE-SPRINT a gap worth feeling (see that row). A realistic 1.65 m/s walking pace was tried and REVERTED the same day: with rooted wind-ups on top, movement went binary between slow and frozen and the fight stopped flowing. The lesson is kept rather than the attempt erased — the problem was the RATIO of time spent moving to time spent stuck, which is why the follow-up went after the roots instead. ⚠ THE SPACING NUMBERS STILL OWE A RE-DERIVATION. The dodge (3.5 m of lateral travel in 0.4 s ≈ 8.75 m/s) is ~2.2× walking speed at 4 m/s, against the 1.75× every §4 claim was written under at 5 m/s — so it is more dominant as evasion than those claims assume, and the walk-out-of-a-fireball break-even moves outward from ~7.7 m under the same pressure. The figures this row used to carry (~14 m break-even, "more than 5× walking speed") were computed against the REVERTED 1.65 pace and are VOID — they were never the numbers at 4 m/s. Nothing has been re-derived against 4; TRI-FB-WALKOUT is tracked as an open spec gap and is where that re-derivation lands. 4 -> 0.82 on 2026-07-27 (owner call), matching the authored gait of the Kung-fu clips as measured by GaitProbe off the planted foot. Backpedal and sprint moved by the SAME ratios (0.7x, 1.25x). This is HALF the 1.65 m/s recorded two sentences up as tried and REVERTED for playing "ROUGH" — that row was put in front of the owner before the change and the change was chosen anyway, so it is a decision made against known evidence, not in ignorance of it. If the fight stops flowing, this is the first number to put back, and the spacing re-derivation this row already owes gets worse, not better: the dodge's 3.5 m in 0.4 s is now ~3.6x walking speed, against the 1.75x every §4 claim was written under. TRI-FB-WALKOUT and every §4 claim are further from honest than before.
MOVE-BACK Backpedal speed ~1.4 m/s — one walk speed in every direction [BUILT]
⚠ 1.85 → 1.4 ON 2026-08-14, IN THE SAME EDIT AS MOVE-FWD — see that row for the blend-tree change …note
⚠ 1.85 → 1.4 ON 2026-08-14, IN THE SAME EDIT AS MOVE-FWD — see that row for the blend-tree change behind it, and for what the step does and does not fix. One walk speed in every direction still holds. ⚠ 2.0 → 1.85 ON 2026-08-14, IN THE SAME EDIT AS MOVE-FWD — see that row for why 1.85 is the number, and for what the change does and does not fix. ⚠ THE TWO FIELDS MUST MOVE TOGETHER, AND NOTHING IN THE GATE COMPARES THEM. maxSpeed and _backpedalSpeed are bound separately — each to its own prefab field and its own spec Target — so a change to one alone passes every check the project has. Tune them in one edit, every time. A future pass that touches only the forward walk puts back a retreat penalty nobody chose: the exact differential this row retired earlier the same day, returning by accident instead of by decision. ⚠ THE SPEED DIFFERENTIAL IS RETIRED 2026-08-14 (owner: "I want the walk forward speed to match all the other walk direction speed"). Forward, strafe and backpedal are now ONE walk speed. Sprint stays forward-only. RECORD THIS AS A RETIREMENT, NOT AS A REQUIREMENT THAT WAS MET. The requirement's whole design reason was that a slower retreat made ADVANCING a small commitment — you could close the gap, but you could not freely un-close it. That pressure is gone, and closing distance is free in both directions now. The project's rule is that an unmet requirement is never edited down to look achieved; the honest entry here is the mirror of it — the owner removed the differential deliberately, and what it cost is stated rather than tidied away. The field is NOT collapsed into MOVE-FWD's read, even though the two now hold the same number: the gate binds this field to the prefab, and a direction that costs nothing today is a direction someone will want to price again tomorrow. ⚠ TWO THINGS LEANED ON THIS ROW AND NOW OWE A RE-JUDGEMENT IN PLAY. (1) MOVE-SPRINT is forward-only for two stated reasons, and one of them was "a sprint that worked backward would undo MOVE-BACK" — that reason names nothing any more, so the rule stands on its other leg alone (an omnidirectional sprint is a dodge with no cooldown, which is true on its own terms). Flagged rather than quietly re-justified. (2) Every interaction-triangle claim written on the assumption that a retreating opponent is the slower one is now written against spacing the game does not have; TRI-ADVANCE carries that note on its own row. Nobody has played the symmetric walk. Previously 1.4 from the 2026-08-02 walk retest — see MOVE-FWD for that experiment and how to revert it; the 0.7 ratio was preserved through every rescale until today, when it was retired outright. CLOSED 2026-07-19 — the project's oldest open spec gap, unbuilt since 2026-07-10. PlayerMovement._backpedalSpeed 1.4 on the prefab, the same number the advance carries (1.85-vs-1.85 and 2.0-vs-2.0 earlier the same day; 1.4-vs-2.0 until 2026-08-14; 1.69-vs-2.41 until the 2026-08-02 slow-walk retest; 1.94-vs-2.77 from 2026-07-28 to 2026-08-01) (2.8-vs-4 until 2026-07-27). Rescaled twice that day: built at 3.5-vs-5, briefly 1.155-vs-1.65 during the walking-pace experiment, then 2.8-vs-4 when that reverted. Each time the 0.7 RATIO was preserved rather than the absolute re-guessed — the ratio is the durable part, not any absolute — so the retreat penalty the spec argues for never moved. The ADVANCE speed is deliberately NOT duplicated — _forwardSpeed is captured from the NCC's authored maxSpeed at Awake, so MOVE-FWD keeps exactly one home and the two cannot drift. The implementation detail that matters: NetworkCharacterController.Move NORMALISES the vector it is given, so scaling direction does nothing — it looks like a fix and changes no behaviour. The speed CAP is the only lever, so maxSpeed is written per tick. Lerped by how much of the input points backward, so diagonals blend rather than snapping at 90° and pure strafe stays at full speed; deterministic from input alone, so the client predicts what the server applies with no networked state. Reached via the M8 feel pass — the question was "should we lower walking speed to slow the fight", and the answer was that the spec already said what to do and it had never been done. UNVERIFIED BY PLAY.
MOVE-SPRINT ~~Forward sprint 2.5 m/s, forward only~~ [DEAD]
RETIRED 2026-08-14 — owner: "Remove the sprint completely, our fire glide replaces it effectively." The …note
RETIRED 2026-08-14 — owner: "Remove the sprint completely, our fire glide replaces it effectively." The design argument is a comparison: the fire glide is 4.0 m/s and works in EVERY direction, against a sprint that was 2.5 and forward-only. A gear slower and more restricted than the ability beside it has nothing left to be the answer to. WHAT LANDED IN CODE. Both WRITERS are removed — the human's Left Shift press in BasicSpawner, and the bot's closing press in AIController. The single READER is removed too — the sprint branch of PlayerMovement's speed chain — so a forward press now falls through to the plain walk, and that is the whole behaviour change in that file. EInputButtons.Sprint = 4 is RETAINED and unbound, because those indices are wire format: a retired one is never deleted and never reused, exactly as RingStance = 5 is held. The reader sweep ran BEFORE the unbind, since doing only the writers is how the ring stance died silently for three days. ⚠ The ring-stance scar does not transfer, and saying so stops it being over-applied: that index fed a HELD state, so a false-forever "not pressed" read as "let go" and collapsed the channel. Sprint's reader was one branch of a speed chain, so the same answer just falls through to the walk. There was no state to collapse. _sprintSpeed stays on Player.prefab at 2.5, dormant, so a revival starts from the number that was actually played. roadmap.py's prefab binding for this row is REMOVED (the DRIFT-CHARGE precedent), so the spec's Target cell no longer has to match the prefab — a binding on a dead requirement reports GREEN forever. ⚠ THE TRADE IS THE PART TO WATCH: the glide is FUELLED and the sprint was FREE. What actually went away is UNLIMITED forward travel, not a top speed. The glide buys more speed in more directions for 3.0 s and then makes you wait (DRIFT-FUEL); Shift could be held all round. This is a spacing change, not a speed change. ⚠ A CONSEQUENCE NOBODY ASKED FOR — THE JUMP LOSES ABOUT 26% OF ITS REACH. The jump has no speed of its own: PlayerCombat stashes your ground velocity at the press as the arc's horizontal, and its own comment says this "preserves the sprint-jump exactly". So maximum jump-in distance falls from 2.5 x airtime to 1.85 x airtime. No jump number was edited and no gate can see this, because nothing in the jump was touched — the reach moved because the thing it was made of moved. Every spacing claim sized against the longer arc owes a re-read. Note that a jump pressed during the glide carries 4.0, which is now the only way to buy a long jump-in. ⚠ THE BOT IS MATERIALLY WEAKER AND NOTHING MEASURES IT. It used to press sprint to close beyond ~15 m (its preferred range, plus the range deadband, plus _sprintGap). Its top closing speed is now the 1.85 walk, against a player who can leave at 4.0 on the glide. It cannot hold a spacing band against that — worse than the July state the sprint press was added to fix, because back then the player's ceiling was a forward-only gear. _sprintGap becomes dead tuning and is retained rather than deleted, like every other retired knob there. This is a play question, not a code one, and it is the first thing to watch in a bot fight. ⚠ _maxGaitScale DID NOT DIE WITH THE SPRINT — it is 1.25 on the prefab and it stays. The field was labelled "the sprint's gear ratio" and its live user is now the fire glide, which saturates it. Anyone reading it as leftover sprint tuning and collapsing it to 1.0 would drop the glide's cadence and push its foot slide up, with the glide as the only symptom — so the cause would look like the glide and would not be. Left Shift is free again. It was taken from the dodge on 2026-07-19 to give the sprint a key, and the dodge moved to Left Ctrl to make room. Nothing claims Shift today. The forward-only rule dies with the ability, which closes the re-argument MOVE-BACK says this row owes — there is no rule left to re-argue. Historical narration below, kept as the record of what was built and why. 2.5 since the 2026-08-02 walk retest — see MOVE-FWD; the 1.25 gear ratio is preserved. NEW 2026-07-19. PlayerMovement._sprintSpeed 2.5 on the prefab (3.01 until the 2026-08-02 slow-walk retest; 3.46 from 2026-07-28 to 2026-08-01, the +15% family retune, walked back with the glide reconciliation; the 0.7x/1.25x ratios preserved through every move). ⚠ IT LOOKED IDENTICAL TO WALKING UNTIL 2026-07-28 — GaitScale was planar / _authoredGaitSpeed clamped to _maxGaitScale 1, and BOTH walk and sprint saturated that clamp, so the sprint played the walk clip at walking cadence. Referenced to walk speed now, with the cap at the sprint/walk ratio 1.25, so sprint is visibly the same walk played 25% faster — which is what it was always specified to be (5 until 2026-07-27), on Shift + forward; dodge rebound to Left Ctrl to free the key. Retuned 5.5 → 5 later the same day alongside the walk's 5 → 4: the number that matters is the GAP, not either endpoint — 5.5-over-5 was a 10% gain that read as noise, 5-over-4 is 25% and a real gear change. EInputButtons.Sprint = 4 was APPENDED, never inserted — those indices are wire format, and renumbering Dodge or Jump to tidy the order would silently remap inputs across peers. Gated on forward INPUT (Move.y > 0.1) rather than on the key, and lerped by how forward the stick is, so it cannot snap on mid-strafe. The bot sprints too — it shipped human-only, which structurally capped the AI at walking speed while a human held Shift: a missing input, not a difficulty setting. AIController now sets the button when the gap exceeds its range deadband by _sprintGap, and mirrors PlayerMovement's own forward rule rather than inventing a second one (pressing Sprint on any other vector is a no-op anyway). VERIFIED BY PLAY 2026-07-19, human-vs-human: passes — forward-only sprint reads as correct rather than arbitrary, and the dodge's move to Left Ctrl does not misfire under pressure. That pass was HUMAN-vs-HUMAN, so it says nothing about how the bot's sprint reads in a bot fight; that is unverified.

Defender budget = startup + flight − interp delay; need = reaction (~0.19) + shield raise. At 9 m from a CLIENT seat: today 0.633 vs 0.517 (+0.116 ✓); 0.25 startup against the OLD 0.327 raise gives 0.433 vs 0.517 (−0.084 ✗ — reactive blocking arithmetically impossible, reactive break-even range 5.3 m → 11.7 m); 0.25 with raise 0.15 restores +0.093 ✓. The defender is better off after this change, not worse.

FB-DAMAGE 12.5 per clean hit [BUILT]

→ 12.5 on 2026-07-29 (owner call): the primary read too strong in the feel pass, so it moved ALONE to the bottom of the Power band (12.5–15) — deliberately NOT another across-the-board pass, so the cannon (22.68), burn (1.62/s) and every absolute-priced grid ability are untouched; only the follow-up links follow it (0.7× → 8.75/link), because they are multipliers by design. 60/12.5 = 4.8, so SHIELD-HP's blocked-hits-to-break stays 5 — the second pass in a row that did not move it. NEW ID 2026-07-19 — the fireball's damage had no requirement, no ledger row and no gate binding until now, so nothing owned it. That absence is exactly how PlayerCombat came to compute cannon damage as _cannonMaxDamage / 20f with the comment "20 = the fireball's base damage": a second, silent home for a live value that would have quietly rescaled the cannon the moment damage moved. It has since moved (20→16→14.4→12.96), and the divisor now reads _weapon.FireballBaseDamage off the prefab. 12.96 in Fireball.prefab — 20 was cut 20% to 16 in the across-the-board damage pass (shipped with FB-BURN), then to 14.4, then to 12.96 (another across-the-board 10% on 2026-07-20), giving ~8 hits to kill against 100 HP.
