HEAD-HITBOX 0.32 m sphere on the head BONE, 1.05x [UNVERIF]
NEW 2026-07-20. HeadHitbox child on Player.prefab (Bot.prefab is a VARIANT, so it inherits it) carrying …note
NEW 2026-07-20. HeadHitbox child on Player.prefab (Bot.prefab is a VARIANT, so it inherits it) carrying three components that must agree about one sphere: a Fusion Hitbox (Sphere, r 0.16) for the LIVE lag-compensated path, a SphereCollider for the PhysX fallback and for PlayerCameraRig.AimPoint, and the HeadHitbox marker holding _damageMultiplier 1.05. Baked by Assets/Editor/HeadHitboxBaker.cs, then linked by the existing HitboxBaker. The bug it closes: the drawn head sat in a 0.10 m gap ABOVE the body capsule, so head shots hit nothing at all. HitboxRoot.BroadRadius 1.2 -> 1.8 — the broad phase must contain the new hitbox (head top was y 1.60, now 0.856) or the narrow phase is never consulted, which would look exactly like the bug being fixed. Fireball reads the multiplier off the STRUCK hurtbox (LocationMultiplier), never from impact height — a height test would disagree with lag compensation, which resolves against the body's REWOUND position. Applies to HP damage only, never a blocked shot. HeadVisual now PARENTS its cosmetic sphere to this hurtbox and sizes itself from it, so what you see and what you are hit by are one sphere by construction. THAT GOTCHA FIRED, 2026-07-24 — recorded because it is the whole value of having written it down. The M9 hero swap arrived and the geometry was re-derived from the rig (HEAD-HITBOX in the spec has the measurements and the radius decision); the sphere moved from y 1.10-1.60 to 0.536-0.856, which is INSIDE the capsule. The old separation is gone, the layer matrix is all-f so Player collides with Player, and a CharacterController only auto-ignores colliders on its OWN GameObject — this is a child. The guard is now explicit: HeadHitbox.Awake calls Physics.IgnoreCollision between the sphere and the controller. Do not delete it as redundant — nothing else prevents the head from shoving its own body. THEN MOVED ONTO THE BONE the same day — the static fix above was measured off the T-pose and the hero does not fight in a T-pose (stance head y 0.39-0.46, jab lunges 0.76 m forward; one static sphere covering every pose needs r>=0.573, a torso-swallowing 1.15 m ball). HeadHitboxBaker now parents the hurtbox to mixamorig:Head and converts the readable bind-pose anchor (0, 0.696, -0.025 in root space) into the bone frame at bake time. Verified rigid: constant 0.0553 m offset from the bone in every clip at every sampled frame. See spec HEAD-HITBOX for the animation->sim carve-out this deliberately takes. Two traps closed, both of which would have baked and linked and reported as correct: (1) the FBX's x100 cm->m node scale means every bone has lossyScale 100, so a bone-parented r 0.16 collider becomes a 16 m sphere — the baker cancels the scale and now LOGS the resulting world radius rather than the constant; (2) CullUpdateTransforms stops writing bone transforms off-camera, which would freeze the hurtbox on the host, the peer that resolves everyone's hits while its camera watches only its own player — head shots would land or miss depending on where the HOST was looking, intermittent and invisible in any log. HeroIntegration now sets AlwaysAnimate. Also removed here: the HeadVisual placeholder sphere, which was still spawning a ball in the hero's skull — HeroIntegration had set hv.enabled = false with a comment predicting that exact symptom, but Unity calls Awake on a DISABLED component (it only skips Start/OnEnable/Update) and the sphere is built in Awake. The component is now REMOVED, and HeadVisual.Awake early-outs on !enabled so the word means what readers assume. unverified, not built: nobody has yet fired a shot at the new head. It owes a measurement — land a head shot at 5-12 m, confirm it registers AND reads 1.05x, and confirm movement is jitter-free with the sphere inside the capsule.

24 in Fireball.prefab (27→30, then →24, both 2026-07-19). The hit-reg block is CLEARED; the bump shipped and was then reversed PAST its starting point. It had been blocked because raising speed on the raw Physics.Raycast widens the phantom-miss band; M8 Inc 3's SubtickAccuracy lag comp removed that coupling, and the claim was verified rather than assumed — a BotDuel Gate 0 run at 30 m/s reproduced the 0% phantom rate measured at 27 (see Docs/m8/inc4/). CHAIN-STEP One short lateral step per chain link [BUILT]

NEW 2026-07-18. _chainStepDuration 0.30 s (0.18→0.30, more lateral reach), deliberately shorter than the follow-up wind-up so the step lands before the shot leaves (plant-and-fire, not slide-while-shooting). ONE MOVER: PlayerCombat publishes [Networked] ChainStepDir and PlayerMovement drives it through the SAME _kcc.Move() as ordinary movement. The first cut drove the raw CharacterController from PlayerCombat while the NetworkCharacterController still owned position — two movers on one body, so the KCC re-asserted each tick and ate part of the step. That mushiness was half of the reported 'inconsistent'. Third revision 2026-08-01 (owner): the volley's step is now a FORCED 1.0 m forward carry (_volleyStep, ChainSpec.StepIntoApex) spanning wind-up + swing so it completes exactly as the apex root plants — the input-directional step (itself the 2026-07-28 replacement for lateral-only, a turn the spec missed until now) has no live user left. FB-FOLLOWUP 2 follow-ups (the 3-beat volley), ~6.0 s decaying window, Power-M1 only since 2026-07-29 [BUILT]
GATED TO THE ARMED POWER STANCE 2026-07-29 (owner call): BeginFireball(input, volley:) opens the chain …note
GATED TO THE ARMED POWER STANCE 2026-07-29 (owner call): BeginFireball(input, volley:) opens the chain only from BeginPowerSlot — a neutral jab leaves Chain == None, so TickChain never offers a follow-up. Follow-ups went 1 → 2 with the S2 chain generalisation. NEW 2026-07-18; window 1.0→2.0 and made CONCURRENT with recovery. _fireballFollowUpWindow 6.0 (2.0 → 6.0 in the 2026-08-01 tripling of every chain window) / _fireballFollowUpShots 2 (this said 1 until 2026-08-14, contradicting both the prefab and this row's own Requirement cell two columns to the left — the 1 → 2 change is narrated one sentence earlier in this very cell and the figure was never moved with it). Once the auto burst is spent, ActiveDuration returns the window instead of 0, so Active is the window; a Fire press during it fires one cone-clamped shot (reusing ClampToCone + _fireballVolleyTrackCone) and commits to recovery, and the timer expiring recovers instead. Press checked before AdvancePhase so a press on the window's last tick still counts. Chain: up to 2 follow-ups, 70% damage, SHRINKING windows (6 → 3 → 1.5 s since the 2026-08-01 tripling), and a PER-LINK COOLDOWN PUSH (_fireballChainCooldownPerShot, which the prefab owns). ⚠ THIS ROW SAID +0.7 s UNTIL 2026-08-14, AGAINST A PUSH THAT NEARLY DOUBLED ON 2026-08-02. The number moved with the cost-economy pass and the one sentence in this cell that states the chain's cost curve did not move with it — the same failure the spec's own §1.5 line carried until the same day. Corrected to name the FIELD rather than a copy of its value, because a second copy is what went stale. ⚠ AND THE PUSH DID NOT ACTUALLY ACCUMULATE — FIXED 2026-08-14, BROKEN SINCE IT SHIPPED. The per-beat write took the phase timer's remaining time as its base, but that write runs from OnActivate, which the phase loop calls on the tick the phase timer EXPIRES and BEFORE it re-arms. The term was therefore always about zero: every beat wrote the SAME value, and beat three cost exactly what beat two did. The chain's price did not grow with its length at all — against this row's own stated intent, against the field note that claimed base plus two pushes, and against TOPO-SHAPE's roster-wide rule that a shape's cost escalates per release. A dead term reads exactly like a live one. Each beat now extends the RUNNING cooldown by one push, so a three-beat volley reaches the number the field note has claimed all along. No tuning value changed — the arithmetic was wrong, not the numbers. ⚠ The recovery term is DELIBERATELY absent from that sum, not lost: the cost model already subtracts the ability's own duration and recovery is part of that duration, so adding it back per beat re-charges what the model deliberately removed — keeping it landed the cycle 14% OVER the model instead of 7% under. ⚠ THE OLD DERIVATION ALSO CITED 37.5 DAMAGE FOR A 3-BEAT VOLLEY, AND THAT WAS WRONG. The true figure is 30 — follow-ups take the 0.7 damage ratio this row already states, so it is one full beat and two at 70%, not three full ones. Against the true 30 the Power row's 6.5 rate wants a slightly longer cycle than the shipped one, and the shipped one sits a few per cent under it. The conclusion survives the correction, which is exactly why nobody tripped over it: a wrong input that lands near the right answer leaves nothing to notice. ⚠ NEW AND UNMEASURED — a SLOW chain is now cheaper than a fast one, because a cooldown that has already expired contributes nothing to the next beat's sum. The window decays and waiting is tempo handed to the opponent, so this is judged an acceptable trade rather than a hole; it is judged, not tested. The emberhook shares the property. The retreat chain does NOT — its push is an absolute base-plus-push-per-beat, which is robust to slow play. Two chains, two shapes, both landing on their target; unifying them would move a shipped number. The cooldown term is what fixed 'spammy', and it was measurable: the chain tripled damage per cooldown (20 → 48) while the cooldown stood still, so sustained DPS went 16.7 → 40 and TTK collapsed 6.0 s → 2.5 s. Logged evidence: 14 of ~37 inter-shot gaps were 10–27 ticks, i.e. chain links — chaining was always correct. At the then-live +0.7 the DPS curve was roughly FLAT across lengths (16.7 / 17.9 / 18.5), making the chain a TEMPO decision rather than a damage multiplier; TTK returned to ~5.4 s, near the 6.0 s that felt right. ⚠ THAT CURVE IS A MEASUREMENT TAKEN ON 2026-07-18 AT 0.7, AND IT IS LABELLED RATHER THAN UPDATED. It is evidence from a run, not a claim about today's push. Rewriting the numbers inside it would silently restate a measurement nobody took — and the run it came from also predates the accumulation fix above, so the curve it drew was the flat-price chain rather than the escalating one. It owes a re-measure at the live push. Weaker on purpose: a full-price shot for a half-price commitment made chaining always correct, so declining had no reason to exist — the chain is pressure, not damage. Follow-up wind-up 0.42 s (0.13→0.26→0.35→0.42; the last on the M8 feel pass 2026-07-19, with FB-STARTUP). ⚠ THAT FIGURE PREDATES THE 15% STRIKE-RATE RESCALE and is a dated snapshot, not the live value — _fireballFollowUpStartup came down with every other strike window in that pass, and the prefab owns what it holds today. Flagged rather than restated here: this cell is already carrying one number that went stale by being copied, and the fix for that is fewer copies, not fresher ones. Nothing binds this field, so no gate will catch it drifting again. The gap between links has now been the answer to "the fireball feels spammy/gimmicky" three separate times — which is itself worth reading as a finding about where the chain's feel actually lives. Chain state is now VISIBLE: PlayerHUD draws remaining-shot pips plus a draining window bar under the crosshair, and a cooldown tick when the fireball is unavailable — the chain was invisible state with a hard edge, which a new player reads as dropped input rather than a rule. Reads: ChainShotsLeft / ChainWindowFraction / FireballOnCooldown.. !FollowUpPending in the gate is LOAD-BEARING: FireballShotsFired only increments at LAUNCH, so throughout the follow-up's own wind-up every other clause still passed — a second press restarted PhaseTimer and the step, so mashing pushed the shot further away instead of firing it. The chain answered some presses and silently swallowed others; that was the other half of 'inconsistent'. (_fireballFollowUpStartup): it used to fire instantly on press — the only attack in the game with zero telegraph. It enters a real Startup phase, tracks live aim through it like shot 1, and FollowUpPending keeps OnActivate from mistaking it for a fresh shot 1 (and is cleared on every abandon path so it cannot leak into a later fireball). (Superseded note: declining once cost ~1.9 s rooted vs ~0.9 s firing — the jab is now strictly worse than pre-change, and it inverts TRI-DODGE-NOPUNISH (see that row). RING-GUARD Channelled ring blocks, omnidirectionally, while it stands [UNVERIF]
NEW 2026-08-01. IsRingGuarding = Action == RingKick && Phase == Active — derived, not a second …note
NEW 2026-08-01. IsRingGuarding = Action == RingKick && Phase == Active — derived, not a second [Networked] flag, because Action/CurrentPhase are already replicated and already authoritative on "is the stance up"; a parallel bool could disagree with them on exactly the transition ticks that are hardest to debug (see Alive()'s note, where a phase test written for the steady state was wrong on the tick that created it). Netcode is the load-bearing part and it cost nothing new: the stance is recorded in the EXISTING guard-history ring beside _guardUp and _flowOn, so WasBlockingAt resolves it at the same rewound tick the body is — the pattern flow established, applied again, and the second time that ring has absorbed a new defensive state for free. No cone (flow's precedent, not the shield's). ⚠ IT SHIPPED ON THE SHIELD POOL AND WAS REVERSED WITHIN THE HOUR in favour of RING-HOLD; the reversal is the instructive part. Sweeps and cones are blocked FREE — PlayerFireSweep/PlayerFireBlast skip a guarded victim without calling DamageShield, which stays honest for the shield only because its CONE makes you face the threat. A ring has no facing, so pool-backed it charged for fireballs and cannons and gave away the streams, flamethrower, both 540s, retreat kick, drift lunge and flow release — an HP bar most of the kit could not touch. A clock prices every block identically whatever threw it. The shield's pool, regen, break and lockout are untouched by the ring again; IsGuarding was deleted with the model it served. Owes, and it is all play: whether an omnidirectional wall is too strong at 2.5 s, and whether it needs a height rule so a shot that visually clears the fire passes (that would make one number govern what the ring burns AND what it stops). ⚠ THE RECOVERY IS NOW THE BURN-OUT (2026-08-01, owner: "I want the recovery fixed actually"). The stance ring was the ONLY fire in the ability with no burn-out — hardcoded to full height for the whole action and then gone with the mode — while every barrage wave around it had a three-act rise/burn/gutter. That made "the ring walls despawn in a flash" TWO bugs sharing one description (the other being the barrage's truncated last wave), and fixing either alone would have left the report half-true — the trap in a symptom that names a whole FAMILY of objects. The ring now sinks to the floor and fades across the recovery, and goes COLD at the same instant it starts sinking, so it keeps the contract the waves already keep: full height means it burns, shrinking means it does not. An earlier edit made the ring vanish at the hold's expiry instead; that was reverted on the owner's call, because it fixed the honesty problem by deleting the thing rather than by making it behave. ⚠ The burn-down gets the recovery's 0.45 s against the waves' 1.12 s, so it is the FASTER burn-out of the two — lengthening _ringRecovery is the lever if it still reads quick, at the cost of a longer window in which you are standing up and vulnerable.

RING-CONCURRENT 2.0 — one burning, one guttering [UNVERIF]
_ringConcurrentRings = how long a ring lives, in intervals. At 1.0 a ring dies exactly as the next …note
_ringConcurrentRings = how long a ring lives, in intervals. At 1.0 a ring dies exactly as the next erupts (a blink); above it the old one spends the remainder guttering while the new rises beyond it. It briefly meant the opposite (3.5, "how many nested rings do I want") under the travelling model — that model is dead, and this now controls OVERLAP, not stacking. Past ~2 the march washes out into the overlapping-bands look the relay replaced. 1.4 → 2.0 on 2026-08-01 (owner: "I need them to 'burn out' down to the ground slowly"). The gutter is interval × (this − 1), so this IS the burn-out length — 0.4 of an interval was a flicker, 2.0 gives a full interval (1.12 s) of sinking and fading, and it lands the timing so one ring starts sinking exactly as the next starts rising (two rings on screen continuously, the picture the owner said he loved; at 1.4 there were moments with only one). ⚠ It spends the documented ~2 ceiling exactly — at 2.0 the second ring is always GUTTERING rather than lit, so the washed-out-bands warning holds, but there is no headroom: a slower burn-out from here needs a longer interval, not more overlap. Guttering rings are COLD, so this adds no damage.
RING-BARRAGE 1.875 s of rings, cadence derived [UNVERIF]
_ringBarrageSeconds 1.875 (4.5 → 1.5 with the 3x speed-up, → 1.875 when RING-INNER closed and the count …note
_ringBarrageSeconds 1.875 (4.5 → 1.5 with the 3x speed-up, → 1.875 when RING-INNER closed and the count rose 4 → 5; it has moved ONLY to hold the derived front speed). 4.5 → 1.5 on 2026-08-02 — the owner's 3x speed-up, taken knowingly ("its dramatic I know"). Cadence is derived, so ONE number moved everything: interval 1.125 → 0.375 s, and the front creeps at step / interval = 0.8 → 2.4 m/s. ⚠⚠ THIS INVERTS THE ABILITY'S COUNTER-PLAY AND THAT IS THE HEADLINE, NOT THE SPEED. The front now travels at 2.4 m/s against a 1.4 m/s backpedal — so you can no longer WALK out of a barrage, where at 0.8 m/s retreating was comfortable. The answer becomes jump-or-be-clear — and even jumping does not cover it: waves arrive every 0.375 s while a jump buys ~0.396 s above RING-CLEAR's 1.5 m and then costs 0.7 s of airtime plus recovery, so ONE jump clears about ONE wave of four. The real counter is now to not be in the 3.5–6.5 m band at all. ⚠ Two more things moved without being tuned, because every ring timing is a FRACTION of the interval: the cold RISE falls 0.28 → 0.094 s (the wave is barely visible before it burns, which spends the telegraph the rise exists to give), and each ring's BURN window falls 0.84 → 0.28 s. ⚠ And the zone becomes a burst: the barrage used to OUTLIVE its root (4.5 s of waves against a 1.5 s root — "you plant a fire zone and fight around it"); at 1.5 s the two end together, so that property is gone. If the speed is kept but the zone is wanted back, the lever is RING-RANGE or RING-STEP (more rings across the same window), not this number. History: 5.0 → 5.6 on 2026-08-01 halving the wave frequency, then down again on 2026-08-02 to HOLD that frequency when RING-INNER dropped the count 5 → 4 — without the correction the interval would have drifted to 1.40 s. Cadence is derived from geometry, not authored: the rings must cover stance-radius → RING-RANGE in RING-STEP increments within the window, so interval = duration / ringCount — now 4 rings, one every 1.125 s, marching 3.5 → 6.5 m, front creeping at ~0.80 m/s. ⚠ That front speed is less than HALF the 1.69 m/s backpedal, so the old "retreat barely outruns it" knife-edge is gone and walking away is comfortable; the pressure has to come from the shorter denser march and from RING-GUARD, not from outrunning the front. ⚠ Everything downstream is a FRACTION of the interval, so halving the cadence moved three things nobody tuned: the cold RISE doubled (0.139 → 0.28 s, handing the defender back much of what the taller RING-CLEAR took), the BURN window doubled (0.42 → 0.84 s, so each ring threatens twice as long), and ring life went 0.78 → 1.57 s. Each ring has three acts: RISE out of the ground over the first 25% of its interval (cold, drawn short — it cannot burn before it is visible), BURN at full height while it creeps its step (the only window it hurts), GUTTER as it sinks and fades. The guttering ring is COLD and drawn shrinking, so the picture under-promises rather than over-. The waves now OUTLIVE the root (5 s vs 2 s, owner's call) — you plant a fire zone and fight around it. That forced a structural change: the barrage can no longer derive its lifetime from the action's phase and runs on its own clock, so an explicit death check replaces the "fire cannot outlive its cause" guarantee that gave up. No per-wave state exists: wave k is emitted at start + k·interval and its radius is (t − k·interval)·speed, so the whole barrage is a pure function of ONE start tick. Nothing spawned, nothing to leak, and the client's visual computes the same circles the server hits with. Emission stops range/speed (0.7 s) early so the last wave completes as the root releases instead of being truncated mid-flight.

12 → 12.5 on 2026-08-02 — the Power floor, closing the OTHER half of the M2 cell swap's damage debt (owner: "bring the ring stance to the Power floor too"). The ring moved Special → Power on 2026-08-01 and kept its old numbers, the exact mirror of the emberhook keeping Power's going the other way; the hook was corrected first and this followed. A wave is a discrete impact, so it is directly comparable to the retreat 540's 12.5. ⚠ RING-STANCE-DPS is deliberately NOT dragged along — it is an interval DRIP (5 per 0.6 s), not an impact, and pricing a drip at an impact's floor would put a channelled wall at ~21 DPS. The floor rule is about what a HIT costs, not what a burn costs. _ringDamage. "Once per wave" is a server-side HashSet<long> keyed on (waveIndex, NetworkId) — safe as a plain field because only the state authority resolves and the server simulates each tick once, forward (the reasoning Fireball._rewindTick rests on).
RING-CD ~10.0 s from initiation [UNVERIF]
_ringCooldown, its own [Networked] TickTimer. Deliberately NOT shared with the fireball: a shared …note
_ringCooldown, its own [Networked] TickTimer. Deliberately NOT shared with the fireball: a shared cooldown would make the stance a way to spend your jab and the jab a way to deny your stance, a coupling neither design asked for. The barrage separately locks the fireball for its wind-up + rooted window, mirroring the cannon's own lockout — the barrage IS the attack that press bought.
RING-CLEAR Feet ≥ 1.5 m above the foot plane (hip) [UNVERIF]
PlayerRingBlast._clearHeight 1.5 (1.0 → 1.5 on 2026-08-01, owner: "increase all rings in height …note
PlayerRingBlast._clearHeight 1.5 (1.0 → 1.5 on 2026-08-01, owner: "increase all rings in height including the first one by 50%"). ONE NUMBER RAISES EVERY RING — the channelled stance ring and all barrage waves resolve through the same height filter in BodiesWithin, so "including the first one" was already true by construction. Checkable rather than guessed: jump apex is 2.205 m (gravity 36, airtime 0.7); solving 18t² − 12.6t + h = 0 gives 0.396 s of the 0.7 s arc above 1.5 m, down from 0.501 s at 1.0 m; the dodge's 0.4 m apex still cannot clear it at all, so the design asymmetry is untouched. ⚠ ~0.1 m of headroom remains — the field's own warning is that past ~1.6 m the jump stops clearing reliably and the ability has no counter (1.6 m = 0.367 s). Read a play report of "I jumped and still got hit" as this number before suspecting hit registration. Partly offset the same day by the halved cadence doubling the cold rise to 0.28 s. Tested against the hurtbox's own rewound centre (LagCompensatedHit.HitboxColliderPosition) minus the target's own controller half-height, read off their CharacterController rather than assumed. FireRingVisual draws the flame to this same value, so the visual cannot overstate the hitbox.

RING-COVER Stopped by static geometry [UNVERIF]
A present-position Physics.Linecast on the cover mask, which is the fireball's hit mask with the player …note
A present-position Physics.Linecast on the cover mask, which is the fireball's hit mask with the player layer stripped — derived at bake time by RingBlastBaker, never retyped. The bug that mask prevents is silent: with the player layer left in, the line-of-sight test strikes the TARGET'S OWN capsule, concludes cover, and skips them — the ring would query correctly, find everyone, and hit nobody, with no error anywhere. Present-position is exact rather than approximate here: static geometry does not move, so there is nothing to rewind.

GRID-SPEND A failed press leaves you armed [BUILT]

MEASURED 2026-08-02, the whole three-step sequence: cast the 540 (stance spent, armed=None), re-arm Special, press LCtrl into the still-closed 8 s cooldown → action=None AND armed=Special, i.e. nothing started and the loadout survived; then once the cooldown reopened the same press cast it and spent the stance. That middle step is the requirement, and it is the one that would fail silently — a player would see a dead button and a lost stance and read it as an input drop. Five instances: cannon, ring barrage, drift lunge, the flow redirect, and the dispatch ORDER that implements them -- conversion checks run before the Action == None grid dispatch, so M1 means "convert what I am holding" while an action owns the skeleton and "the armed stance's attack" when idle. Flow's payload changed 2026-08-17 (FLOW-REDIRECT): the held state used to convert into the heat-funded arc (FLOW-RELEASE, now retired); it now converts into the redirect throw, and the instance count stays at five.

An ABSENCE, verifiable by inspection rather than measurement — the same reasoning RING-UNBLOCKABLE is filed under. READ-COOL Cooling Cell greys, with a ring unwinding [UNBUILT]
Designed 2026-08-02, unscheduled — and the half of the readout that is NOT buildable against the …note
Designed 2026-08-02, unscheduled — and the half of the readout that is NOT buildable against the current code, which is why the design session ended in a finding rather than a task. Every grid ability exposes a public yes/no readiness check, so the grey-out is free; no ability exposes REMAINING time, so the ring is not. The only two public countdowns in the combat script are the shield lockout and the flow release window, neither of which is an ordinary cooldown. Second gap, found alongside and worth closing in the same pass: ten of the eleven grid abilities have a readiness check and the ring stance has none — its gate is written inline. That is one instance of a shared mechanism missing while every sibling has it, and the ring is already the ability this project has been bitten by twice for being the odd one out.

This row said the three-beat chain was UNPRICED rather than merely unmeasured, because the cost model had no term for repeatability. The model gains exactly one term: a REPEATED metre of displacement counts at HALF a fresh one. Under it the chain prices at 5.69 s against the shipped 5.60 s — agreement inside a tenth of a second. So the shipped number was right all along, and it is a DERIVATION now rather than a recorded departure from the model. The arithmetic: 15.6 damage (RETREAT-DAMAGE) at the Motion row's 3.5 damage-per-second-of-commitment rate = 4.46 s, plus 10.5 m of retreat counted as 3.5 fresh metres and 7.0 repeated ones — 7.0 effective metres at the dodge's 0.57 s/m rate = 3.99 s — less the ~2.76 s the three kicks themselves take. The shipped 5.60 s is this field plus two per-beat pushes (RETREAT-CHAIN), so the base is untouched and the chain's total is what the model now reaches. ⚠ WHY THE TERM IS A DISCOUNT AND NOT A SURCHARGE, because a repeatable escape sounds like something you charge extra for: the straight model asks 7.7 s, and it asks for it because it treats 10.5 m as three separate dodges. The third retreat is not a fresh dodge. The opponent has already read the first two and is already moving; ground you give up again buys less than ground you give up once. ⚠ THE TERM IS FITTED TO THIS ONE ABILITY'S OWNER-SET NUMBER, so it is NOT independent evidence that the model is right. It needs a second repeatable ability to test it, and until one lands the term explains rather than predicts. This is the same circularity the model already admits about its dodge-derived displacement constant — a constant read off one ability and then used to price others proves nothing until a second ability lands on it by accident. ⚠ AND THE PER-LINK-vs-WHOLE QUESTION IS DECIDED — the principle was already in the code, it had just never been written down. This row used to say nobody had chosen how a mixed evade/damage chain should pay. The rule is: a chain whose LENGTH IS FIXED arms its whole cooldown at the opener; a chain whose length is the PLAYER'S CHOICE pushes per link. The emberhook is a fixed three beats, so it arms whole. The volley and this chain are both the player's choice, so they push per beat and you pay for what you spend rather than for what you could have spent. The 3.99 s measurement above was already stale against 2.9 before today, and it still owes a re-measure — but the price it would be measuring is now derived rather than asserted. Field unchanged (_retreatKickCooldown 2.9 s). ⚠ The RANGE half of the old debt is untouched here — three beats throw three fireballs where the cone threw one spray, and the model still prices no range at all. DRIFT-CD carries the same debt and the reasoning that was weighed against it.

NEW 2026-08-14. Owner: "add 3 charges to it same way our chain volley works... just with a backward …note
NEW 2026-08-14. Owner: "add 3 charges to it same way our chain volley works... just with a backward dodge," then "Just match the exact format on the numbers the chain volley uses." All four structural numbers are FB-FOLLOWUP's own — window, decay ladder, floor and per-beat cooldown push — copied verbatim rather than re-derived, on direct instruction. The beat's own startup (0.133 s) and recovery (0.28 s) stay the KICK'S own fields, not the volley's: every chain reads its timing off its own ability, the rule that keeps a feel pass on the jab from silently retuning the kick. ⚠ The floor and the kick's own startup share the same 0.133 s by coincidence, not by rule — flagged so nobody reads a shared constant into it, the same caution RETREAT-CD/PDODGE-CD already carry for their own coincidental tie. Matching the kick's own numbers also made three phase-table edits unnecessary, because its floor already sits under its recovery. Joins the volley's rooting carve-out — without it, IsChaining stays true for the whole 6 s window and roots every non-volley chain across it, which would have planted a Motion-Evasive press for six seconds after a use meant to read as an escape. ChainKind.Retreat = 4, ShotAbility.Retreat = 7, both appended (wire format, append-only; no retired index moved). ⚠ THREE SILENT HALF-ABILITIES CAUGHT BEFORE PLAY, worth recording because each would have LOOKED correct. (1) The apex latch that fires each beat's shot cleared where the ABILITY begins, not where each ARC does — beats two and three would have dodged backward and thrown nothing. (2) The arc was built once at the press and follow-ups never re-entered that method, so beats two and three would have flown beat one's STORED direction — the way the body faced at the first press, even after turning — behaviour that only ever matched RETREAT-DIST's facing rule by coincidence, because facing used to lock for the whole (then single-beat) action. It now builds fresh per beat, off the body's live facing. (3) The kick never captured an aim POINT, because a cone never needed one — the fireball would have flown at whatever the LAST ability had aimed at, and a stale aim point does not error, it just fires somewhere the player never looked. All three were caught and fixed the same day, before any build shipped with them live. ⚠ AND BUG (3) HAD A FOURTH SIBLING, FOUND IN PLAY THE NEXT DAY (2026-08-15, owner: "each strike is fixed to the original one no matter where I aim"). The aim was captured at the OPENER only. Follow-up beats re-enter through the shared chain machinery, which deliberately leaves the committed aim ALONE — the volley's follow-ups cone off that anchor, so for the volley not rewriting it is the mechanism. The retreat's payload instead fires at the arc's apex from the committed world POINT, so beats two and three flew at beat one's point — and drifted further off the crosshair as the caster fled it (measured 97.0° and 101.7° against asked-for bearings of 160° and 37.6°). The fix: the retreat re-commits its aim per beat at the press — the opener's own two lines, run at the tick facing re-locks, so the spray line and the held facing cannot disagree. Why the harness never saw it: RETREAT-CHAIN-3M holds the aim on the bag for all three beats, so the stale anchor and the live aim were the same line and every bolt flew correctly by coincidence — its own header lists this exact fault while being unable to produce it. A chain's re-aim is only falsifiable in a scenario that TURNS: RETREAT-CHAIN-TURN (new the same day) turns ±70° between beats, and the [LabShot] stream now carries each bolt's launch bearing (Fireball.LaunchDir, a read-only measurement property). Measured fixed: bolt bearings match each beat's asked-for aim exactly (160.0°, 37.6°), and the bag, aimed away from twice, takes one impact instead of three. The straight-line scenario re-ran unchanged — damage, count and displacement identical. ⚠ A PRE-EXISTING BUG WAS FOUND AND FIXED ON THE WAY — NOT PART OF THIS ABILITY, recorded here because this is the code path that surfaced it. DropAction cleared the action, the shield, the pending ignition and the heat, but left the chain kind, its window and its pending flag alive. A player killed or gated mid-chain kept a live window that went on claiming its continue key — the same ~1.9 s swallowed-press failure class of 2026-08-02, reached down a path nobody had checked, and it survives a respawn in any session with no GameManager. Same file, same shape, as the IgnitePending line directly above it, added for the frozen drift and never swept down to its neighbour. See ROADMAP C9 for the same note. STATUS: built, and judged in play 2026-08-15 — the owner played the chain, found the stale-aim sibling above, and confirmed the fix: "Dodge strikes are working properly now." The bot debt stands: it rolls chain intent only at the emberhook, so it throws beat one at the retreat kick and never continues; it also writes the dodge key raw in four other places a live chain would swallow as a follow-up press instead.

NEW 2026-08-14 — NO PRECEDENT IN THE KIT. Fuel drains 1:1 while firing and the whole tank refills in 3.0 s, gated by a 0.5 s delay before refill starts and a 0.5 s fuel minimum required to open the stream at all. Refill is NOT 1:1 either — fuel returns 1.67x faster than it burns, so a 0.5 s tap costs only 0.3 s of refill time. 5.0 s of beam + 3.0 s of full refill = 8.0 s for the bar's 40-damage cap (FLAME-DPS) = the Special row's 5.0 damage-per-second-of-commitment rate, the same rate every other Special-row ability pays. ⚠ THE GATING MECHANISM CHANGED (cooldown → fuel bar with drain, refill, refill delay and a minimum to open), so the MEASURED 2026-08-02 entry above is measuring a channel that no longer exists in this form* — it proved the OLD fixed-cap behaviour and says nothing about drain rate, refill rate, refill delay, or the open-minimum. This row owes a fresh measurement of the fuel-bar mechanics specifically, not a re-check of the old cap number.

STRIKE-HOVER Hold at the crest pauses the arc up to 1.0 s; a tap hangs for zero [BUILT]
_strikeHoverMax 1.0 on Player.prefab. StrikeHoverStartTick/StrikeHoverEndTick are the two networked …note
_strikeHoverMax 1.0 on Player.prefab. StrikeHoverStartTick/StrikeHoverEndTick are the two networked stamps; the pause is a derived pure function of them (PausedTicks), subtracted inside ArcElapsedTicks, which position, the crest test, the release and the landing all read — so they stop together, and a resim recomputes rather than replays. ⚠ Scoped inside the derivation (Action != AirStrike returns 0) after the escape bug threw dodge and jump into the sky — the arc mover is shared by SIX abilities, and the guard-in-the-getter is what a launch-site clear could never be: unforgettable. ⚠ The retreat kick's and the 540's apex latches deliberately keep the RAW elapsed expression — routing them through the shared clock would silently hand them a hover the day anything else sets the stamps. The Animator freeze re-sourced from networked state the same day (IsStrikeHovering), replacing the 08-15 local cosmetic hold that let the body fall the whole arc under a held pose. STRIKE-HOVER-CAP Hard server-side ceiling; a dropped release packet cannot extend the hang [BUILT]
The ceiling compares the current tick against the networked start stamp — it advances with no input at …note
The ceiling compares the current tick against the networked start stamp — it advances with no input at all, so Fusion's stale-input reuse (a lost release reads as "held" forever) cannot touch it. _strikeHoverMax is load-bearing and may never be zeroed or removed (its tooltip says so). The airborne failsafe is extended by the ceiling at AirStrike launch without changing what ActiveDuration() reports, because that number feeds clip fitting. Measured: a held hang ended by the ceiling at 64 ticks = 1.0 s at the 64 Hz sim.
STRIKE-HOVER-HIT A damaging impact ends the hang and forfeits the punch; damage-over-time does not [BUILT]
PlayerHealth.LastImpactTick, stamped only when isImpact in ApplyDamage — a stamp, not a flag or event …note
PlayerHealth.LastImpactTick, stamped only when isImpact in ApplyDamage — a stamp, not a flag or event, so a rollback re-derives the same answer; compared >= so a hit on the hang's opening tick still cancels, and cleared on round reset so a stale stamp cannot kill round 2's first hover. The burn never reaches ApplyDamage (TickBurn writes Health directly — routing it through would refresh its own timer), so the exclusion was free by construction; the flow self-cook, the one DoT that does go through, passes isImpact: false explicitly rather than riding the burn flag. Forfeit: the payload latch closes unconditionally on exit and the spawn is gated on no-hit — spent, not deferred. Measured, three AbilityLab scenarios (HOVER-HOLD / HOVER-HIT / HOVER-BURN): hang 64/21/64 ticks, 0.0000 m height drift, 1/0/1 strike bolts, health 95 → 93 from burn during a hang that held.

DRIFT-SPEED 4.0 m/s omnidirectional [BUILT]
⚠ 3.3 → 4.0 ON 2026-08-14 (owner call): THE FIRE GLIDE IS NOW THE FASTEST MOVEMENT IN THE GAME. …note
⚠ 3.3 → 4.0 ON 2026-08-14 (owner call): THE FIRE GLIDE IS NOW THE FASTEST MOVEMENT IN THE GAME. _driftSpeed 4 on the prefab, applied by PlayerMovement ahead of the backpedal branch. It beats the forward sprint, and it beats it in EVERY direction where the sprint is forward-only — so this cell owns both the top speed and the only omnidirectional way to reach it. What pays for that is the resource, not the cap: the glide is a fuelled state you cannot attack out of, and DRIFT-FUEL holds the tank, the delay and the refill that price it. ⚠ THE DRIFT THAT HID THIS IS WORTH MORE THAN THE NUMBER. This field never moved. The walk family was rescaled TWICE underneath it, so this row's own description of the glide as "above a walk and near the sprint" became wrong without anybody editing a word of it — by 2026-08-14 a glide called "near the sprint" was already well clear of one. NOTHING CAUGHT IT, AND NOTHING COULD: the gate binds this NUMBER to the prefab and the number was always green. What rotted was the RELATIONSHIP between two fields that were each individually correct, and no file holds a relationship. A sentence that describes one value in terms of another is a claim with no owner — re-read it whenever either side moves. HISTORY, MEASURED AT THE OLD 3.3 AND KEPT AS EVIDENCE OF THE SHAPE RATHER THAN THE SPEED — MEASURED 2026-08-02: 3.30 m/s peak STRAFING and 3.30 m/s peak BACKWARD — so the "omnidirectional" half is confirmed as directly as it can be, and that half survives the retune untouched. Backward is the load-bearing direction: against the backpedal of the day the drift moved you 1.95x faster backwards than walking, which is the exemption the guard slot is bought for. Both sides of that ratio have since moved — the glide up today, the backpedal up with MOVE-BACK's retirement the same day — so the ratio is history and the gap is wider than it reads here; the peak speeds owe a re-measure at 4.0. A trap survived here: Active is neither Startup nor Recovery, so without an explicit case the drift would have fallen through to the commitment fallback (then a 0.3 flat, now the glide's valley floor of 0) — a "high-mobility evasive state" crippled or frozen.
DRIFT-CHARGE ~~10 m for a full charge~~ [DEAD]
Retired 2026-08-14, superseded by DRIFT-FUEL. The charge filled by DISTANCE, accumulated from …note
Retired 2026-08-14, superseded by DRIFT-FUEL. The charge filled by DISTANCE, accumulated from replicated KCC velocity, and the bar drew only above zero — which read as a live meter for as long as the drift was a fast walk, because circling always kept the number climbing. The fire glide is a hover you can sit in: hold still and the charge stays at zero, and the bar does not draw at all. Owner, after playing the hover: "I forgot we'd done that for the bar. I'm not sure I want to go in that direction anymore. Lets make it a true resource." Nothing in the HUD had changed; the ability moved out from under the meter that used to fit it. The 2026-08-02 measurement (circling banked the charge to exactly the 10.00 cap and held there) stays true of the code that is now gone — kept so the history reads as a decision, not a lost result. The accumulator and its full-charge value survive in code, unreferenced and always zero, per the retired-field rule. No prefab binding is recorded here on purpose: a backticked field name in a dead row is read as a live binding by the drift check.
DRIFT-FUEL 3.0 s tank [BUILT]
Replaces DRIFT-CHARGE as the cell's resource (owner, 2026-08-14: "Lets make it a true resource"). The …note
Replaces DRIFT-CHARGE as the cell's resource (owner, 2026-08-14: "Lets make it a true resource"). The shape is the flamethrower's own fuel bar (the ability has since left the kit, 2026-08-15; the PATTERN is what was borrowed and it survives), deliberately, not a new mechanism. Drains 1:1 with wall time while the glide is held; running dry ends the glide exactly as releasing does, and BOTH arm the refill delay, so feathering the trigger costs the same as a deliberate tap. The M1 conversion is checked BEFORE the dry and release branches (BeginDriftLunge is read first in the held-drift tick), so a lunge thrown on the last drop of fuel lands rather than being swallowed. Refill runs through every other action — gating it on standing idle would make every OTHER ability cost glide fuel. Shorter than the flamethrower's 5.0 s tank because the glide is the fastest movement in the game in every direction. The bar draws on the flame bar's own rule — visible whenever the tank is below full, dimmed below the threshold that opens the glide — through PlayerHUD's existing DriftFuelFraction read. ⚠ Unlike the flamethrower's fuel, DriftFuel also initialises in Spawned() — the flamethrower's shows empty for about 3 s on a fresh spawn, a small existing wart this deliberately does not copy. _driftMaxHold 3, _driftRefillSeconds 2.5, _driftRefillDelay 0.4, _driftMinFuelToStart 0.5, all on PlayerCombat, verified written into Player.prefab at the 2-space component indent. STATUS: built. The FEEL is unverified — nobody has held the tank down to empty in a real exchange.
DRIFT-LUNGE 7.75 flat damage, 3.2 m flat reach [BUILT]
⚠ THIS RESOLVES THE ROW'S LONG-STANDING partial, closed 2026-08-14. The spec wanted damage to floor at …note
⚠ THIS RESOLVES THE ROW'S LONG-STANDING partial, closed 2026-08-14. The spec wanted damage to floor at 6.5 while reach floored at 0.5x; the code applied ONE scale to both (core = _lungeCoreDamage ScaleOrOne), so a zero-charge lunge did 4.5 — open since 2026-08-02 because closing it meant a 44% buff to an unearned lunge, the owner's call to make. Owner: "DMG from the lunge can just be fixed to our dmg grid for this ability. Its just more intuitive that way." There is no unearned lunge any more (DRIFT-CHARGE is dead), so the question dissolved rather than being answered. Damage sits FLAT at _driftLungeMaxDamage 7.75, the MIDDLE of the Motion band — picked over the old ceiling of 9 because fixing at the ceiling would have quietly bought every lunge the best one used to earn, for nothing asked in return. _driftLungeDistance 3.2 m is flat too, for the same reason: the Mathf.Lerp(0.5f, 1f, DriftSpent) that used to scale it with charge is still in the code, but BeginDriftLunge now forces DriftSpent to 1 every time, so the Lerp always resolves to the ceiling. ⚠ AND IT IS A FIREBALL NOW, NOT A CONE (owner: "Lets change the lunge to ignite a fireball instead of a close range fireblast"). BlastKind.DriftLunge and its four _lunge fields in PlayerFireBlast are retired, not deleted — the same treatment BlastKind.BicycleKick got. ShotAbility.DriftLunge = 6 is appended (wire format, append-only; retired index 3 stays retired) so the flight ledger can tell a lunge's fireball from a jab's. The damage stays flat for free: Fireball.CannonDamageFactor only applies its falloff curve when CannonRadius > 0, and the lunge's spawn passes 0 — there is no curve to exempt from, no tag work needed. The ember cannon needed its own tag only because its oval look demanded a radius, which is what dragged it onto the shield cannon's curve and cost it 4 damage at 12 m before anyone had fired it. ⚠ A BUG CAUGHT IN THE SWAP: the lunge never captured an aim POINT, because the cone never needed one — so the fireball would have flown at whatever the LAST ability had aimed at, and a stale aim point does not error, it just sends the shot somewhere the player never looked. BeginDriftLunge now captures CommittedAimPoint on the same tick it captures CommittedAimDir, and the spawn resolves direction toward that point rather than the raw direction, because the muzzle sits off the camera axis. ⚠ THREE CONSEQUENCES FLAGGED AND NOT PRICED, 2026-08-14: (1) lag compensation INVERTS — the cone resolved at the present tick with no rewind, deliberately ("the counter-play to a cone is to not be standing in it"), and a fireball rewinds per-seat, handing the attacker rewind he did not have; (2) the opponent gains counterplay he did not have either — a fireball can be shielded and caught by flow, where a cone could be neither; (3) DRIFT-CD's 3.7 s was derived from damage plus displacement alone, with range nowhere in the model, so the conversion becoming a projectile leaves that cell owing a fresh derivation — see that row. STATUS: built. Nobody has thrown this fireball at a moving opponent — the owner has flown the drift in a sandbox only.
DRIFT-CD 3.31 s [BUILT]
⚠ RE-DERIVED 3.7 → 3.31 s ON 2026-08-14, BECAUSE THE DAMAGE TERM WAS STALE. _driftCooldown 3.31 s. The …note
⚠ RE-DERIVED 3.7 → 3.31 s ON 2026-08-14, BECAUSE THE DAMAGE TERM WAS STALE. _driftCooldown 3.31 s. The lunge's 7.75 damage (DRIFT-LUNGE) at the Motion row's 3.5 damage-per-second-of-commitment rate is 2.21 s, plus 3.2 m of lunge displacement at the dodge's 0.57 s/m rate = 1.83 s, less the ~0.73 s the lunge itself takes. The old figure used a damage term of 9 — the CEILING of the 6.5-to-9 charge ramp, which the owner retired earlier the SAME DAY when the lunge became flat. The displacement and duration terms did not move. ⚠ THE CLASS OF ERROR MATTERS MORE THAN THE 0.39 s: A COOLDOWN IS DERIVED FROM OTHER FIELDS, AND NO GATE JOINS A DERIVED NUMBER TO ITS INPUTS. The prefab check proved the cooldown was stored faithfully. The damage check proved the damage was stored faithfully. Both were green while the arithmetic between them was wrong. When a number feeds a derivation, moving it owes the derivation a re-run — the master rule's paired-number rule, applied to a pair that lives in two different fields instead of one. ⚠ THE STRUCTURAL HALF: THE TIMER IS ARMED BY THE LUNGE NOW, NOT BY THE GLIDE'S PRESS (owner call, same day). BeginDriftLunge arms it and BeginDrift arms nothing, so entering the glide costs FUEL and nothing else — the flamethrower's rule, applied to the only other fuelled ability in the kit (and since the flamethrower's 2026-08-15 retirement, the only one). Before this, the full timer was set at the press. That did two wrong things at once: it charged the LUNGE'S price whether or not you ever lunged, and it made this the one cell paying a cooldown AND a fuel bar for a single commitment. FLAME-CD names that double-charge as the thing the project refuses everywhere else; the drift was quietly doing it. ⚠ WHAT THE MOVE BUYS THE PLAYER IS NEW AND UNJUDGED. Dropping the glide without spending it is cheap now, and so are short taps — cheap in a way a full lockout never allowed. The refill DELAY is the only thing standing against feathering the trigger. At the shipped tank, delay and refill this settles near a 50% duty cycle: sustained, omnidirectional, and above the forward sprint. Watch it first in play. MEASURED 2026-08-02 — but BOUNDED, not bracketed: the harness found it already ready 5.91 s after the press, so the measurement says "at most 5.91 s" rather than "5.0 s" — against the then-authored 5.0. Recorded as built because the code path is the identical TickTimer the other five cooldowns use, and all five of those measured exact to within one tick. ⚠ That sentence used to add "set at initiation", and since 2026-08-14 initiation is the LUNGE rather than the glide's press — the timer shape is unchanged, the moment it starts is not. THE SUPERSEDED DERIVATION, KEPT SO THE 0.39 s READS AS A CORRECTION RATHER THAN A NUDGE — 5.0 → 3.7 s on 2026-08-02, the cost-economy pass: the lunge's 9 damage (DRIFT-LUNGE, the ceiling of its 6.5–9 range) at the Motion row's 3.5 damage-per-second-of-commitment rate is 2.57 s, plus 3.2 m of lunge displacement at the dodge's 0.57 s/m rate = 1.83 s — a 4.4 s cycle, less the ~0.73 s the lunge itself takes, giving 3.7 s. Priced on the FULL-charge lunge deliberately — pricing the floor of DRIFT-LUNGE's range instead would make a fully-charged drift cheaper per point of damage than a half-charged one, rewarding exactly the wrong side of DRIFT-CHARGE's distance-earns-power design. That reasoning died with the charge ramp: there is no half-charged lunge left to be cheaper than. The bound measured above is against the superseded 5.0 s target and owes a fresh bound at 3.31. ⚠ THE RANGE DEBT IS STILL OPEN, AND THE RE-DERIVATION ABOVE DID NOT PAY IT. The conversion this cell prices is a projectile now (DRIFT-LUNGE), and the model still has no range term at all — it prices damage, displacement and duration, and nothing else. Three shifts ride on that swap and they were WEIGHED rather than measured: the attacker gained lag-compensation rewind that a cone never had, the opponent gained counterplay he never had (a fireball can be shielded and caught by flow; a cone can be neither), and the reach itself is no longer a lunge's length. The judgement is that those roughly offset, so the price stands. That judgement is UNMEASURED, and it is the second thing on this row to watch in play.
DRIFT-HEIGHT 0.30 m lift, body and hit area together [BUILT]
Mechanism confirmed against the actual NetworkCharacterController, not assumed: PlayerMovement writes …note
Mechanism confirmed against the actual NetworkCharacterController, not assumed: PlayerMovement writes the capsule's vertical velocity immediately before its one _kcc.Move call, pre-cancelling the gravity Move is about to add. It works because Move reads velocity first, adds gravity at exactly one line, touches only x and z in its horizontal branches, and re-derives velocity from the capsule's actual displacement afterward — Velocity is a public settable property, so nothing new had to be built to reach it. The floor is CAPTURED, not cast: PlayerCombat.DriftBaseY takes the standing height once, in BeginDrift, on a body DriftReady has already required to be Grounded — the same pattern the jump arc's own launch data uses. ⚠ It assumes a FLAT floor; a sloped arena would need a per-tick downward ray, and that ray would need a ground layer mask, which the capture avoids paying for. ⚠ The drift is deliberately NOT in IsAirborne — that set means a committed arc owns the capsule, and PlayerMovement returns on it before the entire speed and steering block runs, so joining it would delete the 3.3 m/s omnidirectional movement that IS the ability. ⚠ It is also not the raw CharacterController — driving that from PlayerCombat is the chain step's own recorded mistake; the KCC fought it. ⚠ THE FIRST PLAYTEST FOUND A REAL BUG, AND ONLY A LOG TRACE COULD HAVE FOUND IT. 2470 [Hover] lines from the owner's session showed every glide ending with the body 13-20 cm above its own captured floor, falling at ~0.8 m/s, with grounded FALSE — so for a fraction of a second after every glide, all thirteen ground-gated abilities (dodge, jump, flow, ring, flurry, another drift) silently refused, and none of them logs anything when they refuse. Root cause: spring 60 against damping 8 settles over about a second, against a 0.18 s rise and a 0.20 s fall — the hover was never going to arrive in time. Fixed in two places: _hoverSpring 60 → 300 and _hoverDamping 8 → 22 (the RATIO held at 0.63, so the hover got faster without reading stiffer), plus a new _hoverLandBias 0.35 that sinks the target BELOW the floor as the profile falls, pulling the body down and planting it rather than easing toward a height it cannot reach in time — the capsule cannot pass the ground, so the overshoot is free. ⚠ VERIFIED FIXED FROM A SECOND SESSION, 1124 trace lines: at profile 0 the body reads its standing height with grounded TRUE, and the hold now reaches height and slightly overshoots it — 1.338 against a 1.329 target — the under-damped settle working as designed. ⚠ ALSO ANSWERED: the design record's open question of whether the CharacterController would fight a small sustained lift near the floor. It does not. The damping stays under critical on purpose (spring 300 wants ~34.6 to be critical; it gets 22, the same 0.63 ratio the original 60/8 pair had), so the body overshoots and settles instead of snapping to height — the owner's call over "rock steady," because a dead-level hold reads as a rig on a rail rather than a body held up by its own fire. _hoverSag 0.015 m per m/s of speed sinks the body while driving and lets it float back as it steadies. ⚠ HoverProfile reaches 0 BEFORE the action ends, and that is load-bearing, not tidy: thirteen gates refuse to start an ability while the body is off the floor, and none of them logs anything when they refuse — a press landing one tick before touchdown is eaten in silence. The height rose from the design record's 0.23 m to 0.30 m the same day it was built, on the owner's call, to make room for the centre jet ("If we need to raise the height to make room for the larger jet thats fine too"). 0.30 m is the TOP of his original 6-to-12-inch band, not past it. ⚠ It is no longer a free knob: the jets clamp against the floor (DRIFT-JETS), so lowering the hover shortens the engine holding the body up. ⚠ Accepted and UNPRICED: clearing a ring stance needs 2.5 m of rise — 1.5 m clear height plus 1.0 m for half the capsule — so a 0.30 m hover shaves about 12% off that margin. Flagged to the owner; he may price it later. _hoverHeight 0.3, _hoverSpring 300, _hoverDamping 22, _hoverSag 0.015, _hoverLandBias 0.35, all on PlayerMovement, verified written into Player.prefab at the 2-space component indent. STATUS: built, and the landing is verified twice over — once as the bug, once as the fix. The rest of the ability, how it plays in a real exchange rather than a sandbox, is still unjudged.
DRIFT-POSE The jump loop, held, unmodified [BUILT]
The drift's FIRST animator state, ever: Drift_Hold, keyed on Phase == Active, fade 0.30 s, clip JUMP …note
The drift's FIRST animator state, ever: Drift_Hold, keyed on Phase == Active, fade 0.30 s, clip JUMP LOOP referenced BY NAME — the flurry's five-day dangling-fileID lesson applied on the first try rather than relearned. ⚠ It replaces a DELIBERATE absence, and the note it replaces was right when written: Locomotion is the correct render for a movement state, and a held pose would make the kit's most mobile stance the only one that does not move its feet. That held while the drift was a fast walk. It stops holding the moment the body leaves the floor. THE OWNER KEPT THE TUCK ON SIGHT. The design record asked for arms raised and palms turned down. A frame strip of the raw take showed what the clip actually is: a flying TUCK — knees folded, feet drawn up behind with the toes pointed, torso leaning forward, arms reaching FORWARD at chest height, not up. Two consequences no description would have surfaced: the arms are nowhere near raised, and the soles face backward-up, so the downward foot jets the brief asked for would have fired into the character's own back. Offered a re-pose of four limb chains, a sweep of the packs for a different base clip, or keeping the tuck as shot, the owner chose the tuck: "a flying glide rather than a standing hover." So there is NO surgery here at all — the raw clip is wired unmodified. The clip earns its place on numbers it already had, not on anything reworked for this: 0.0 degrees of yaw sweep, 0.03 m of hip drift across all 15 frames, 0 degrees of spin per frame, already looping with a baked loop pose. ⚠ ActDrift had to be added to the AnyState -> Locomotion guard list. That list carried every action from 1-12 and 14-18; 13 was simply absent, because nothing had ever exercised it before this pass. Without the addition, the drift's own recovery tick yanks the hover pose back to Locomotion mid-ability — which presents as a fade bug and is not one. The gate log confirms exactly ONE condition was added: "guarded with Action != 13" and no other, so every sibling action was already covered and only the drift was ever exposed. Startup and Recovery still render Locomotion on purpose: the body is climbing and settling through those, not holding a pose — if the entry or the landing reads badly in play, the remedy is two more rows of the same shape, never a longer held state. states and motions both pass: Drift_Hold <- JUMP LOOP, AnyState -> Drift_Hold, fade 0.300 s, no dangling reference. STATUS: built. The LOOK is unverified — nobody has watched the hold pose in motion yet.
DRIFT-JETS Three jets — two palms down, one larger centred under the feet [BUILT]
⚠ This project has ZERO authored fire assets — no particle systems, no VFX graphs, no fire materials …note
⚠ This project has ZERO authored fire assets — no particle systems, no VFX graphs, no fire materials, no fire prefabs anywhere in the project. Every flame is a procedural mesh built in C# at runtime, so this is a new component in the blast cone's family, not an import. THREE jets, not four — the first draft put one jet on each foot, matching the design record's four-limb brief literally. The owner corrected it mid-build: "Fire jets propulsion firing downward from both hands and a single larger jet down centered from the feet." The centre jet is the better read on this pose anyway, since the tuck (DRIFT-POSE) draws the feet together and two separate soles would fight for the same space. The owner's bar for the look, verbatim: "these jets logically would need to be powerful in world to hold a body against gravity. They need to be convincing." So each jet is TWO nested cones — a hot near-white CORE inside a wider, dimmer orange FLARE, so the fire reads as pressure holding a body up rather than as decoration. ⚠ THE CONES WERE BUILT BACKWARD, AND THE OWNER CAUGHT IT BY EYE, WITH A SCREENSHOT: "Invert the cones so that the cones base comes from the hands and the feet and the cones tip sits at the bottom." A jet leaves a nozzle at its WIDEST and tapers; the blast cone this was modelled on starts at an apex and spreads — copying the blast's geometry copied the wrong physics along with it. The mouth now sits full width at the limb and narrows to a point below. Heights cut by two thirds the same build: _handLength 0.95 → 0.32, _footLength 1.25 → 0.42. The half-angle parameters (_handHalfAngle, _footHalfAngle) are GONE, replaced by an explicit mouth radius — _handRadius 0.05, _footRadius 0.07 — because with the cone this way round the mouth width is the thing being judged, so it is typed in rather than derived from an angle. ⚠ TWO PLACEMENT FAULTS FOUND FROM SCREENSHOTS, BOTH INVISIBLE TO EVERY INSTRUMENT IN THIS PROJECT. The hand jets left the WRISTS, because a Mixamo LeftHand/RightHand bone IS the wrist joint — fixed by walking 60% of the way from that joint toward the middle finger's first bone, a second landmark rather than a fixed offset in metres, since an offset in metres is right for one rig and silently wrong for the next; falls back to the wrist if the finger bone is unmapped, and to the capsule if there is no rig at all. The foot jet rode the midpoint of both feet, which floats up beside whichever foot the held tuck pose happens to raise — it now picks whichever foot is LOWER every frame, which is also the one that reads as carrying the weight; a per-frame choice, not a fixed foot, since the tuck's feet swap heights across the clip. No probe in this project measures where a cosmetic mesh sits relative to a hand; the gate checked that the fields existed and the bake succeeded, and both were already true. ⚠ AND THE CONES CLIPPED THE MODEL, FOR A REASON WORTH KEEPING: a bone landmark sits INSIDE the body part it names — the palm point inside the hand, the ankle inside the foot — so a cone whose widest end sits exactly on the landmark buries that end in the mesh. The mouth now stands off along the JET'S OWN DIRECTION instead of sideways: _handStandoff 0.08, _footStandoff 0.16 — two values, because a foot is a great deal thicker than a palm. _palmReach 0.6 (the 60% walk from wrist toward the finger bone) and _floorClearance 0.1 carry forward unchanged. ⚠ NO JET CAN EVER REACH THE FLOOR, and it is enforced by construction rather than by eye — the owner's rule: "Dont allow the feet jet to collide with the floor ever." The hover (DRIFT-HEIGHT) already captures the floor at the press, so each jet's length is clamped to the room actually beneath it, minus a clearance. The clamp is applied to all three jets rather than the foot jet alone, and written general over direction, so it keeps holding when the jets swing backward for the lunge (DRIFT-STRIKE). There is no tuning value anyone can set that puts fire through the ground. ⚠ EXECUTION ORDER BROKE ONCE, AND THE FIX IS THE RULE NOW: DriftJetVisual ran at order 101 and the new DriftLean at 102, so every frame the jets sampled the hand and foot bones and THEN the lean rotated the whole rig out from under them — fire pinned in space with the body swinging around it, exactly as the owner described it. The chain is now ChestTwist (100) → DriftLean (101) → DriftJetVisual (102): anything that READS a bone must run after everything that WRITES one. ⚠ COSMETIC ONLY, a hard boundary: it must never route through PlayerFireBlast, the server-authoritative DAMAGE geometry whose drawn origin doubles as the hit origin — which is why that component may not read a bone and this one may, and it is also why the jets could not have gone through it in the first place, since its kind is a single [Networked] enum and exactly one cone exists at a time by construction. Bones are SAMPLED fresh every frame, never parented: the rig imports through a 100x node scale, so a parented child renders a hundred times too big. Built as a mesh, never a primitive: a primitive ships a collider, and a collider under the feet would shove bodies around and read as a wall to the ring's cover linecast. No authority checks anywhere in it — everything derives from replicated state, so host, client and proxy converge with nothing extra on the wire. All eight serialized fields verified present in Player.prefab at the 2-space component indent. STATUS: built. The LOOK is unverified in a real exchange — the owner has seen it only in the sandbox that found and fixed the faults above.
DRIFT-LEAN Up to 14 deg of lean at full drift speed [BUILT]
Owner: "I also need the player to lean in the direction they are moving." _maxLean 14, _leanAtSpeed 3.3 …note
Owner: "I also need the player to lean in the direction they are moving." _maxLean 14, _leanAtSpeed 3.3 (the speed at which the lean reaches its own maximum), _leanFollow 9 (an exponential smoothing rate, so a direction change swings the body rather than snapping it). ⚠ TILTS THE RIG, NEVER THE NETWORKED ROOT — the honest choice, not the convenient one. The authoritative position stays server-simulated and upright; HEAD-HITBOX is parented to the head BONE, so it is a child of the rig and leans WITH the body — the head the opponent sees and the head the opponent can shoot stay the same head. Tilting a root while the hitbox stayed upright would be exactly the prettier lie the visual-honesty rule forbids. ⚠ PIVOTS AT THE FEET, NOT THE HIPS (owner: "the body should pivot around the feet jet"). Rotating about the rig's own origin pivots at the hips, and a figure tipping about its hips reads as sliding sideways rather than leaning; DriftLean instead rotates about the midpoint of the two foot bones — where the centre jet fires from — turning the orientation AND swinging the rig's position around that same pivot. The rig is reset to its authored pose before the feet are read, so the pivot is measured off an upright body rather than off last frame's lean; falls back to the capsule's own foot plane when the rig cannot answer. The angle derives from replicated velocity and HoverProfile, so every peer computes the identical lean with nothing extra on the wire, and it decays to zero on its own as the glide ends. ⚠ EXECUTION ORDER COST ONE BUILD, RECORDED IN FULL AT DRIFT-JETS: the jets sampled bones this lean then moved, until the two were reordered ChestTwist → DriftLean → DriftJetVisual. _maxLean, _leanAtSpeed, _leanFollow verified present in Player.prefab at the 2-space component indent. STATUS: built. The LOOK is unverified — this is the newest piece of the cell and the owner has not yet watched it in a real exchange.

The recorded measurement was right; a foreshortened arm on the strip was read wrong. A known-good reference beat an eyeball, for the cost of one export. Gates: split, states, motions and audit all pass; the logs read DriftLunge <- DOUBLE PUNCH FIST LUNGE, playback 1.00x, resolved with no dangling reference, and BACK KICK CUT stays split in the pool but is now unreferenced anywhere in the controller. Net: the twisting is fixed and measured; the position is authored into the take and needs a re-posed chain or a different take — that is authoring, not curve surgery. ⚠ THE INSTRUMENT LESSON: the chamber track APPROVED the -0.9 re-pose (clearance 0.20 → 0.28 m off the chest) while the frame strip showed a diving pose nobody would ship — clearance is a necessary number and nowhere near a sufficient one, and nothing in the probe kit yet separates "out at the side" from "flung backward," the same instrument-class gap the week already paid for once, when pose sampling alone could not certify motion.

FLOW-REDIRECT M1 throws the held fireball as a standard fireball, from the neutral jab [UNVERIF]
ShotAbility.FlowThrow. The throw fires through the ordinary FireShot path and plays the jab's own …note
ShotAbility.FlowThrow. The throw fires through the ordinary FireShot path and plays the jab's own FB_Startup/FB_Strike states -- no new clip. HoldingCatch clears on the throw and the stance ends. Windows _flowReleaseStartup 0.357 / _flowReleaseRecovery 0.4675 equal the fireball's own, so the borrowed states fit without a re-time. ⚠ OPEN DESIGN QUESTION, OWNER'S CALL, RECORDED NOT DECIDED (2026-08-17): the catch is an UNPRICED UPGRADE PATH. Fireball.ResolvePlayerContact is the single caller of the catch and it asks nothing about the shot it is catching, so EVERY fireball in the game is catchable -- a flurry bolt at 1.75 damage, a neutral jab at 12.5, a shield cannon at 22.68. What comes back out is always a stock 12.5 jab. So catching a flurry bolt multiplies it about sevenfold, and catching a cannon halves it. The old heat model did not have this shape: heat scaled with the damage absorbed, so a big catch bought a big release and a small one bought little. The redirect throws one fixed shot whatever it swallowed. Nothing is changed here pending the verdict -- the options are all live (scale the throw off the caught shot, filter what is catchable, or accept the flattening as the rally's own logic).

HoldingCatch clears on the toggle-off and on the throw, and ResetForRound/DropAction clear it at death …note
HoldingCatch clears on the toggle-off and on the throw, and ResetForRound/DropAction clear it at death and the round gate. ⚠ THE SET OF CLOSES IS SMALLER THAN THIS ROW SAID UNTIL 2026-08-17. Nothing else pre-empts the stance: PlayerCombat's new-action block is gated on Action == ActionKind.None, and flow holds Action through its whole Active phase, so dodge, shield and jump presses are SWALLOWED -- no action starts, and the stance survives the press untouched. TryArmStance runs ABOVE that gate and is ungated on purpose (arming is a latch, not an action), so arming a Shape mid-hold succeeds and leaves the stance open -- you come out of the hold with a Shape already armed. Deliberate narrowing, recorded rather than smoothed over, and it owes the owner's play verdict: a stance you can only leave on its own key is a different bargain from one a dodge bails you out of.

PlayerHealth.ApplyDamage(..., lightsBurn) -- default true for impacts; the flow overhold passes false …note
PlayerHealth.ApplyDamage(..., lightsBurn) -- default true for impacts; the flow overhold passes false (PlayerCombat, the only DoT caller today). The one-instance half is structural: BurnEndTick/NextBurnTick are single networked fields, so a second concurrent burn has nowhere to live. Found and fixed 2026-07-29; the overhold tail bug shipped with S8 and was never play-reported -- the surcharge hid inside the escalating cook. 2026-08-02/03: the burn now STACKS (owner: "allow this burn to stack (I wanna try it)") -- see BURN-STACKS. This row's own claim is UNCHANGED by that: still ONE instance per victim, still no DoT lighting a DoT. Stacking added a COUNT on top of the one instance rather than a second instance, and pulse sources still only REFRESH it, never add to the count.
BURN-STACKS 3 stack cap, impacts add a stack, pulses refresh only [UNVERIF]
BurnStacks [Networked] int, _burnMaxStacks 3 on PlayerHealth. ApplyBurn(attacker, addStack): impacts …note
BurnStacks [Networked] int, _burnMaxStacks 3 on PlayerHealth. ApplyBurn(attacker, addStack): impacts add a stack up to the cap; PULSE sources route through burnStyle (PlayerFireBlast/PlayerRingBlast's existing flag, built for a different distinction and reused whole -- "burnStyle already encodes exactly the distinction burn stacking needs") so a pulse only refreshes the burn's clock, never its count. Stacks multiply the per-second RATE (1 HP/s per stack), never the duration -- one shared 3 s expiry, refreshed on every relight, worst tail 3 x 1 x 3 = 9 HP. Cleared on expiry, death, round reset. unverified rather than built: the code is complete and the mechanism is played, but the cap and the pulse-never-stacks rule have never been OBSERVED -- nobody has demonstrably watched 3 stacks accrue and hold in a fight.

SHIELD-CONE ~60° horiz (±30°), ±35° vert [BUILT]
RENARROWED 2026-07-29 (owner call) from ±45°/±45°: the guard read as far too big in play (a 1.63 × 2.30 …note
RENARROWED 2026-07-29 (owner call) from ±45°/±45°: the guard read as far too big in play (a 1.63 × 2.30 m door), and the horizontal half-angle is the dodge-flank dial — at ±30° the break-even is ~6.1 m, so a dodge flanks a raised guard from live duel range, the intended counter (TRI-SHIELD-NOFLANK, resolved). The ±45° vertical's jump-in rationale died with the air attack (2026-07-28). Draws 1.15 × 1.61 m at radius 1.15. ⚠ The two-peer verification below was run at the OLD ±45° angles — the mechanism it verified (guard-locks-at-raise, in/out-of-cone) is angle-independent and stands, but nobody has probed the new edges from a client seat. History: RETUNED 2026-07-18 from ±60°/±26.57°. Guard aims by look direction (yaw+pitch). Why: the old cone was wide and short, and a jump-in cleared it at any range inside 4.4 m (apex 2.21 m ÷ tan 26.57°) — the shield had no answer to a jump-in at all. Equalising the angles buys the vertical lane; the price is horizontal, moving the dodge-flank break-even out from ~2.0 m to ~3.5 m. That price CONTRADICTS TRI-SHIELD-NOFLANK — flagged in spec §4, unresolved pending the p2p verdict (a deliberate call, not an oversight). Renders as a tall oval because height scales tan and width sin (2.0R × 1.41R). Two-peer RE-VERIFICATION IS DONE (2026-07-19, M8 Inc 4 / S4). Both checks were run by hand from the CLIENT seat at ~116 ms and both passed: the guard still blocks along the line it was raised on after the shielder spins (guard-locks-at-raise), and a shot from outside the cone gets through. This had been owed since before the ~9.7° aim-skew fix, across three shield reworks and a cone retune. Scripted evidence backs the same path: 18/18 BLOCKED verdicts in s4-held-host.log resolved through the rewound guard test.

FB-BURN 1.0 dmg/s for 3 s [BUILT]

DURATION 7 → 3 on 2026-07-29 (owner call: "too much" — the tail drops ~11.34 → ~4.86 HP, a sting rather than a second shot riding every hit). NEW 2026-07-19. PlayerHealth._burnDamagePerSecond 1.62 (2 → 1.8 on 2026-07-19, → 1.62 on the 2026-07-20 across-the-board cut) / _burnSeconds 3 (was 7). Re-lit by every damaging hit via ApplyDamage; ticks in FixedUpdateNetwork above the match-manager early-out, because the burn is a property of time and putting it below would silently disable it in every managed match (the same trap SHIELD-HP's regen documents). BurnEndTick is [Networked] as an ABSOLUTE tick rather than a remaining-time float — a float re-decrements on every resimulated tick and burns down faster than wall time. BurnResidue carries the sub-1-HP remainder so the rate is exact. Cleared by ResetForRound and Spawned so a round never opens with you already burning. The burn tick deliberately does NOT route through ApplyDamage — that method re-lights the burn, so it would refresh its own timer and never expire. Shipped with an across-the-board 20% burst cut (fireball 20→16, cannon 35→28) — the two are one pacing change and should be judged together. VERIFIED BY PLAY 2026-07-19, human-vs-human: passes. Judged as the paired pacing change it was shipped as. ⚠ STILL OUTSTANDING, and it is NOT covered by that pass: the burst cut silently moved the shield from 3 blocked fireballs to 3.75 (60 HP ÷ 16) — see SHIELD-HP. Nobody chose 4, and a "passes" verdict on pacing is not a verdict on that.
