SHIELD-CANNON M1 while guarding → slow heavy projectile [BUILT]
SPEED −30% ON 2026-07-29 (owner call: still "moved way too fast"): _cannonSpeed 14 → 9.8, and …note
SPEED −30% ON 2026-07-29 (owner call: still "moved way too fast"): _cannonSpeed 14 → 9.8, and _cannonLife 2.6 → 3.7 WITH it so reach stays ~36 m — the range-follows-speed trap this row itself documents, dodged on purpose this time. Undodgeable zone re-derived at 9.8: solving 0.49 = 0.50 + d/9.8 − 0.233 gives ~2.2 m (was ~4.0 m at 14) — body contact still belongs to the cannon, and everything past a step is now dodgeable on reaction. THE SIZE SHRINK BECAME A DAMAGE FALLOFF ON 2026-07-30 (owner call, from play: the reworked shield finally made the launched guard "look coherently like the same object", and a wall dissolving mid-flight undercut that). The guard now flies at FULL SIZE for its whole life; Fireball.CannonDamageFactor tapers its DAMAGE to 35% over the same first 10 m instead (fields renamed _cannonEndDamageFraction / _cannonFalloffDistance with FormerlySerializedAs, so the prefab values carried over). Applied to all three damage consumers — HP, blocked-shot shield damage, and the flow catch's harvest — via the one folded factor, so no path lands full-strength at range. Distance stays the denominator ON PURPOSE, against the owner's literal "over the lifespan" phrasing: the 2026-07-20 lesson in this row is that a per-lifetime curve is unobservable at 5–12 m fight ranges, and it applies to damage exactly as it did to size. Note the constant size REVERSES this row's hit-geometry history in the favourable direction: the late-flight cannon is now wider than the shrunk one the M8 measurements flew, never narrower. AND THEN THE CANNON ALONE SHRANK 30% (2026-07-30, owner call, the same session): _cannonRadius 1.15 → 0.805 while ShieldVisual._radius stays 1.15 — the guard↔cannon equal-size rule is REVERSED for the launched form (spec SHIELD-SIZE carries the reversal; the projectile keeps the guard's oval geometry at 70% scale, constant for the whole flight). NEW 2026-07-18. _cannonStartup 0.50 (0.25→0.40→0.50) / _cannonRecovery 0.15 / _cannonSpeed 9.8 (18→14 2026-07-19, →9.8 2026-07-29) / _cannonMaxDamage 22.68 (35→28→25.2→22.68 across the three across-the-board damage cuts) / _cannonRadius 0.5 (1.6→0.5, see SHIELD-SIZE) / SHIELD-LOCKOUT NOW ARMS on cannon fire (reversed 2026-07-19 — see the spec row for the original rationale, kept rather than deleted) / _cannonMinHealth 20 / _cannonRadius 1.6 (matches ShieldVisual._radius) / _cannonMaxDamage 35. Renders as the SHIELD'S OWN OVAL, not a scaled fireball (corrected 2026-07-18 — the first cut spawned a 2.5x fireball sphere, which is not what launching your guard should look like): Fireball.CannonRadius > 0 swaps the sphere mesh for a procedurally-built oval using the same elliptical taper and angle-derived extents as ShieldVisual, so the thing in flight is recognisably the thing that was guarding a moment ago. [Networked], so the mesh swap happens on every peer, and double-sided so it reads from behind. New ActionKind.Cannon; trigger sits BEFORE the shield-release branch so the press isn't read as "stop shielding". Launch aim CORRECTED 2026-07-20 — it now aims at a committed crosshair POINT, not along a committed direction. It used to launch along AimLookDir, and that was a real aiming bug reported from play as "offset to the left of my crosshair": a look DIRECTION taken from the camera and applied at the MUZZLE traces a line parallel to the camera's aim line, and the camera sits 0.85 m right of the body (_cameraOffset.x) while FirePoint is on the centreline (local x 0) — so the guard flew a constant ~0.85 m left of the crosshair at every range (~4° at duelling distance, on a 1.15 m-wide object) and never converged. Two parallel lines cannot meet; only a POINT can express "hit where the crosshair was" from a different origin. BeginShield now captures CommittedAimPoint = ValidatedAimPoint(input) alongside CommittedAimDir, from the same input on the same tick, and the cannon launches from the muzzle toward that point (falling back to the direction if the point is degenerately close). The commitment is unchanged — it is still captured once at guard-raise and never rewritten, so turning between raising and firing still sends it where you were looking when you RAISED. The guard's own cone keeps CommittedAimDir, which is correct for a cone attached to your body: a cone never leaves you, so it has no parallax to correct. Same class as FB-MUZZLE ("a displaced muzzle breaks the crosshair's promise without breaking any single line of code"), one level up — there the origin was displaced, here the origin the direction was applied at. Reuses the Fireball scale/damage/speed plumbing kept from the reverted charged shot — that retention paid off immediately. Empties the pool and drops the guard; no SHIELD-LOCKOUT (see spec for why). 2026-07-20 — the dissipation now tapers against DISTANCE FLOWN, not life fraction: new Fireball._cannonShrinkDistance (10 m, prefab; since 2026-07-30 renamed _cannonFalloffDistance and governing DAMAGE — see above) replaces LifeFraction as the lerp's denominator, and the LifeNet [Networked] field that existed solely to be that denominator was deleted (written every spawn, read by nobody once the curve moved — dead networked state that also implied the taper still depended on lifetime). Reported from play as "the cannon doesn't seem to shrink"; the report was right and the cause was arithmetic, not rendering — 2.6 s × 14 m/s = 36.4 m of curve against a 30 m ring and 5–12 m fights, so play only ever traversed 14–33% of it (91% width at 5 m, 79% at 12 m). Spec §SHIELD-CANNON was amended rather than the code being "fixed to match", because the code already met the spec — this is a change to what should exist, not a correction of what does. CannonWindup is [Networked] so the draw-back telegraph is identical on every peer. Fires the guard's own oval; SphereCast (radius = half the guard) rather than a hairline ray, because a ~3 m wall was sailing through geometry its edges visibly overlapped. Keeps the guard's RED and gets a widened trail. Explicitly locks the fireball out for _cannonStartup + 0.6 s — the short cannon recovery previously returned you to idle fast enough that a mashed M1 fired a jab straight out of a cannon. Telegraph lengthened 0.25→0.40 (arithmetic) then →0.50 (2026-07-19, M8 feel pass) after arithmetic showed it was UNDODGEABLE inside ~6 m from a client seat (dodging needs reaction 0.19 + dodge startup 0.15 + 0.149 s to clear the 1.3 m hitbox = 0.49 s, against startup + flight − interp). Lengthened the WIND-UP rather than slowing the projectile: the flight speed is what makes it feel heavy, and a powerhouse should announce itself. ⚠ Undodgeable zone RE-DERIVED 2026-07-19 with M8's measured interp delay (0.233 s at ~116 ms), and the old ~3.4 m figure does not reproduce — it shared the FB-STARTUP row's mistake of a far smaller interp term. Solving 0.49 = startup + d/18 − 0.233: 5.8 m at startup 0.40, 4.0 m at 0.50. So the zone was materially larger than recorded, and this change shrinks it ~1.8 m. Body contact stays undodgeable, which is intended — at contact range the cannon should win. Audio: release one-shot, looping 3D travel bed on the projectile, heavy impact that REPLACES (not layers with) the fireball cue; synthesized placeholders, weight from low frequency rather than level. ⚠ HIT-REG REGRESSION FOUND AND FIXED 2026-07-19 — the cannon had silently lost its WIDTH. HitboxManager has no sphere-cast (only Raycast / OverlapSphere / OverlapBox), so when M8 Inc 3 moved hit registration onto lag compensation, the cannon fell back to a hairline ray down the centre of a ~3 m wall on the path that is now always taken. The PhysX branch had sphere-cast it since it was built; nothing errored, and the guard simply stopped connecting unless its dead centre passed through the target. Found by PLAY, not by any harness. It was visible in the ledger and still missed for a day, because the ledger named the wrong shape: miss=0.80m/r0.50 used the TARGET's radius with no allowance for the projectile's own 0.8 m half-width, so an obvious hit read as a clean miss. Fixed in two halves — bodies via a per-tick lag-comp OverlapSphere at the real radius (at 14 m/s it advances 0.23 m per tick against a 0.8 m probe, so consecutive probes overlap threefold and form a continuous swept volume), and walls via Physics.SphereCast because static geometry does not move and therefore needs no rewind — present-position IS the right answer there, not an approximation of it. ShotRecord.HitRadius now includes the projectile's half-width. Verified: 3 of 6 cannons in the 0.6–1.0 m band scored WALL before; 8 of 8 score HIT after (Docs/m8/inc4/). ⚠ THE GUARD RE-POINTED ON LAUNCH — FOUND BY PLAY 2026-07-19 (human-vs-human), FIXED THE SAME DAY. PlayerCombat.cs:633 read CommittedAimDir = AimLookDir(input) under the comment "launches along the GUARD's line, not the crosshair point" — wrong about its own code. AimLookDir is LIVE look; CommittedAimDir already WAS the guard's line, locked once at BeginShield. The assignment therefore threw the lock away. Two consequences, one visible and one not: (1) ShieldVisual orients the oval from GuardDir => CommittedAimDir, so pressing M1 SNAPPED the guard to face the mouse — reported as "the shield face rotates when I launch, a lot of the time", intermittent and unreproducible-on-demand precisely because it only shows when the mouse moved between raise and fire; (2) it punched a hole in guard locks at raise, the premise TRI-SHIELD-NOFLANK stands on — the cannon was free aim wearing a commitment's clothes. Fix = delete the assignment (the correct value was already in the field). The cannon now fires along the line the guard was raised on, which is stricter than before: you cannot re-point a cannon. This is a FEEL change as well as a bug fix and is UNVERIFIED BY PLAY.

V1TEST-SEAT Verdict from the client seat across 20–150 ms (optimal 20–80) [BUILT]
The cadence exists and M8 is in flight — the trigger fired 2026-07-17 and Inc 1 landed — but no …note
The cadence exists and M8 is in flight — the trigger fired 2026-07-17 and Inc 1 landed — but no instrumented client-seat verdict has been produced yet. Band revised 2026-07-18 from ~80–120: past 80 ms is high, not normal, and the common wired/fibre case (20–50) was unrepresented. VERDICT DELIVERED 2026-07-19 (M8 Inc 4), and it is POSITIVE. SHIELD-CAMLOOK Known blockout artifact: the camera orbits a correctly-locked guard [BUILT]
NOT A BUG; three correct systems plus one unconstrained one (diagnosed 2026-07-20). Reported twice as …note
NOT A BUG; three correct systems plus one unconstrained one (diagnosed 2026-07-20). Reported twice as "the shield face rotates". It does not. FacingLocked (PlayerCombat.cs:459) already returns true for Shield in Startup+Active and PlayerMovement.cs:171 gates transform.rotation on it, so the BODY is rooted; ShieldVisual orients from the locked GuardDir, so the GUARD is rooted. What moves is PlayerCameraRig.Yaw, which follows the mouse unconditionally and is gated on nothing. So the viewpoint orbits a stationary character and shield, and from behind your own back a correct commitment renders as a detached wedge swinging around you. Confirmed by play: "it stayed pinned in the world at the shield hold angle." A camera-yaw clamp was proposed and REJECTED, and the rejection is the useful part: the body cannot turn anyway, so a clamp would not change what the shield DOES — it would only remove the defender's ability to SEE, which is a silent nerf to TRI-SHIELD-DEFAULT (a balance claim confirmed by play 2026-07-19, hours earlier) and would undercut TRI-SHIELD-BAIT (still unverified) by making the bait a blindfold rather than a read. The real cause is believed to be the blockout art: a featureless capsule cannot show a committed body, so the guard reads as hovering near the character rather than held by it. Deferred to M9 (stock hero + animations — the current step), on the reasoning that a permanent input constraint should not be spent solving what the next milestone may dissolve. If it still reads wrong on a rig with arms, revisit then — and prefer a VISUAL tell (guard tint, ground marker along the locked line) over a constraint, since a tell costs no gameplay.
FB-MUZZLE Muzzle sits near the aim origin so the shot follows the crosshair [BUILT]
NEW 2026-07-19 — this was a REAL AIM BUG, reported from play as "he aims at the top of the capsule but …note
NEW 2026-07-19 — this was a REAL AIM BUG, reported from play as "he aims at the top of the capsule but it fires way off", with close range worse than long. FirePoint was at local (0, 0.5, 1.2): 1.2 m in front of the body and 0.825 m below the camera pivot, on a yaw-only root (so it never pitched — aim up and the muzzle stayed put). The aim math was never wrong — AimDir is (crosshairPoint − muzzle).normalized, which correctly converges on the crosshair — but the muzzle and camera were in such different places that the shot LEFT along a line wildly divergent from the one the crosshair draws. With _cameraOffset (0.85, −0.075, −1.36) and pivot _headHeight 1.4, camera−muzzle was (0.85, 0.825, −2.56), giving a launch error of atan(1.184 / (R − 1.2)): 56° at 2 m, 33° at 3 m, 23° at 4 m, 14° at 6 m, 9.9° at 8 m — the 1/(R−1.2) curve the testers described as "a triangle where further shots are more accurate". Inside 1.2 m the target is BEHIND the muzzle and the shot fires backward. Also the sole cause of the "fireball looks like it's curving" report: tracking a close target, the 1.2 m lever arm swings the launch angle far faster than the crosshair moves. FIXED to (0, 1.3, 0.2) — height matched to the camera pivot (1.325), killing the vertical term outright, and the forward offset cut 1.2 → 0.2. Residual error is atan(0.85 / (R − 0.2)) = 25° at 2 m, 13° at 4 m, 6° at 8 m: the leftover is the camera's 0.85 m SHOULDER offset, which is inherent to an over-the-shoulder camera and cannot be removed without moving the muzzle out to the shoulder (x = 0.85) and having shots visibly leave from beside the character. That trade is available and deliberately not taken. ⚠ COST: the 1.2 m almost certainly existed so the fireball appeared to leave from in FRONT of the character rather than out of its chest; M9's muzzle VFX is where that gets re-solved properly. ⚠ CONTAMINATES PRIOR MEASUREMENTS: TRI-FB-COMMIT (4–6 m), TRI-FB-WALKOUT (4 m) and TRI-FB-ANTIAIR were all measured 2026-07-19 UNDER this bug, at exactly the ranges where the error curve is steepest. Re-run all three. UNVERIFIED BY PLAY.
SPAWN-FACING Both combatants open every round facing each other [BUILT]
NEW 2026-07-19. Every runner.Spawn call site passed Quaternion.identity, so both fighters opened facing …note
NEW 2026-07-19. Every runner.Spawn call site passed Quaternion.identity, so both fighters opened facing world-forward (+Z) — the same wall, perpendicular to their opponent — and every round began with a mandatory "where is he" mouse hunt. It also quietly contaminated feel passes: the opening seconds of every measured round went on re-aiming rather than on the spacing game the round is meant to open with. BasicSpawner.SpawnYawFor(index) derives the facing from SpawnPositionFor rather than hardcoding two yaws, so moving a spawn can never leave the facing aimed at where the spawns used to be. The round-reset half is the harder half and is where the real bug was: PlayerHealth.ResetForRound teleported POSITION only, so rounds 2+ opened aimed wherever the player last looked (usually the floor, from wherever they died). Position and rotation are now both restored, via a new [Networked] SpawnYaw. The non-obvious part: the server owns the transform but the LOCAL PlayerCameraRig owns the aim — it seeds Yaw once in OnEnable and integrates mouse deltas forever after — so re-pointing the body alone would have been yanked straight back by the next input tick. A [Networked] FacingEpoch counter is bumped on every reset and the rig re-seats itself when it changes (cached local copy, no ChangeDetector, works on host and client alike; starts at -1 so the first replication of a legitimately-0 epoch is not read as a reset). Pitch resets to level too — dying face-down should not open the next round aimed at the floor. UNVERIFIED BY PLAY.
V1TEST-CADENCE Per-milestone feel passes [BUILT] Running since M5; M7's 0.75 bot judgement is the most recent.

Startup/recovery windows exist (M3 skeleton) and the fireball is live, so the mechanism is there. Never measured against a stationary target to confirm it is genuinely escapable — and M8's own sweep shows 40% hit on a strafing dummy at 0 ms, which is evidence about hit-reg, not about commitment.
TRI-FB-WALKOUT Past ~1.5 m you can walk sideways out of one [DEAD]
MEASURED 2026-07-19, human-vs-human (dev + brother), and the claim is FALSE: the fireball is NOT …note
MEASURED 2026-07-19, human-vs-human (dev + brother), and the claim is FALSE: the fireball is NOT escapable by strafing at 1.5 m. Dev's verdict on the finding: "totally fine with me." So this is a deliberate design decision to drop the requirement, not an unmet gap — recorded rather than edited out, per the authority rule: a requirement that dies should leave a body. STATUS CORRECTED 2026-08-01 falsified — accepted → dead. The old wording was prose the generator's status vocabulary does not speak, so it fell through to the default and this row was reported as an open spec gap owing work for thirteen days — the precise opposite of what it records. dead is the vocabulary's word for superseded, will never be built, kept for history, which is exactly this. A status cell must be a word the ledger's own vocabulary defines; anything else is a comment wearing a status's clothes. ⚠ SPEC §4 ALSO WENT ON ASSERTING THE CLAIM until 2026-08-01 — now struck through there with the falsification recorded, so the two agree. ⚠ THE MEASUREMENT'S OWN LIMIT: taken by eye, no distance markings in the arena — "1.5 m" was estimated, not read. DebugGrid (2026-07-19) makes an exact re-check cheap, and the whole claim is a single number. What does NOT die with it: the spacing re-derivation the claim was a symptom of — the dodge is now ~3.6× walking speed against the ~1.75× every §4 claim was written under. That is M10's, and MOVE-FWD carries the warning.

3D aim and the committed jump arc (JUMP-ARC) both exist, so the interaction is possible. Whether the arc is actually punishable on reaction has never been tested.
TRI-DODGE-WORST Dodging a not-yet-committed fireball is the worst play [UNVERIF]
Follows from DODGE- having no i-frames (INV-2) plus a locked lead. Sound reasoning, zero measurement. …note
Follows from DODGE- having no i-frames (INV-2) plus a locked lead. Sound reasoning, zero measurement. Now LOAD-BEARING for TRI-DODGE-NOPUNISH (2026-07-18): that claim's arithmetic holds only for a dodge reacting to the release*; a pre-empting dodge is actionable 0.05 s before the whiffer, so the recovery race does not punish pre-empting — free aim resolving at launch has to. Measure the two together.

CONFIRMED 2026-07-19, human-vs-human: the shield IS still the default answer. TRI-SHIELD-BAIT Shield loses to baits and out-of-cone shots [UNVERIF]

SHIELD-CONE is built, so out-of-cone shots do get through. The bait half needs a competent opponent closing under a raised guard; the M7 bot has a difficulty dial but was never pointed at this specific test.
TRI-SHIELD-NOFLANK Dodge-flank is an INTENDED counter inside ~6.1 m; the locked guard holds beyond it [UNVERIF]
THE REQUIREMENT ITSELF CHANGED 2026-07-29 (owner call), so the old verified status died with the old …note
THE REQUIREMENT ITSELF CHANGED 2026-07-29 (owner call), so the old verified status died with the old claim: the spec row is now a RANGE BOUND (break-even ~6.1 m at the renarrowed ±30° horizontal), resolving the contradiction that had been flagged since 2026-07-18 — dodge-flanking is the deliberate answer to a raised guard, not a leak. The new bound has never been measured; DebugGrid makes the re-read cheap. Everything below verified the OLD "cannot flank" claim at the old angles and is kept as history. FLANK HALF MEASURED 2026-07-19, human-vs-human, and the (old) claim HELD: flankable only from close range, dev's estimate 2–4 m. That brackets both derived break-evens (~2.0 m pre-retune, ~3.5 m post), so play and arithmetic agree for the first time on this row. ⚠ The estimate is by eye — no arena markings existed; DebugGrid now makes an exact re-read cheap. ⚠ A HOLE IN THE PREMISE WAS FOUND AND CLOSED THE SAME DAY: this claim rests on guard locks at raise, and PlayerCombat.cs:633 was overwriting CommittedAimDir with live look on every cannon press — so the cannon was free aim wearing a commitment's clothes, and the invariant was not actually held. Fixed 2026-07-19 (see SHIELD-CANNON). The measurement above was taken WITH the bug present; it survives it, since the bug affected the cannon rather than the guard's block arc, but a re-check after the fix is cheap. (Prior arithmetic retained below.) Arithmetic DONE 2026-07-18 — claim holds, stated mechanism was WRONG; spec §4 corrected. (1) Guard locks at raise — verified in code, structurally: BeginShield writes CommittedAimDir once in Startup (PlayerCombat.cs:342); the only other writes are the fireball's (:243, :321), and IsBlocking reads it (:366). No measurement needed. (2) "Shield recovers before the dodge does" — FALSE. Re-pointing a guard costs SHIELD-CD (gated from raise) + a fresh SHIELD-RAISE = 1.35 s, vs the dodger actionable at startup+arc+recovery = 0.85 s. The dodge wins by ~0.5 s. (3) The claim survives on geometry the spec never stated: a dodge swings atan(dist/R) off the locked guard, flanking only when R < dist / tan(cone) → ~2.0 m at current tuning (body-contact range, where FB-STARTUP is unusable). The shielder never needs to re-face, so the 0.5 s window is real but unexploitable. Stays unverified, but its BLOCKER is gone: SHIELD-CONE's two-peer re-verification was completed 2026-07-19 and both checks passed, so the guard-locks-at-raise premise this claim rests on is now confirmed live rather than only structurally. What is still owed is the FLANK half — a dodge actually attempting to swing around a raised guard at ~2 m — which nobody has tried against a real opponent. That is a fun-test question and belongs to M10. Tuning hazard recorded: the fragile knobs are DODGE-ARC distance and SHIELD-CONE horizontal — not the recovery timings the old text implicated. Re-check at the M9→M10 rescale beat.
TRI-DODGE-ROLE Dodge answers an unavailable or mis-angled shield; reacts, never pre-empts [UNVERIF]
The cooldown and cone that create this role are built (SHIELD-CD, SHIELD-CONE). "Never pre-empts" is a …note
The cooldown and cone that create this role are built (SHIELD-CD, SHIELD-CONE). "Never pre-empts" is a claim about what is correct play, which needs a real opponent to falsify.
TRI-DODGE-NOPUNISH Dodge punishes nothing; whiffer actionable ~0.36 s before the dodger is actionable [BUILT]
RESTORED to built 2026-07-18 after being demoted the same day. The demotion was caused by the follow-up …note
RESTORED to built 2026-07-18 after being demoted the same day. The demotion was caused by the follow-up window ROOTING the whiffer (decline = 2.9 s); making the window run CONCURRENTLY with recovery removed that cost entirely, so the claim no longer depends on which branch the attacker takes. Arithmetic RE-DERIVED 2026-07-19 against the post-rebuild values (the previous derivation cited FB-STARTUP 0.45 and "the jab now fires on RELEASE" — both dead: the flash package cut startup to 0.35, and the charged shot that moved the jab to release was reverted, so the jab fires on PRESS again): whiffer presses t=0 → FB-STARTUP 0.35 → launch → FB-RECOV 0.45 → actionable 0.80. Dodger reacting to the launch presses at 0.35 → DODGE-STARTUP 0.15 + DODGE-ARC 0.40 + DODGE-RECOV 0.2625 = 0.8125 → actionable 1.1625. Whiffer is up 0.3625 s first (and only 0.10 s before the dodger LANDS at 0.90 — the 'lands vs actionable' distinction still matters). The margin is UNCHANGED by the flash package, and that is structural rather than lucky: a shorter startup moves the whiffer's recovery and the dodger's reaction cue by the same amount, so it cancels. But the RE-THREAT RACE INVERTED, and that is new. FB-CD 1.2 runs from the press, so the whiffer re-threatens at 1.20 while the dodger is now actionable at 1.1625 — the dodger acts 0.0375 s BEFORE the gun comes back up, where under the old 0.45 startup they acted 0.0625 s after it (1.2625 vs 1.20). The dodger got a small free window out of a change made for telegraph readability, which nothing recorded at the time. It is 2¼ ticks at 60 Hz and does not threaten the claim itself (TRI-DODGE-NOPUNISH is about the recovery race, which still favours the whiffer by 0.3625 s), but it is the second knob-interaction the flash package moved silently — and the margin is the tightest it has been, so another dodge-safety buff could flip the real claim too. ⚠ OWED 2026-08-14: DRIFT-LUNGE re-cut the conversion into a ranged fireball behind a 0.35 s recovery (_driftLungeRecovery) the same day this row's glide re-derivation was already owed. A whiffed ranged conversion is a different punish question from a whiffed cone, and it makes the existing debt larger, not smaller — still unmeasured.
TRI-ADVANCE Advancing beats a spent zoner; the jump-in is readable [UNVERIF]
Depends on FB-CD and the readability of JUMP-ARC. ⚠ THE OLD TENSION IS BACK, FROM THE OPPOSITE …note
Depends on FB-CD and the readability of JUMP-ARC. ⚠ THE OLD TENSION IS BACK, FROM THE OPPOSITE DIRECTION (2026-08-14). This row read "in direct tension with the unmet MOVE-BACK" from the years the backpedal penalty was an open gap. That gap closed on 2026-07-19 and this sentence was never updated, so it went on calling a built requirement unmet for almost a month — the plainest kind of ledger drift, one row contradicting another. It is retreat == advance again today, but by owner decision rather than by omission: MOVE-BACK's differential is retired, so a retreating opponent is no longer the slower one. The consequence for this claim is unchanged and now permanent — advancing costs nothing that retreating does not also refund, so "advancing beats a spent zoner" is measured against spacing the spec's §4 arithmetic never assumed. This row and every other TRI-* claim that leaned on a slower retreat owe a re-judgement in play before any of them can move off unverified.
TRI-HEIGHT Height grants angle and sightline; reaching it is contestable [UNVERIF]
M4's arena and cover give real height, and the committed jump makes reaching it contestable in …note
M4's arena and cover give real height, and the committed jump makes reaching it contestable in principle. Never evaluated as an advantage.

The latency-fairness half is the one M8 is actively measuring — Branch C's ~0.55 m intercept, flat across a 12× RTT span, is the first real evidence for it. Not sufficient on its own: the loop as a whole needs a human duel (S3), which is Inc 4.

Roadmap ahead — Alpha & Beta preview, not Prototype scope

Not Prototype scope. Not earned. Downstream of a yes on M10's completion test — a signpost, not a checklist.
ALPHA — is the game fun?
  • Four elements as four unique hand-built stock characters (each with its external physical expression)
  • 1v1 multiplayer + NPC difficulty (fallibility, never stats)
  • Progression — several upgrades per element, on the essence system (core to the world)
  • First art pass — house style and asset types locked
BETA — does it hold up?
  • The character creator (Brink-model: preset parts + colour tints, no morph sliders)
  • Content-complete; external testers; netcode under real load
SOURCE OF TRUTH — ROADMAP.md (order) · PROJECT_STATE.md (status) · Shapers_Combat_Design_Spec.md (requirements). Git is evidence only: it can raise "not started" to "partial", never mark anything done. Regenerate with python tools/roadmap.py.
